- **Failure modes:** no `400`/`422` (nothing is parsed). `404` — invalid bucket name (`6045-6048`), refused path (`6049-6051`), no config / `enabled=false` / `public_read=false` / bucket row missing (`6062-6064`), no candidate and no error document, or the error document itself missing (`6085-6087`); all return the identical `b"Not Found\n"` with `no-store` (`_site_not_found`, `web/app.py:5972-5980`), but after 1–3 DB round trips and up to two head lookups, so 404s are *body*-uniform, not *timing*-uniform. `404` with the error-document body when one is configured and exists (`6079-6083`). `405` — `HEAD` (verified: `Allow: GET`), `POST`/`PUT`/`DELETE`. `200` — object, or index document. `200` with a truncated body — an SSE-C object: the status line and `Content-Length` are emitted, then `iter_bytes` raises `SSECError` on the first iteration (`teledrive/storage/engine.py:632-636`); verified in-process as `200`, `content-length: 16`, `0` body bytes, exception re-raised after the headers. `500` — only on corrupt catalog state (a ready row with `sha256 IS NULL` makes `_site_headers` raise at `web/app.py:6037`); an exception inside the generator is invisible to the access log because the middleware already logged the `200` it captured from `http.response.start`.
- **Findings:**
  - [high] **This route skips the read gate both sibling read paths apply, so an SSE-C object is a 200 with a full `Content-Length` and zero bytes, and an archived object is served without `403 InvalidObjectState`.** `_serve_site` calls `engine.iter_bytes(item.id)` with no `sse_key` (`web/app.py:6105`), while the authenticated REST route calls `_rest_read_gate(manifests, catalog, item)` first (`web/app.py:3759`) and its docstring (`web/app.py:3701-3712`) records this very defect as "probe-confirmed: curl -f treated that as a successful empty download". That fix landed on the REST and admin routes (`web/app.py:3759`, `5730`) but never here. Probe against the real catalog + fake transport: an SSE-C marker produced `200`, `content-length: 16`, `len(body) == 0`, re-raised `SSECError`; `storage_class='ARCHIVE_ACCESS', restore_until_ns=NULL` returned `200 b'cold-bytes'`. Anonymous clients, crawlers and CDNs are told a broken file is fine, cold data is served with no `InvalidObjectState` signal, and the response is indistinguishable from a legitimately empty object. Fix: run `_rest_read_gate` (or a shared helper) before `_site_headers` and collapse both failures into the uniform 404, since this surface has no XML error body.
  - [medium] **The two halves of the gate have opposite defaults, and the S3 contract silently does nothing.** `PutBucketWebsite` returns `200` and `GET ?website` reports the config, but `/site/...` stays `404` until an admin separately sets `public_read` — the repo asserts this as intended (`tests/s3/test_s3_website.py:105-110`), and it is verified. A boto3/aws-cli/IaC user gets a success and a dead site with no warning in the response. In the other direction `web/s3_governance_api.py:857` defaults `public_read = payload.get("public_read", False)` and writes it unconditionally at `:880`, so a governance PUT that omits the field silently **unpublishes** a live site; the SPA's checkbox also defaults to `false` (`web/static/app.js:1120`), so a save issued before `GET .../website` populates the form takes the site offline. The admin endpoint does the opposite and hard-`422`s when the field is absent (`web/app.py:3957`). Code-verified; not probe-verified (the governance router signs and dials the real loopback S3 listener, which is absent under `TestClient` — I got `502 ... ConnectionRefusedError`). Fix: one rule — require `public_read` explicitly on every writer, or pass `public_read=None` when absent in the governance PUT; and make `PutBucketWebsite` report when the gate is closed.
  - [medium] **No rate limit and no range support on a credential-free endpoint where every hit is a fresh full download from Telegram.** `/share` has a 60/min per-IP sliding window (`web/app.py:5758-5783`, used at `5901`, `5923`); `/site` has none (`_share_rate_limited` is never called from `_serve_site`). Every request re-reads every chunk over MTProto: `Cache-Control: no-store` is set twice (`web/app.py:1408`, `6036`), `ETag`/`Last-Modified` are advertised but conditional requests ignored (`6037-6039`), and `Range` is ignored (verified: `Range: bytes=0-3` on a 10-byte object → `200`, 10 bytes, no `Content-Range`), so a dropped transfer restarts at byte 0. One anonymous prober can drive unbounded Telegram traffic and burn the account's flood budget, degrading the whole service — the failure mode the share limiter exists to prevent. Fix: reuse `_share_rate_limited` with its own bucket so the two surfaces cannot starve each other, and implement `Range` with the existing `_byte_range` helper used at `web/app.py:3778`.
  - [medium] **`Content-Type` is uploader-controlled metadata, served inline on the app origin; one CSP header is the only thing preventing stored XSS.** `_site_content_type` (`web/app.py:6016-6027`) trusts `ObjectMetadataStore(...)["content-type"]` (writable by any `s3:PutObject`, `web/s3.py:261`), accepts any syntactically valid `type/subtype` (`_SITE_MIME`, `web/app.py:5969`) and appends `charset=utf-8` to every `text/*`. No `Content-Disposition`, no `X-Robots-Tag`. The mitigation is real and duplicated (route header at `web/app.py:6030-6036`, middleware at `web/app.py:1391-1397`: `sandbox; default-src 'none'; …`) and `nosniff` is set, so script execution is blocked today — but the safety of the surface rests on that one directive surviving a proxy rewrite, and nothing stops a bucket from hosting a convincing fake login page on the operator's domain (`form-action 'none'` blocks submission, not the deception) or getting it indexed. Fix: allowlist renderable types or force `Content-Disposition: attachment` outside `text/html|text/css|image/*|application/json`, add `X-Robots-Tag: noindex, nofollow`, and test `text/html`, `image/svg+xml`, `application/xhtml+xml`.
  - [low] **`HEAD` is 405, and the handler's `HEAD` branch is dead code.** The `if request.method == "HEAD"` short-circuit (`web/app.py:6090-6092`) can never run: FastAPI's `@app.get` registers only `GET`, so Starlette answers `Allow: GET` first (verified). `curl -I`, link checkers, uptime probes and some CDNs treat a working site as broken, and the dead branch makes the code read as if HEAD were supported. Fix: register `methods=["GET", "HEAD"]` (or `api_route`) and keep the branch.
  - [low] **Repeated percent-decoding makes some legal keys unreachable and lets one URL address two keys.** `_site_path` unquotes up to three times (`web/app.py:5982-6012`), so keys `100%25.html` and `100%.html` both resolve from `/site/b/100%2525.html` and `/site/b/100%25.html` (verified: `'100%2525.html' -> '100%.html'`, `'x%2fy.txt' -> 'x/y.txt'`); the literal-percent key is never servable. Not a boundary escape (still the same bucket) and it fails closed on traversal, but it breaks S3's exact-key semantics, so one URL means different objects on `/site` and on the S3 API. Fix: decode exactly once (Starlette already did) and reject a remaining `%xx` only where it would form a traversal segment.
  - [low] **`public_read` is a misnomer that no other surface honours and no public-access-block flag constrains.** Nothing in `web/s3.py` consults `public_read`, so it publishes *only* the `/site` URL space and only while a config exists; and `public_access_block` (`teledrive/storage/catalog.py:427-452`) is enforced for ACLs (`web/s3.py:1353`, `2919`, `2960`) but never consulted here, so a bucket with all four block-public-access flags true can still be published anonymously. Whether AWS's own block-public-access would suppress a website endpoint is a parity question I did **not** verify. Publication scope is also the whole bucket (any key, not just the two document names); the UI notice (`web/s3_governance_api.py:890-893`) is the only place that says so. Fix: refuse to enable the gate while `RestrictPublicBuckets`/`BlockPublicPolicy` is set, or fold those flags into the 404 decision and document the interaction.
  - [nit] **Every `/site` response is written to `LOG_BUFFER` with the full object key** (`GET /site/bucket/path from <ip>`, `web/app.py:1333-1349`), which any `viewer` can read via `/api/logs`; for a public path that is not a credential leak, but object keys can be sensitive and this buffer is treated as untrusted elsewhere. Query strings are not logged and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` is global (`web/app.py:1367`), so referrer leakage is not an issue.
  - [nit] **`ETag`/`Last-Modified` are advertised but conditional requests are ignored** (`web/app.py:6037-6039`), and 404s differ in timing even though the body is uniform (bucket-missing = 1 query, gate-closed = 2, miss with an `error_document` = 3 head lookups), so an anonymous prober can separate "bucket+gate+key present" from a plain 404 by timing alone.
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** path traversal does not reach the catalog. `../`, `.`, empty segments, absolute paths, `~`, backslashes, drive letters, `scheme://`, raw/encoded NULs and 4×-encoded `%25252e%25252e` are all refused before any key lookup (`web/app.py:5982-6012`; `tests/s3/test_s3_website.py:183-205`). There is no filesystem resolution at all, so the only conceivable "escape" is a different key in the same bucket. A leading-`/` key and the empty key are unaddressable (root maps to `index_document`; `ManifestStore.get` requires a 1..1024-byte key, `teledrive/storage/catalog.py:152-155`).
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** delete markers, retired versions and pending/failed uploads are unreachable. The route resolves the *head* (`teledrive/storage/catalog.py:551-562`) and `ManifestStore.get` filters `status == "ready"` (`teledrive/storage/manifest.py:512-517`), so a delete-marker head raises `FileNotFoundError` and falls through to the uniform 404 — never a zero-byte 200 (`tests/s3/test_s3_website.py:164-181`; I re-ran that file: 10 passed). `?versionId` is not routed here at all.
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** the document names cannot leave the bucket key space or inject into the request. `validate_document_name` rejects `% ? # \`, control chars, `.`/`..`/empty segments, leading `/`/`~` and a trailing `/` (`teledrive/s3/website.py:47-78`), and it is applied on all three write paths — S3 XML (`web/s3.py:1466` → `parse_website_xml`), admin (`web/app.py:3959-3962`) and governance (through the S3 XML builder). A missing index falls through the candidate list; a missing `error_document` falls through to the plain 404. There is no loop (2 candidates + 1 error doc) and no 500.
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** redirects are not a surface — `RedirectAllRequestsTo` and `RoutingRules` are rejected with `501` at parse time (`teledrive/s3/website.py:105-110`), and the site route honours no per-object redirect metadata, so there is no open redirect and no `javascript:` target.
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** a config-only bucket is not readable, and `public_read` cannot be flipped by a non-admin: the admin endpoint checks `role == "admin"` itself after `require_auth` (`web/app.py:3950-3951`), API keys carry role `"api-key"` (`web/app.py:625`) and are refused, and `test_operator_cannot_enable_public_read` / `test_permission_separation_for_website_controls` (`tests/s3/test_s3_website.py:294-347`) cover the denial.
  - *(unverified)* The governance-PUT revocation is code-read only (that router signs a request to `127.0.0.1:<s3_port>` and cannot run under `TestClient`), as is the exact on-the-wire shape of a mid-stream `SSECError` against a real ASGI server rather than `TestClient`.
- **Tests that should exist:**
  - `test_public_site_refuses_sse_c_and_archived_objects` — with an SSE-C object and an `ARCHIVE_ACCESS` object (no restore window) in a published bucket, `GET /site/b/enc.bin` and `GET /site/b/cold.bin` must both be `404` with body `b"Not Found\n"`, and explicitly **not** `200` with `Content-Length > 0` and an empty body (today: `200/16/0` and `200 b"cold-bytes"`, verified). **This is the mandatory regression:** it is the one defect that leaves a public client believing a failed read succeeded, and it is the third copy of a bug this codebase has already fixed twice (`web/app.py:3701-3712`).
  - `test_put_bucket_website_never_publishes_and_says_so` — `PUT ?website` on a bucket with `public_read=false` returns 200 *and* a signal (header or body field) that the gate is closed; `/site/...` is 404; after setting the gate the same config serves 200. Today the 200 is silent (verified).
  - `test_governance_website_put_preserves_public_read_when_omitted` — publish, then `PUT .../s3-governance/{b}/website` with `{"index_document": "index.html"}` and no `public_read`; assert the gate and `/site/{b}/` are still live (today it is set to `False`, code-verified).
  - `test_site_is_rate_limited_per_client` — flood one address until `429`, then assert a second address still gets 200 (mirror the `/share` limiter test).
  - `test_site_supports_head_and_range` — `HEAD /site/b/x` is 200 with `Content-Length` and no body (today 405); `Range: bytes=0-3` returns 206 with `Content-Range` and 4 bytes (today 200/10 bytes).
  - `test_site_content_type_never_executes_on_the_app_origin` — upload `a.html` (`text/html`), `b.svg` (`image/svg+xml`) and `c.xhtml` (`application/xhtml+xml`); each response must carry a `Content-Security-Policy` containing `sandbox` and `default-src 'none'`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, and either a restrictive `Content-Disposition` or an allowlisted type.
  - `test_site_percent_keys_do_not_alias` — two distinct keys `100%.html` and `100%25.html`: each has exactly one URL that serves it and the other URL 404s (today both resolve to `100%.html`, verified).
  - `test_site_404_is_uniform_for_bucket_config_and_gate` — extend the existing uniformity test to assert identical body *and* headers for a nonexistent bucket, a bucket with a config and `public_read=false`, and a bucket with `public_read=true` but no config, with no `403`/`500` distinction.
  - `test_site_serves_the_current_head_only` — already covered (`tests/s3/test_s3_website.py:164-181`); keep it as the guard if head resolution changes.
- **Verdict:** the gate is genuinely two-key (config **and** `public_read`), traversal and delete-marker handling are sound, and CSP+nosniff blocks stored XSS today — but the route hand-rolls the read path, so it drops the SSE-C/archive gate its siblings enforce (200 + full `Content-Length` + zero bytes) and is the only credential-free surface with no rate limit or range support; fix the missing read gate first.

## CompleteMultipartUpload — POST /{bucket}/{key}?uploadId=

- **Handler:** `_mpu_complete` web/s3.py:2175-2408, dispatched from `_handle_multipart` web/s3.py:2083-2084. Store logic `MultipartStore.complete` teledrive/s3/multipart.py:521-798, with `_validate_selection` multipart.py:508-519, `_claim_completion` 362-380, `_heartbeat_completion` 393-408, `_reset_owned_completion` 410-436, `recover_stale_completion` 455-506, `snapshot_copy_parts` 882-891, `object_parts_for_version` 800-880.
- **Auth model:** SigV4 `s3:PutObject` on the destination key (verified by `verify_header_signature` before routing, web/s3.py:4791). The upload's owner is verified twice: `mpu.require(upload_id, bucket, key, owner, allow_completed=True)` web/s3.py:2184 and again inside `complete()` multipart.py:528. `require` selects on all four of `(id, bucket, object_key, owner_access_key)` and requires `state IN ('active','completed')` (multipart.py:142-149), so an upload id belonging to another credential, another bucket/key, or an aborted upload answers the same `404 NoSuchUpload` — no existence oracle. Upload ids are `uuid4()` (multipart.py:120), i.e. not enumerable. SSE-C: the marker written at initiate (`encryption = {"mode":"SSE-C","key_md5":…}`, multipart.py:121-132) must be reproduced by the caller via `x-amz-server-side-encryption-customer-key`/`-md5` and compared with `hmac.compare_digest` (web/s3.py:2185-2199); a marker-less upload rejects stray SSE-C headers, and an encrypted upload rejects a missing/wrong key (`SSECError` `InvalidRequest`, 400). Every selected part must additionally carry the *same* `key_md5` (multipart.py:577-584), so a part uploaded under another key cannot be smuggled in.
- **Inputs:** (1) Query `uploadId` — bounded by the route guard; parsed as an opaque string (web/s3.py:2040-2042, 2083). (2) The `CompleteMultipartUpload` XML body — read with a hard 1 MiB cap (web/s3.py:2203-2207), SHA-256 compared to the signed `x-amz-content-sha256` (2208; presigned completes must therefore sign a digest, which `tests/s3/test_s3_presigned_complete.py` enforces), `<!DOCTYPE`/`<!ENTITY` rejected case-insensitively before parsing (2210-2211), parsed with `SafeET` (2213). Root tag must be `CompleteMultipartUpload` (2214-2215); every child must be `Part` (2219-2220); each part needs a digits-only `PartNumber` and a non-empty `ETag` (2221-2224); optional `ChecksumSHA256` per part is collected (2225-2227); ETags are `strip('"').lower()`ed (2228). Any structural problem is `MalformedXML` 400 (2229-2230). (3) Selection is re-validated in the store: non-empty, ≤ 10000, strictly increasing (so duplicates and descending order are `InvalidPartOrder`), number 1..10000, ETag matching `[0-9a-f]{32}|[0-9a-f]{64}` (multipart.py:508-519). (4) `x-amz-checksum-type` ∈ {absent, COMPOSITE, FULL_OBJECT} else `InvalidArgument` (web/s3.py:2234-2237); non-SHA checksums require an explicit type (2244-2249); the whole-object/per-part checksum headers `x-amz-checksum-{sha256,sha1,crc32,crc32c,crc64nvme}` (2238-2243). (5) Precondition headers via `check_write_precondition` (web/s3.py:2200-2202), forwarded as `expected_generation`/`expected_object_id` (2392-2399).
- **Side effects:** No Telegram write, ever — completion re-parents existing immutable part chunk rows. One SQL transaction (multipart.py:643-785): insert `objects` row `pending` with `size=total_size` (650-654); for every selected part, `UPDATE storage_chunks SET object_id=<final>, ordinal=<running>, offset=<running+ref.offset>` guarded by `rowcount == 1` (656-675); mark each consumed part object `status='consumed'` (676-678); flip the final object to `ready` with `sha256=digest` (681-683); insert `object_encryption` (SSE-C only, 690-695), `multipart_etags` (696-698) and one `multipart_object_parts` row per selected logical part (702-708, carrying the per-part plaintext SHA-256); re-attach the Create-time HTTP metadata (711-713); append the idempotency journal `completion_parts` (714-718); CAS the head — insert `generation=1` when absent, otherwise `UPDATE … WHERE object_id=current AND generation=current` (720-752); record the published version (754-756); CAS `uploads` → `completed` + `completed_object_id`/`completed_etag` (757-762); delete the completion lease (763-766); enqueue `s3:ObjectCreated:CompleteMultipartUpload` (767-775) and journal `created` with `detail={"multipart": True}` (776-784); single `commit()` (785). Telegram messages are only ever *read* (verification passes at web/s3.py:2338-2347 and multipart.py:588-618). No `storage_multipart_parts` or `storage_multipart_uploads` row is deleted (the only deletes are the lease, multipart.py:763-766, 428-431, 499-501).
- **Failure modes:**
  - **200** — published, body `CompleteMultipartUploadResult` with `ETag` quoted; `ChecksumSHA256` included only when a composite SHA-256 was declared (web/s3.py:2401-2408). Replay with the *identical* part list returns the committed object id/ETag/size without touching parts (multipart.py:528-539).
  - **400 `MalformedXML`** — body > 1 MiB, wrong root, unexpected child, missing number/ETag, parse error, DOCTYPE/ENTITY (web/s3.py:2207, 2210-2215, 2229-2230).
  - **400 `XAmzContentSHA256Mismatch`** — the XML is not the byte string whose SHA-256 was signed (web/s3.py:2208-2209).
  - **404 `NoSuchUpload`** — unknown/other-owner/other-bucket/other-key/aborted upload (multipart.py:148-149), or a resumable-after-failure upload caught in `completing` before admin recovery.
  - **400 `InvalidArgument`** — unsupported `x-amz-checksum-type`, non-SHA checksum without a type (web/s3.py:2235-2249), or a store-level `ValueError` surfaced generically (web/s3.py:4990).
  - **400 `InvalidPart`** — a selected part is not staged, or its ETag differs from what was uploaded (multipart.py:568-570); a declared per-part checksum names an unstaged part (web/s3.py:2269-2271); encrypted/plaintext mixing or a part whose ciphertext digest changed (multipart.py:577-607); a part manifest is incomplete (multipart.py:673-674, 679-680).
  - **400 `InvalidPartOrder`** — duplicates or descending numbers (multipart.py:513-515).
  - **400 `EntityTooSmall`** — any non-final selected part < 5 MiB (multipart.py:571-572).
  - **400 `BadDigest`** — per-part `ChecksumSHA256` mismatch (web/s3.py:2273-2275), composite SHA-256 mismatch (2291-2293), or a streaming FULL_OBJECT/non-SHA mismatch (2384-2388).
  - **409 `OperationAborted`** — a second concurrent complete while one holds the `completing` lease (multipart.py:371), a lost/expired lease (627, 649), a head CAS loss (752), or a completion CAS loss (762).
  - **412 `PreconditionFailed`** — `If-Match`/`If-None-Match` no longer matches the head at publish time (multipart.py:725-728, 734-740).
  - **413 `EntityTooLarge`** — bucket quota exceeded by the selected part bytes (web/s3.py:2312-2314, `_quota_guard` 180-192).
  - **404 `NoSuchKey`** — replay of a completion whose object manifest has since been physically removed (`manifests.get` in the replay path, multipart.py:538 → web/s3.py:4989).
  - **503 `ServiceUnavailable`** — `RuntimeError` from transport/catalog escaping the handler (web/s3.py:4992-4993).
  - Every pre-commit failure leaves the upload resumable: `_reset_owned_completion` returns `active` and the lease row is dropped (multipart.py:410-436, 790-795). Verified: a bad FULL_OBJECT checksum → 400 `BadDigest`, `GET ?uploadId` still 200 listing the part, and a correct retry → 200 with the exact bytes; missing-part and out-of-order rejections also leave the upload usable.
- **Findings:**
  - [high] **A multipart completion bypasses the bucket's object-lock default retention, so a WORM bucket's objects become deletable.** `BucketCatalog.publish` applies `apply_default_lock(item.id)` after both the create and replace paths (teledrive/storage/catalog.py:1074, 1120; helper 342-353), but `MultipartStore.complete` builds its own head CAS and never calls it (teledrive/s3/multipart.py:720-785). Nothing else compensates: `_record_published_uncommitted` only inserts a `published_versions` row (catalog.py:822-833) and the completion path itself never writes `retention_mode`/`retention_until_ns`. Verified end-to-end with a bucket configured `DefaultRetention GOVERNANCE/1 day` (web/s3.py:2977-3048): a single `PutObject` object answers 200 on `GET ?retention`, while an object published via `CompleteMultipartUpload` in the same bucket answers **404 `NoSuchObjectLockConfiguration`** and an explicit `DELETE …?versionId=` returns **204** — i.e. an upload client silently opts out of the bucket's immutability guarantee. The existing test only covers the PUT path (`tests/s3/test_s3_object_lock.py:138-153`). Fix: call `apply_default_lock` inside the same completion transaction (or in a post-commit step before the response) for the new `object_id`, and mirror the copy path, which has the same gap (`snapshot_copy_parts`/`copy.py` never applies it either — grep-verified, behaviour unverified).
  - [medium] **An idempotent completion replay ignores write preconditions and returns a stale ETag for bytes that are no longer at the key.** The replay branch returns before consulting `expected_generation`/`expected_object_id` (multipart.py:528-539), even though the handler computed and passed them (web/s3.py:2200-2202, 2392-2399). Verified: complete part 1 → `200 ETag "afacf6…-1"`; `PUT` new bytes to the same key → HEAD `"2a1154…"`; replay the same completion XML → **200 with the old `"afacf6…-1"`** while `GET` serves `second-generation`. A client that treats `200 + ETag` as proof that *its* multipart bytes are the current object is wrong, and a conditional replay (`If-None-Match: *`) can report success where the precondition should fail. Fix: in the replay branch, re-run the head/ETag check against `expected_*` and either answer `412` or (preferably) `200` only when `uploads.completed_object_id` is still the current head, otherwise `409`/`412`.
  - [medium] **The completion XML's declared composite SHA-256 is computed over a *filtered* selection, silently skipping parts that were never staged.** The generator is `bytes.fromhex(part_digests[number]) for number, _ in sorted(selected) if part_digests.get(number) is not None` (web/s3.py:2281-2285), so an unstaged number is dropped from the hash rather than making the comparison fail; the surrounding `except KeyError` (2286-2289) is dead code. Verified: select staged parts 1+2 plus a never-uploaded part 3, declare the correctly computed SHA-256 of `sha256(p1)||sha256(p2)` → the checksum check passes and the request fails later in the store with `InvalidPart` (verified 400 `InvalidPart`) instead of `BadDigest`. Today it cannot publish wrong bytes (the store re-binds every etag), but it is a fail-closed violation in the integrity layer: the checksum comparison no longer describes the request. Fix: compute over exactly `selected` and let the `KeyError` → `InvalidPart` branch fire.
  - [medium] **Concurrent completions are fenced, but a completion that crashes mid-flight permanently wedges the upload until an operator intervenes.** `_claim_completion` CAS-es `active → completing` and inserts a single lease row (multipart.py:362-380), so exactly one writer can publish (the loser gets `409 OperationAborted`; the per-upload lease PK and the head/`uploads` CAS at 744-752 and 757-762 mean the race itself is code-verified but **unverified** under a real concurrent stress test). However the `completing` state is only unwound by `_reset_owned_completion` in the same process (790-795) or by an *admin* POST `/api/v1/admin/s3-multipart/{upload_id}/recover` with `execute=true` and `min_age_seconds ≥ 60` (web/app.py:5293-5311, multipart.py:455-506) — there is no worker that sweeps `stale_completions` (grep: the only caller is the admin route). After a hard kill the upload answers `404 NoSuchUpload` to both Complete *and* Abort (`abort` requires `state='active'`, multipart.py:333-337) and the staged parts are unreachable until someone reads `GET /api/v1/admin/s3-multipart/stale` and executes recovery. Fix: run `recover_stale_completion(min_age_seconds=…, dry_run=False)` from the existing maintenance loop, and let Abort fence a stale lease instead of answering 404.
  - [medium] **Completion never deletes its multipart bookkeeping, and those rows are treated as live media references by the GC safety layer.** `complete` keeps every `storage_multipart_parts` row, the `uploads` row (state `completed`) and every `completion_parts` row forever (multipart.py:757-766; the only `delete()` calls in the module target `completion_leases`), and `abort` likewise only flips `state='aborted'` (333-358) — there is no retention/cleanup job anywhere (`delete(parts)`/`delete(uploads)` do not exist in the tree). Meanwhile `teledrive/storage/gc.py:189-192` reads all `parts.object_id` into `multipart_objects` and `teledrive/storage/recovery.py:125` refuses physical cleanup for any object still referenced by `storage_multipart_parts`, so the rows are not inert. The completion path itself is safe (chunks are moved, so no *data* is pinned), but the aborted-upload table grows without bound and the GC "queued" candidate query additionally requires `published_versions` + `status='ready'` (gc_collect.py:245-255), which a staged part object does not satisfy, so the media that `_handle_multipart` enqueues at abort time (web/s3.py:2065-2079) appears unreclaimable — the GC consequence is **unverified** (code-read only), the unbounded rows are grep-verified. Fix: prune `storage_multipart_parts`/`completion_parts`/`uploads` for completed and aborted uploads older than the idempotency window, and exclude aborted uploads from the GC reference set.
  - [low] **Quota is checked once, before the lease, against a snapshot of part sizes.** `selected_size` is summed from `storage_multipart_parts` rows read outside any fence (web/s3.py:2302-2312), then `mpu.complete` claims the lease and re-reads the parts (multipart.py:542, 549-551). Two completions that both pass the check can both publish, and an upload that replaced parts after the check is measured with the old sizes. The published size is still whatever the verified parts contain (`total_size`, multipart.py:620), so this is an accounting TOCTOU only. Fix: re-check the quota inside the publication transaction using `total_size`.
  - [low] **The SSE-C multipart ETag is not `<md5-of-md5s>-N`, and the `-N` layout tag that would reconcile it is computed and discarded.** Encrypted completions store `final_etag = full_cipher.hexdigest()` (64 hex, no suffix; multipart.py:628-633) in both `objects.sha256` and `multipart_etags`. `layout_etag` is assigned at 633 and never read (dead variable). `object_parts_for_version` parses `multipart_etags.etag` with `re.fullmatch(r"[0-9a-f]{32}-([0-9]+)")` (816-818), which cannot match a 64-hex SSE-C tag, so it would raise `409 InvalidObjectState` — currently unreachable because `GetObjectAttributes` refuses SSE-C outright with `501 NotImplemented` (web/s3.py:4800-4806; verified 501 on a plain SSE-C PUT as well as on an SSE-C multipart object), and `copy.py:76,107` skips the snapshot because the tag equals `sha256`. Keep the opaque ETag if intentional, but delete `layout_etag` or use it, and make `object_parts_for_version` tolerant of opaque tags so a future attributes route does not 409.
  - [low] **A zero-length trailing part can publish an empty object.** `UploadPart` accepts a 0-byte body (web/s3.py:2454-2473, no lower bound) and the minimum-size rule exempts the last part (multipart.py:571-572), so `CompleteMultipartUpload` with one 0-byte part produces a `ready` 0-byte object. Harmless for integrity, but it is an object shape AWS does not create, and it makes "the upload succeeded" indistinguishable from "nothing was uploaded".
  - [low] **`CompleteMultipartUploadResult` reports only `ChecksumSHA256`, and only for a composite.** Other requested algorithms and the `ChecksumType` are dropped from the body and no `x-amz-checksum-*` response headers are emitted (web/s3.py:2401-2408), so an SDK that requested CRC32 or FULL_OBJECT cannot confirm from the response what was verified even though the server did verify it (2319-2389).
  - [nit] **Part-number bounds are enforced late and with the wrong code.** A `PartNumber` of `999999999` parses fine in the XML layer (web/s3.py:2221-2228) and is rejected in `_validate_selection` as `400 InvalidPart` (multipart.py:516-518) rather than `InvalidArgument`, unlike `UploadPart` (web/s3.py:2440-2441).
  - [nit] **`ChecksumSHA256` is accepted for parts that are not selected.** `part_checksums` is keyed over *all* staged parts (web/s3.py:2259-2275), so a caller can add a checksum for an unselected, not-yet-published part and get it verified; harmless, but it verifies something the request is not about.
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** the part list is strictly ascending and duplicate-free (multipart.py:512-519; verified `400 InvalidPartOrder` for both `[1,1]` and `[2,1]`), every listed part must exist with the exact uploaded ETag (568-570), and non-final parts must be ≥ 5 MiB (571-572; verified `400 EntityTooSmall`). Gaps in part *numbers* are allowed, as AWS allows, and cannot leave a hole because the published layout is built from the selected parts' byte ranges in order (`offset` accumulator, multipart.py:655-680) and re-checked against `total_size` (679-680).
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** parts are re-read and re-hashed before publication — plaintext parts must match both the recorded size and the manifest SHA-256 (multipart.py:608-618), SSE-C parts must match the ciphertext size and digest and the upload's `key_md5` (577-602) — so a caller cannot publish bytes that differ from what `UploadPart` staged, even by lying about size or ETag.
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** the advertised multipart ETag is `md5(concat(part md5s))-N` and matches what is published (multipart.py:619, 635-636; `tests/s3/test_s3_multipart.py:71-81` asserts Complete, HEAD and GetObjectAttributes agree), and `object_parts_for_version` re-derives the part count from that tag and rejects any layout whose count/size/order disagrees (820-880).
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** exactly one writer publishes — the `active → completing` CAS plus the per-upload lease PK (multipart.py:362-380) and the head CAS on `(object_id, generation)` (744-752) — and a fenced/expired writer cannot commit because the final publication re-asserts the token (646-649) and the `uploads` CAS (757-762); `recover_stale_completion` refuses to reopen a `completed` upload and refuses leases with no recorded row (475-478, 484-492).
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** a failed completion is non-destructive. Checksum, part-list and size failures happen before the publication transaction; anything inside it is rolled back (786-788) and the lease is released so the upload returns to `active` (790-795) with its parts intact. Verified by probe: bad checksum → 400 `BadDigest`, ListParts 200, subsequent correct complete 200 with byte-identical content.
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** expiry cannot be used as an oracle. There is no per-upload expiry check at completion (`created_at` is only used by the lifecycle sweeper, teledrive/s3/lifecycle.py:235-249, which flips `active → aborted`), so an aged upload either completes normally or, once swept, answers the same `404 NoSuchUpload` as an unknown id — status and body are identical for "expired/aborted", "wrong owner" and "no such upload". The corollary (unverified, by design) is that an upload is completable until the sweeper happens to run.
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** key validation is repeated at publish time (`catalog._keyhash(key)`, multipart.py:720; 1..1024 UTF-8 bytes, catalog.py:152-155) inside the transaction, so a key that somehow became invalid cannot half-publish.
- **Tests that should exist:**
  - `test_bucket_default_lock_applies_to_multipart_complete` — configure `DefaultRetention` (GOVERNANCE, 1 day) on a bucket, complete a multipart upload into it, then assert `GET ?retention` returns 200 with `<Mode>GOVERNANCE</Mode>` **and** `DELETE ?versionId=<id>` returns 403. Today the retention GET is 404 and the delete is 204 (verified). **This is the mandatory regression**: a bucket-level WORM guarantee must not depend on which write API the client used.
  - `test_multipart_complete_replay_does_not_vouch_for_replaced_head` — complete, overwrite the key with a plain PUT, replay the identical completion XML; assert either 412/409 or, if 200 is kept for idempotency, that the returned `ETag` equals the *current* HEAD ETag. Today it returns the superseded `-N` ETag with 200 (verified).
  - `test_complete_composite_checksum_covers_exactly_the_selected_parts` — select staged 1+2 and unstaged 3, declare `sha256(digest1||digest2)`; assert `400 BadDigest` (not `InvalidPart`), proving the checksum did not silently skip part 3. Today it passes the checksum step and fails later as `InvalidPart` (verified).
  - `test_two_concurrent_completes_publish_exactly_one_object` — drive two `complete()` calls from two connections/tasks for the same upload; assert exactly one 200, one `409 OperationAborted`, exactly one `published_versions`/`multipart_etags` row for the key, and that the loser's staged parts are still present. Code-verified; not covered by a concurrency test today.
  - `test_stale_completion_is_recovered_by_maintenance_not_only_by_admin` — kill a writer between `_claim_completion` and commit, run the maintenance loop, then assert `require(...,state='active')` and a successful re-complete; and assert `Abort` on a stale lease does not answer 404.
  - `test_multipart_rows_are_pruned_after_the_idempotency_window` — complete (and separately abort) an upload, advance past the window, run the pruner, and assert `storage_multipart_parts`/`completion_parts` rows for that upload are gone while the published object still resolves; today the rows live forever (`grep` shows no `delete(parts)`/`delete(uploads)`).
  - `test_complete_quota_is_rechecked_under_the_completion_lease` — stage parts near the quota, pass the pre-check, replace a part with a larger body, then assert the publish is refused with 413 rather than exceeding the bucket quota.
  - `test_complete_rejects_zero_byte_trailing_part` — one 0-byte `UploadPart` selected alone; assert 400 `EntityTooSmall`/`InvalidPart` rather than a published 0-byte object.
  - `test_complete_returns_every_verified_checksum` — request FULL_OBJECT CRC32C and assert the Complete response (body and/or `x-amz-checksum-*` headers) echoes the value it verified, matching GetObjectAttributes.
- **Verdict:** ownership, part binding, byte re-verification and the single-writer fence are genuinely strong — nothing here can publish unverified or foreign bytes — but CompleteMultipartUpload quietly skips the bucket's default object-lock retention (verified: retained bucket, deletable multipart object), and its idempotent replay and composite checksum both assert less than the caller is told, so fix the lock default first.

## admin_download_object — GET /api/v1/admin/storage/buckets/{bucket}/objects/download

_No route has the path in this heading: `grep -r "objects/download"` matches nothing in the repo. The handler `admin_download_object` is actually mounted at `GET /api/v1/admin/storage/buckets/{bucket}/content` (`web/app.py:3770-3775`), which is what `web/static/app.js:808-811` opens. Line numbers are from commit `fe5c41e` (`git show fe5c41e:web/app.py`); the working tree has since gained an uncommitted `_log_safe` patch that shifts them, so treat function names as the authoritative anchors. The duplication question has a clean answer: it **was** a real bug at `d7881dd`, and commit `48e8d95` closed it by making this route and `get_object_content` call one `_stream_object_response` helper._

- **Handler:** `admin_download_object` `web/app.py:3773` (route `3770-3775`), delegating to `_stream_object_response` `web/app.py:3707-3767`; gate `_rest_read_gate` `3672-3698`; key contract `_valid_rest_key` `3701-3704`; range parser `_byte_range` `1569-1590`; engine `TelegramObjectStorage.iter_bytes` `teledrive/storage/engine.py:612-704`.
  - **The duplication regression, verified:** at `d7881dd`, `admin_download_object` (`git show d7881dd:web/app.py:3675-3724`) was a standalone copy with **zero** calls to `_rest_read_gate`, `catalog.object_read_state`, `manifests.encryption_info`, `record_object_access` or `_valid_rest_key` (grep over that blob: no matches). `48e8d95` deleted the copy and both GET handlers now call the helper. So (a) `403 InvalidObjectState` for archived objects, (b) `400` for SSE-C instead of a 200 with zero bytes, (c) `record_object_access`, (d) the 422 key-byte check and (e) the shared streaming code are all present here now. The one remaining copy is the REST **HEAD** handler, which still inlines the gate and skips access counting (`web/app.py:5666-5687`) — out of scope, but it is the next drift candidate.
- **Auth model:** `Depends(require_auth)` only (`web/app.py:3775`, implementation `582-636`); no `_same_origin` (correct for a GET, and the only write is the counter). Two principals:
  - **Session cookie / `Bearer td_sess_`:** `_get_current_user` then `_enforce_role_policy` (`531-536`) and `_enforce_user_permission` (`539-554`). The path is a GET and is not in `_ADMIN_ONLY_PREFIXES` nor the `/quota`,`/website` suffixes (`web/permissions.py:76-105`), so `_required_roles` returns `_ROLE_READ` (`web/app.py:507,526-527`) and the auto-generated permission is `get:/api/v1/admin/storage/buckets/{bucket}/content`; `viewer`, `operator` and `admin` all hold every `get:`/`head:` permission (`web/permissions.py:111-117,335-346`). **A viewer can download raw bytes of any bucket** — pinned as intended by `tests/web/test_auth_rbac.py:670-672`. No per-bucket ACL or policy is consulted for any session route, so this is consistent with the rest of the WebUI read surface (a viewer can already list every object and full-text search its bytes), but it means "read-only" includes "exfiltrate every payload"; if buckets are a tenant boundary this needs an operator-only gate.
  - **Bearer API key:** `require_auth` resolves `permission_for(method, concrete_path)` and requires that exact scope (`web/app.py:587-622`, `web/permissions.py:316-321`); it skips the role gate by design. A key minted with only `get:/api/v1/admin/storage/buckets/{bucket}/content` (or `admin:*`, which expands over the live route table, `web/app.py:561-579`) can read every bucket; `rest:objects:get` does **not** grant it, and this scope does not grant the REST route. No key-level or bucket-level scoping exists.
  - Unauthenticated → `401` + `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer` (`632-636`); a bad key → one generic `403` (`619-622`). No rate limit or concurrency cap (the `/share` limiter is the only one in the file).
- **Inputs:**
  - `bucket` — path segment, plain `str`, no `pattern`/`max_length` and no `BucketCatalog.validate_bucket`; it is only compared against `catalog.list_buckets()` (`3724`) and used in SQL equality, so an invalid/oversized name is a 404, not an injection. Cost: `list_buckets()` materializes every bucket name per request (`teledrive/storage/catalog.py:525-526`).
  - `key` — required query, FastAPI `min_length=1, max_length=1024` **characters** (`3774`); `_valid_rest_key` re-checks 1..1024 **UTF-8 bytes** before `db_connect` (`3701-3704,3719`), so a 600×`é` key is `422`, not the 500 the sibling used to return. No control-character/NUL rejection, but the key never becomes a filesystem path (scratch names come from `part.ordinal`, `engine.py:656`).
  - `Range` — single header, `re.fullmatch(r"bytes=(\d*)-(\d*)")` after `.strip()` (`1573`): `a-b`, `a-`, `-n`; malformed/multi-range/`-0`/empty-object/`start >= size` → `416` with `Content-Range: bytes */size` (`1574-1589`). Validated against `item.size`; arithmetic is otherwise bounded.
  - **Ignored:** `If-Match`, `If-None-Match`, `If-Modified-Since`, `If-Range`, `x-amz-server-side-encryption-customer-*`, `versionId`, `partNumber`, `response-content-*`, `Origin`, `x-teledrive-csrf` (no state-changing method). The body is never read (the 32 MiB buffer applies only to POST/PUT/PATCH, `web/app.py:1344`). `Host` is allow-listed (`1290-1296`).
- **Side effects:**
  - SQL reads: `list_buckets`, head lookup, manifest, `object_read_state` and `encryption_info`; then `UPDATE storage_objects SET access_count = access_count + 1, last_accessed_ns = :now` via `record_object_access` (`teledrive/storage/catalog.py:271-283`), committed inside the method **and** again at `web/app.py:3735` (redundant).
  - `_log("download: bucket/key", "storage", user=<actor>)` **before** the object is resolved or the gate runs (`web/app.py:3786-3788`); appended to the 1 500-entry `LOG_BUFFER` (`web/app.py:172`), pushed to WebSocket subscribers and written through `_FILE_LOG_HANDLER` (`web/app.py:248-256`, formatter `1188-1191`).
  - One `db_connect()` per request held from `3720` until the generator's `finally` (`3751-3756`) — i.e. for the whole Telegram transfer — against the default 15-connection pool (`teledrive/db.py:557`).
  - `_secure_mkdir(STORAGE_SCRATCH_DIR)` (`3742-3743`), one temporary directory per read, one ≤16 MiB chunk on disk at a time, each digest-verified up to 3 attempts (`engine.py:644-704`).
  - One `transport.download` per overlapping chunk on the USER-MTProto session (`engine.py:601`), with replica fallback; no send, no journal row. Response is a `StreamingResponse`, never buffered (`3765-3767`).
- **Failure modes:**
  - `400` — Host not allowed (`1294-1295`); SSE-C object (`3692-3697`).
  - `401` — no session/key (`632-636`). `403` — role denied (`533-536`); key invalid/out of scope (`619-622`); archived object with no open restore window (`3686-3691`).
  - `404` — unknown bucket (`3724-3725`, body "Bucket not found", unlike the REST route's uniform "Object not found") or unknown/deleted/delete-marked key (`3726-3729`).
  - `416` — malformed or unsatisfiable Range, with `Content-Range: bytes */size` (`1574-1589`); note the access counter has already been incremented by then (finding below).
  - `422` — missing/empty/oversized `key`, including 1025+ UTF-8 bytes (`3701-3704`).
  - **`500`** — a `Range` whose first or last group exceeds Python's 4 300-digit `int()` limit: `int(first)`/`int(last)` raises an uncaught `ValueError` (`1581`, `1586-1587`) because `except BaseException` at `3747-3749` only closes the connection and re-raises. Probed the parser in isolation: `bytes=`+`9`*5000+`-` and `bytes=-`+`9`*5000 both raise `ValueError: Exceeds the limit (4300 digits)`; the 5 007-byte header is inside h11's 16 KiB default, so it should reach the app (**socket reachability unverified**; the parser behaviour is verified). Also `503` when no Telethon USER session is authorized (`storage_transport` raises at `web/app.py:1566`, called at `3741`).
  - `200`/`206` — `Content-Length: end-start`, `ETag: "sha256"`, `Accept-Ranges`, `X-TeleDrive-Object-ID`, `Content-Range` on 206 (`3758-3767`).
  - **Mid-stream failures after headers are sent:** corrupt Telegram chunk after retries, `IntegrityError` from an inconsistent manifest, transport/FloodWait, lazy DB errors → truncated `200`/`206` with a full `Content-Length`; `access_count` is already incremented.
  - **Cancellation before the first `__anext__`:** `stream()`'s `finally` never runs, so the eagerly checked-out connection is released only by GC/SQLAlchemy finalization (`3720` vs `3751-3756`). The sibling review probed this shape on Starlette 0.46.2.
- **Findings:**
  - [high] **(closed in `48e8d95`) The handler really was the duplicate that missed all three sibling fixes.** At `d7881dd:web/app.py:3675-3724` it streamed archived bytes with `200/206` regardless of `storage_class`/`restore_until_ns`, sent SSE-C objects as `200` + `Content-Length` + zero bytes (and advertised their plaintext `sha256` in `ETag`), never called `record_object_access` (so tiering's idle clock demoted objects read through the WebUI download button), and returned `500` for a >1024-byte multibyte key. All are gone in the current tree (`3707-3767`); the regression test covers the first two (`tests/web/test_auth_rbac.py:1475-1523`). Do not re-open; do keep the test.
  - [medium] **An oversized `Range` is an unhandled `ValueError` → 500, not 416.** `int(first)`/`int(last)` (`web/app.py:1581,1586-1587`) hit CPython 3.11+'s 4 300-digit conversion cap (`requires-python = ">=3.11"`, `pyproject.toml:9`). Any viewer can drive 500s and exception logs with `Range: bytes=<5000 digits>-`; the S3 parser has the identical flaw (`web/s3.py:508-512`). Fix: bound each group (`if len(group) > 20: raise HTTPException(416, ...)`) or catch `ValueError` in `_byte_range` and map it to 416.
  - [medium] **A read that never delivers bytes still counts as an access.** The counter is committed at `3731-3735`, *before* `_byte_range` (`3736-3738`) and before `storage_transport` (`3741`), so a `416` (bad range) or `503` (no session) increments `access_count` and refreshes `last_accessed_ns`. That makes tiering's idle clock forgeable with failing requests and contradicts `record_object_access`'s own "successful GetObject" docstring (`teledrive/storage/catalog.py:271-283`); the S3 path deliberately counts only after preconditions and range parsing (`web/s3.py:3941-3951`). Fix: parse the range and build the transport first, then count, or count only after the first chunk.
  - [medium] **The DB connection is held for the whole multi-minute stream, and the early-disconnect path leaks it.** `db_connect()` at `3720` is closed only in the generator's `finally` (`3751-3756`); if the client aborts before the first `__anext__`, Starlette never advances the generator and the checkout is released only by GC. Default pool is 5+10 (`teledrive/db.py:557`), and every auth request needs a connection too, so a handful of slow/failed downloads can starve the process. Same shape as the REST GET (already reported there), but this route is reachable by `viewer` and has no rate/concurrency cap.
  - [medium] **The per-download audit line is forgeable through to the durable file log, and is written before the outcome is known.** `_log` is called at `3786-3788` with the raw query `key`, *before* `_stream_object_response` resolves anything, so a `404` with `key=x%0A2026-01-01 00:00:00 [admin] [storage] ...` is enough — no upload or write privilege, only `viewer`. The uncommitted `_log_safe` patch sanitizes the `LOG_BUFFER`/WebSocket string, but the `LogRecord` at `web/app.py:248-256` is still built from the raw `msg`/`user`/`source` and the file formatter writes `%(message)s` verbatim (`1188-1191`), so the on-disk audit trail still gets the injected line. It also means every 403/404/416/503 attempt reads as `download: bucket/key`, with no object id, byte count or status. Fix: sanitize the values passed to `LogRecord` too, and emit the audit after the gate with `object_id` and the outcome.
  - [low] **`viewer` is a raw-byte principal for every bucket.** Verified intentional by `tests/web/test_auth_rbac.py:670-672` and the role model (`web/permissions.py:64-70,335-346`); no bucket/prefix scoping exists for sessions, and an API key minted with just this route's generated permission is equivalent. If bucket isolation is meant to survive a compromised read-only account, this must become operator-only (or per-bucket ACL); otherwise document it as "viewer can read all content", not merely "read-only".
  - [low] **No conditional requests; a resumed range can splice two versions.** `If-Range`/`If-Match` are ignored (`3758-3767`), and the advertised `ETag` is the plaintext `sha256` while the S3 surface advertises the multipart/ciphertext ETag (`web/s3.py:233-241`). A client that saves a partial file, lets the key be overwritten and resumes gets a `206` for the new head; the only defence is re-hashing. Inherited from the shared helper, but this endpoint is the one the browser download button uses.
  - [low] **`Content-Disposition` is injection-safe but mangles non-ASCII names.** The basename is reduced to `[A-Za-z0-9._ -]` (`3785`), so `réçu.bin` becomes `r__u.bin` and `dir/` becomes `download` (verified with the exact regex). No raw CR/LF/quote/`;` can reach the header, and the bucket is never echoed into a header — so header injection is genuinely closed. Fix (optional): use the RFC 5987 `filename*=UTF-8''…` form with `urllib.parse.quote`, as `/share` already does (`web/app.py:5838`).
  - [nit] `bucket not in catalog.list_buckets()` loads every bucket name per download and returns a distinct `404 Bucket not found`, while the REST twin returns the uniform `404 Object not found`; both are enums for a principal that can already list buckets.
  - [nit] `_rest_read_gate` returns the encryption marker but the helper discards it (`3730`), and `allow_range=False`/`count_access=False` have no callers (`3708-3710`); `conn.commit()` after `record_object_access` is a second commit (`3735`).
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** range arithmetic against the object size, `-0`, zero-length objects, `start >= size` and suffix overflow all produce a correct `416` or a clamped whole-object range, and the engine re-validates `[start,end)` against the manifest (`engine.py:626-629`).
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** streaming is chunked and never buffered; each chunk is digest-verified before its bytes are yielded (`engine.py:599-609`), and the object id is pinned before streaming, so an overwrite mid-transfer cannot mix versions.
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** object lock/legal hold is **not** a read gate in the S3 data plane either (it fences overwrite/delete), so this route bypasses no hold the sibling honours; archive/tiering is the only read-time storage-class gate, and it is now applied (`3686-3691`).
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** header injection is closed (probe: CRLF, `"`, `;`, NUL and Unicode all become `_`/are dropped); `Content-Type` is hard-coded `application/octet-stream` and `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` is global (`1363`), plus `Cache-Control: no-store` for every `/api/*` path (`1389-1392`).
  - *(unverified)* Whether the 5 007-byte `Range` header survives the deployed server/proxy to reach `_byte_range` (the parser's `ValueError` is verified; the socket path was not run); and the early-disconnect connection leak on the current Starlette version, which is inherited from the sibling probe rather than re-measured here.
- **Tests that should exist:**
  - `test_admin_download_records_the_access_counter` — publish an object, read `access_count`/`last_accessed_ns`, GET 200, assert both advanced; then GET `Range: bytes=999999-` (416), a missing key (404), an archived object (403) and, with the transport removed, a 503, and assert the counter did **not** move. **This is the mandatory regression:** of the three fixes that motivated `48e8d95`, the archive and SSE-C gates are covered (`tests/web/test_auth_rbac.py:1475-1523`) but access accounting — the one whose absence caused silent tiering demotion — is not asserted anywhere for either REST or admin GET; the new test's docstring claims it, its body does not.
  - `test_admin_download_rejects_an_oversized_range_with_416` — `Range: bytes=` + `"9" * 5000` + `"-"` and `bytes=-` + `"9" * 5000`; assert `416` with `Content-Range: bytes */<size>`, never `500`, and the object is untouched.
  - `test_admin_download_does_not_log_a_forged_record_through_the_file_handler` — request `key="x\n[2026-01-01 00:00:00] [admin] [storage] delete object: ..."`, read both `LOG_BUFFER` and the rotating file handler's output, assert exactly one line was added and neither contains the forged prefix; repeat for a 404 (the log is written before resolution).
  - `test_admin_download_viewer_can_read_any_bucket` — pin today's `viewer` 200 and add the explicit statement that all buckets are readable, so a future per-bucket ACL decision is deliberate rather than a silent drift.
  - `test_admin_download_matches_rest_on_unknown_bucket_and_range` — same bucket/key/range against `/api/v1/buckets/{b}/content` and the admin route; assert identical status, `Content-Range`, and no extra plaintext `ETag` for SSE-C.
  - `test_admin_download_releases_the_db_connection_on_early_disconnect` — pool of 1, abort before the first byte, assert the next request is served immediately and the checkout returns to 0 without GC.
- **Verdict:** The duplication bug was real and is now correctly closed — one helper carries the archive gate, SSE-C refusal, 422 key contract and access counter for both GET routes — but the shared path still counts failed requests as reads, turns an oversized `Range` into a 500, and logs attacker-controlled keys to a forgeable file audit, so the remaining work is accounting/robustness, not S3 parity.

## SigV4 verification — teledrive/s3/sigv4.py

- **Handler:** `verify_header_signature` at teledrive/s3/sigv4.py:322 (header mode) and `_presigned_signature` at teledrive/s3/sigv4.py:126 (selected at sigv4.py:329-330); single call site `web/s3.py:4793` (`owner = verify_header_signature(request, store.resolve, action)`), after routing and before every handler except the POST-policy form route (`web/s3.py:4791-4792`).
- **Auth model:** A valid signature proves possession of the stored secret for one access key, over exactly `METHOD\nraw-path\ncanonical-query\nsigned-headers\nsigned-header-names\npayload-hash` plus the `x-amz-date` timestamp and the date/region/service scope. It authenticates *the request line and signed headers*, not the credential's rights: the coarse permission check is fused into `lookup_secret(access_key, permission)` (`sigv4.py:392`, `teledrive/s3/credentials.py:244-260`), and the resource-level decision happens later in `_authorize_resource` (`web/s3.py:4837`). **Not covered:** the request body whenever the payload hash is `UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD` or a `STREAMING-*` mode (delegated to `_verify_upload_sha256`/`AwsChunkedDecoder`), any header absent from `SignedHeaders` (each handler must demand its own), the region (see findings), and any notion of one-time use.
- **Inputs:** `Authorization` is one `fullmatch` (`sigv4.py:332-337`): access key `[^\s,/]{1,64}`, 8-digit date, region `[a-z0-9-]{1,32}`, service `[a-z0-9]{1,16}`, `SignedHeaders=[a-z0-9;\-]+`, signature exactly 64 lowercase hex. `x-amz-date` must match `\d{8}T\d{6}Z` and its date prefix must equal the scope date (`sigv4.py:353-355`). `x-amz-content-sha256` must be 64 hex, one of three `STREAMING_*` literals, or `UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD` (`sigv4.py:363-383`). Every signed header must occur exactly once (`sigv4.py:346-351`; presign `161-167`), is decoded latin-1 and whitespace-collapsed. Query: ≤16 KiB, ≤100 fields, ASCII, strict UTF-8, no repeated key in presign mode (`sigv4.py:41-59,132-141`); presign fields are `X-Amz-Algorithm` (exact literal), `X-Amz-Credential` (same regex), `X-Amz-Date`, `X-Amz-Expires` (`int()` in 1..604800), `X-Amz-SignedHeaders` (sorted, unique, lowercase, must contain `host`), `X-Amz-Signature` (64 hex); `X-Amz-Security-Token` is refused (`sigv4.py:144-160,221-238`). Path is `raw_path`, ASCII, must start `/`, never normalized (`sigv4.py:70-81`). Service is pinned: `s3` or `s3express`, and `s3express` only for `s3:ListDirectoryBuckets` / `s3:CreateSession` (`sigv4.py:87-123`) — verified by probe (Express scope rejected for `s3:GetObject` and `s3:ListAllMyBuckets`, accepted for `s3:ListDirectoryBuckets`).
- **Side effects:** No Telegram, scratch-file, or object-metadata work occurs before `web/s3.py:4793`; every early return in `_handle` between 4207 and 4792 is an `_error` (checked exhaustively). Two writes are nevertheless made before the signature is verified: the `S3CredentialStore` is constructed at `web/s3.py:4760`, and its constructor runs `ensure_schema()` (`teledrive/s3/credentials.py:175-176` → `teledrive/storage/schema.py:590-624`, including `ALTER TABLE` steps on a lagging DB), and `db_connect()` (`web/s3.py:4759`) opens a connection that executes `PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL` (`teledrive/db.py:610-612`). The audit middleware also emits a log line before `call_next` (`web/s3.py:5013-5020`), which writes to the file handler when one is configured; it logs method/path/client and the access-key ID only, never the query string or secret.
- **Failure modes:** `AccessDenied` 403 (bad/absent Authorization, unknown key, service/scope refusal, missing presign fields, ambiguous Authorization+query, bad date/expiry), `SignatureDoesNotMatch` 403 (header/query/path mismatch, ambiguous signed header, repeated presign field, malformed signature), `RequestTimeTooSkewed` 403 (bad `x-amz-date`, >900 s skew, presign timestamp >900 s in the future), `InvalidToken` 403 (session token), `NotImplemented` 501 (unsupported method / non-multipart presigned POST), `InvalidArgument` 400 (malformed/oversized query, unparseable `X-Amz-Expires`) — note the generic `except ValueError` at `web/s3.py:4990` also lands here, `InvalidRequest` 400 (unsigned streaming without a signed `x-amz-trailer`), `XAmzContentSHA256Mismatch` 400, `BadDigest` 400 (`web/s3.py:4980-4987`).
- **Findings:**
  - [medium] **A presigned PUT to `?retention` / `?legal-hold` authenticates a body that no signature or checksum covers.** `_presigned_signature` accepts a PUT that signs only `x-amz-checksum-sha256` (`sigv4.py:204-220`) and, with no signed `x-amz-content-sha256`, canonicalizes the payload as `UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD` (`sigv4.py:249-252`). Verified by probe: such a URL (signed against the SHA-256 of retention document A) is `ACCEPTED` by `verify_header_signature`. But the object-lock handler validates the body **only** against `x-amz-content-sha256` (`web/s3.py:3104-3111`) and never calls `_verify_upload_sha256`, so the signed `x-amz-checksum-sha256` is ignored and document B (e.g. a 30-day COMPLIANCE lock shortened to one day, or `LegalHold` flipped OFF) parses and applies (`web/s3.py:3117-3154`). The exact header shape the verifier advertises as the safe presign-PUT contract is the one this handler does not enforce. Trigger is narrow (the URL must be hand-signed with a checksum header; boto3's `put_object_retention` presign signs no checksum and is rejected outright), but the capability is body-mutable, which is precisely what the presign rule exists to prevent.
  - [medium] **Database/schema work happens before authentication.** `web/s3.py:4758-4760` loads the master key, connects, and constructs `S3CredentialStore` (which calls `ensure_schema`) before `verify_header_signature` at 4793. Any unauthenticated request — including a junk `Authorization` — can therefore trigger a pending migration (`teledrive/storage/schema.py:596-624`) and a `journal_mode=WAL` change (`teledrive/db.py:610-612`) on the S3 port. It is code-determined and idempotent once migrated, so this is hygiene rather than data compromise, but `route_guard.validate_s3_query`'s promise of "no DB side effects before SigV4" (`teledrive/s3/route_guard.py:124`) is not true at the call site. The store only needs to exist as a `resolve` callback, so this is hoistable past the signature check.
  - [medium] **`presign_get_url` signs a different key than the one it was asked for** (already reported at `docs/API_REVIEW.md:853`; repeated here only because it is a defect in this module). `sigv4.py:290-292` drops empty segments (`if segment != ""`) while `_canonical_path` preserves them byte-for-byte (`sigv4.py:70-81`). Re-verified: key `a//b` → `/bucket/a/b`, key `dir/` → `/bucket/dir`, key `/leading` → `/bucket/leading`. The bearer URL opens a different object (or 404s) than the one the issuer validated.
  - [low] **The signing region is never checked.** The region is parsed from the credential scope and used only to derive the signing key (`sigv4.py:340,390-397`; presign `154,258,265-267`); nothing compares it to a configured deployment region. Probe: signatures minted with `eu-west-1` and `ap-south-1` are both `ACCEPTED`. AWS answers `AuthorizationHeaderMalformed`. No privilege escalation follows (the secret is still required), but region pinning is absent, and a future multi-region deployment could not isolate credentials by scope.
  - [low] **Replay is the design, but it is not documented.** There is no nonce or one-time state: a header-signed request remains valid for ±900 s (`sigv4.py:361-362`) and a presigned URL for up to `X-Amz-Expires` = 604800 s (`sigv4.py:229-235`). The module docstring (`sigv4.py:1-6`) says "short-lived query presigns" and nothing about replay, and there is no test of either window boundary. A captured signed PUT is replayable for 15 minutes; a leaked presigned PUT/DELETE for up to 7 days.
  - [low] **The canonical query is not wire-faithful.** `_canonical_query` decodes with `parse_qsl` and re-encodes (`sigv4.py:62-67`), so `%41`/`A` and `+`/`%20` collapse to one signing string; a holder of a presigned URL may re-encode any query value and keep the signature valid. The decoded value is identical, so no scope changes, but the claim "the signature covers these wire bytes" is false for the query — the same class of bug as the path finding above. `parse_qsl` also silently drops a bare `key` field (no `=`), which routing drops identically, so that part fails closed.
  - [nit] `x-amz-create-session-mode` is read without requiring it to be signed (`web/s3.py:1955`). It only selects an error code (400 vs 404) inside an operation that can never succeed, so impact is nil, but it is the one unsigned S3 control header still consulted.
  - [nit] `signing_service_hint` uses a prefix `re.match` with its own copies of the scope literals (`sigv4.py:91-93`) while the verifier uses `re.fullmatch` (`sigv4.py:332-337`). They agree for every header the verifier accepts, so routing cannot diverge today, but the duplication is a latent divergence point.
  - [nit] The payload line uses the raw `x-amz-content-sha256` (`sigv4.py:363,388`) while the signed-header block uses the whitespace-normalized value (`sigv4.py:352`). A client that signs AWS's normalized form but sends trailing whitespace is rejected; AWS would accept. Stricter, not unsafe.
  - [nit] A header with a non-ASCII name raises `UnicodeDecodeError` inside the signed-header loop (`sigv4.py:349`), surfacing as the generic `InvalidArgument` 400 at `web/s3.py:4990` rather than an S3 auth error.
  - *(verified sound, no finding)* Signature comparison is constant-time (`hmac.compare_digest` at `sigv4.py:269,399`); duplicate signed headers and repeated presign query keys are rejected (`346-351`, `138-140`); `host` is mandatory in both modes (`158`, `343`) so virtual-host bucketing cannot be redirected (`_virtual_host_bucket`, `web/s3.py:639-664`, consumes the same signed Host); `x-amz-content-sha256` is bound by the canonical last line even when it is absent from `SignedHeaders` (probe: signing `0`×64 and sending a different header value is rejected); the presigned PUT body is genuinely bound for PutObject, tagging and ACL (`_verify_upload_sha256`, `web/s3.py:328-330,3707`; `?tagging` `web/s3.py:487`; `?acl` `web/s3.py:2841`); duplicate `Authorization` headers cannot change the decision because Starlette returns the first value and the verifier, `signed_header_names` (`teledrive/s3/object_metadata.py:79-83`) and the audit log all read that same first value, while a proxy-joined pair fails the `fullmatch`; and no S3 success path exists before `web/s3.py:4793`.
- **Tests that should exist:**
  - `test_presigned_put_retention_body_is_bound_to_the_signed_checksum` — mint a presigned `PUT /{bucket}/{key}?retention` with only `x-amz-checksum-sha256` signed (the shape `sigv4.py:204` accepts), replay it with a different `Retention` document, and assert 400 `XAmzContentSHA256Mismatch`/`BadDigest` **and** that `catalog.retention(item)` is unchanged. Today the second body is applied. **This is the mandatory regression**: a presigned capability must deliver exactly the bytes it was signed for, especially when the bytes are a deletion-control policy.
  - `test_signature_minted_for_another_region_is_rejected` — valid secret, `Credential=…/eu-west-1/s3/aws4_request`; assert 400/403 `AuthorizationHeaderMalformed`. Today `verify_header_signature` returns the access key (probe-verified).
  - `test_skew_window_boundary_is_exactly_900_seconds` — `now` fixed, `x-amz-date` at −900, −901, +900, +901; assert accept / `RequestTimeTooSkewed` / accept / `RequestTimeTooSkewed` (`sigv4.py:361`).
  - `test_presigned_expiry_bounds_and_future_skew` — `X-Amz-Expires` of `0`, `-1`, `604800`, `604801`, and `"9"*30`; assert 400 except exactly 604800, and accept at `now = date + 604799 s`, reject at `+604801 s`; assert a `+901 s` future `X-Amz-Date` is `RequestTimeTooSkewed` (`sigv4.py:229-235`).
  - `test_presigned_url_round_trips_every_key_shape` — keys `a//b`, `dir/`, `/leading`, `a b`, `..%2F`, Unicode; assert `presign_get_url(...)` → GET returns the same key. Fails today for the first three (`sigv4.py:290-292`).
  - `test_no_schema_or_wal_write_before_signature_verification` — monkeypatch `teledrive.storage.schema.ensure_schema` and `connect_database` to record calls, send an unsigned `GET /`, and assert neither migration nor `journal_mode` runs before a 403 (or assert `S3CredentialStore` is constructed only after `verify_header_signature`).
  - `test_s3express_scope_matrix` — extend `tests/s3/test_s3_sigv4_defense.py:91` with the two positive cases (`s3:ListDirectoryBuckets`, `s3:CreateSession` → accepted) and the negatives `s3:ListBucket`, `s3:PutObjectRetention`, `s3:PutObject`, plus one presigned variant, so the pin in `sigv4.py:87-123` cannot regress.
  - `test_canonical_query_reencoding_cannot_change_scope` — sign `?prefix=A`, then request `?prefix=%41` (and `+`/`%20`); today both verify. If that is accepted as intentional, assert the decoded values are identical and document it; otherwise reject non-canonical re-encoding.
  - `test_x_amz_content_sha256_is_bound_when_unsigned` — signature over last-line `deadbeef…`, then send a different `x-amz-content-sha256` value; assert `SignatureDoesNotMatch` (locks in the property the verifier relies on instead of `SignedHeaders`).
  - `test_duplicate_authorization_header_uses_the_first_value` — first valid + second garbage → 200; first garbage + second valid → 403; joined `A, B` → 403 (documents Starlette `.get` semantics on which the code depends).
- **Verdict:** The core construction is careful — every signed header really is in the canonical form, `host` and `x-amz-date` are mandatory, the payload hash is bound by the canonical last line even when unsigned, comparisons are constant-time, duplicates fail closed, and the `s3express` scope is genuinely pinned to the two Express permissions — but the presigned-PUT checksum contract is not honoured by the object-lock handler (a body-mutable retention/legal-hold capability), the region is never validated, and the store that must resolve the secret performs schema work before the signature is checked, so this is a strong verifier with three loose joints rather than a bypass.

## replication and tiering — teledrive/s3/{replication,tiering}.py

- **Handler:** replication routes `web/s3.py:1562-1594` (Get/Put/DeleteBucketReplication), store `teledrive/s3/replication.py:326-419`, parser `replication.py:227-271`, engine decision `teledrive/storage/engine.py:208-310`; tiering routes `web/s3.py:1407-1447`, store+scheduler `teledrive/s3/tiering.py:332-498`, parser `tiering.py:234-291`; RestoreObject `web/s3.py:4189-4232` + `teledrive/s3/restore.py:49-128`; read gate `web/s3.py:3954-3965` and `teledrive/storage/catalog.py:243-269`.
- **Auth model:** Config writes are ordinary SigV4 scoped actions: `s3:Put/Get/DeleteBucketReplication` (`web/s3.py:4680-4685`), `s3:Put/Get/Delete/ListBucketIntelligentTieringConfiguration(s)` (`web/s3.py:4545-4554`), then the resource decision in `_authorize_resource` (`web/s3.py:4868-4873`). RestoreObject requires `s3:RestoreObject` (`web/s3.py:4626`), so Get-only principals cannot lift a gate — correct. **The data movement has no S3 permission at all:** once a rule is stored, the storage engine (`engine.py:233-257`, `270-310`) sends chunks as the operator's Telegram transports. Any principal holding `s3:PutBucketReplication` on a bucket can therefore cause every future matching single-part PUT in that bucket — and every existing object via the admin repair endpoint — to be copied to *any* configured Telegram account label (`web/s3.py:676-698`), with no per-bucket ownership or per-destination scope check.
- **Inputs:** Replication: ≤16 KiB defused XML, 1..10 rules (`replication.py:46-47,267-269`), rule ID ≤255 bytes and unique, `Status` ∈ {Enabled,Disabled}, prefix ≤1024 bytes and no C0 controls (`97-132`), destination ≤255 chars, must be a bare label (ARN → 501) and must be in the injected credential-label set (`135-168,372-379`), enabled prefixes must be pairwise disjoint (`208-225`), `DeleteMarkerReplication`/`ReplicaModifications`/`ExistingObjectReplication`/`Priority`/filter `And|Tag|ObjectSize*`/`Destination/StorageClass|Account|AccessControlTranslation|EncryptionConfiguration|Metrics|ReplicationTime` all rejected 501 (`64-78`). Tiering: ≤16 KiB defused XML, id `[A-Za-z0-9._-]{1,64}`, URL id must equal document id, `Status` ∈ {Enabled,Disabled}, exactly one `Prefix` or `Tag` filter, `Days` integer 1..36500, `AccessTier` ∈ {ARCHIVE_ACCESS,DEEP_ARCHIVE_ACCESS} (anything else → 501), deep days ≥ archive days (`tiering.py:160-291`). Restore: 16 KiB, `Days` 1..36500, Tier ∈ {Standard,Bulk,Expedited}, SELECT-style → 501 (`restore.py:49-105`). No input path can set an arbitrary `storage_class`: `x-amz-storage-class` is pinned to `STANDARD` and everything else is 501 (`web/s3.py:279-290,3416-3420`).
- **Side effects:** Replication PUT deletes+reinserts the `storage_replication_configs` row in one transaction and touches the bucket row as a fence (`replication.py:372-403`); DELETE removes the row (`405-419`) and bucket deletion explicitly clears both config tables (`catalog.py:1421-1439`). A matching upload then writes a real second Telegram message on the destination account and a `storage_replica_chunks` row per chunk (`engine.py:233-257`, `manifest.py:575-597`); a failed replica send is swallowed and only logged (`engine.py:254-257`) and is backfilled only by the operator-triggered repair endpoint (`web/app.py:3422-3434`). Tiering PUT writes `storage_tiering_configs`; the hourly scheduler (`web/app.py:1088-1108`) changes `storage_objects.storage_class` for up to 1000 rows per pass and optionally clears `restore_until_ns` (`tiering.py:425-498`). **No Telegram byte is ever moved, copied, compressed or deleted by tiering** — it is a catalog read gate, documented in `tiering.py:22-38`, `docs/UPGRADING.md:269-284` and the operator UI banner `web/static/s3-governance.html:405`. RestoreObject writes `restore_until_ns = now + Days*86400s` (`web/s3.py:4177-4182`); reads then pass until the deadline, after which the gate closes with no job (`catalog.py:263-266`).
- **Failure modes:** Replication: 404 `NoSuchBucket` (`web/s3.py:1563-1564`), 404 `ReplicationConfigurationNotFoundError` (`1570-1573`), 400 `MalformedXML` for oversize/parse/duplicate/unexpected/extra-namespace elements (`replication.py:91-94,235-266,186`), 400 `InvalidRequest` for bad status, duplicate IDs, overlapping enabled prefixes, unknown/absent destination, over-long prefix/destination, rule count (`163-167,187-199,208-225,267-269`), 501 `NotImplemented` for ARNs/unsupported fields and for HEAD (`web/s3.py:1594`), 400 `InvalidRequest` for a DELETE with a body (`1586-1591`), 403 from SigV4/ACL. A corrupt stored rule set makes `matching_destination` raise, and the engine catches it and disables replication for that object (fail closed, `engine.py:217-228`). Tiering: 404 `NoSuchBucket`, 404 `NoSuchConfiguration`, 405 `MethodNotAllowed`, 400 `InvalidArgument` (missing/duplicate Id/Status/Tiering, id mismatch, bad Days/filter/tag, empty URL id), 501 for unknown AccessTier and SELECT restore, 400 `MalformedXML` for entities/oversize/unknown elements/body on DELETE. Restore: 404 `NoSuchKey`/`NoSuchVersion`, 403 `InvalidObjectState` for a non-archived object, 409 `RestoreAlreadyInProgress` while a window is open, 400 `MalformedXML`, 501 SELECT, 202 success (`web/s3.py:4225-4232,4169-4186`). A single corrupt tiering document raises inside `apply_due` (`tiering.py:459-468`) and aborts the whole pass for every bucket; the hourly loop only logs it (`web/app.py:1103-1104`).
- **Findings:**
  - [high] **Deleting an object never deletes its cross-account replica, then GC drops the only coordinates that could delete it.** Physical GC deletes the *primary* chunk messages (`gc_collect.py:461-480`), then unconditionally `DELETE FROM storage_replica_chunks WHERE object_id = …` (`gc_collect.py:485-486`) without ever calling `transport.delete` for the replica account/message. Key rotation does the same: it drops replica rows and relies on a "repair sweep" that does not exist automatically (`engine.py:855-861`). Grep confirms nothing in the tree deletes a replica coordinate (`transport.delete` is only ever given a `ChunkReference` from `storage_chunks`). Result: a version delete, bucket lifecycle expiry or GC pass leaves a complete second copy of the (plaintext unless SSE-C) object in the other Telegram account, forever and unreferenced — DeleteObject does not mean "all copies gone", and the operator can no longer even find the message. The same leak occurs when a rule is retargeted: `append_replica` UPDATEs the row to the new target and orphans the old message (`manifest.py:584-591`). `docs/S3_SPECS.md:89` promises replicas are "never deleted implicitly", so the media survives while the audit row does not.
  - [high] **The archive gate is bypassable with CopyObject and SelectObjectContent.** CopyObject (`web/s3.py:3381-3599`) never calls `object_read_state`; a non-range copy is a metadata alias that inserts a fresh object row with the default `storage_class='STANDARD'` (`teledrive/storage/copy.py:77-107`). A principal with GetObject+PutObject can therefore "restore" an archived object for free by copying it to another key and reading the copy — AWS answers 403 `InvalidObjectState` for an archived copy source. `SelectObjectContent` is worse: it streams the archived object's real bytes (`web/s3.py:4144`) with no gate at all.
  - [high] **Public share links serve archived objects with no gate.** `_stream_share` resolves the pinned version and streams it (`web/app.py:5933-5983`) without calling `_rest_read_gate` (only 3804, 5832 and 6198 do), so an existing `/share/{token}` keeps reading an object that has since been demoted to `ARCHIVE_ACCESS`/`DEEP_ARCHIVE_ACCESS`. This is the same bypass already recorded at `docs/API_REVIEW.md:908`; it is still present.
  - [high] **Replication is silently not applied to multipart and SSE-C uploads.** `put_file` sets `replicate=dedup` (`engine.py:129-131`), and `dedup` is forced `False` for SSE-C (`engine.py:108-112`) and for non-content-addressed staging uploads (`engine.py:119-124`). Multipart completion re-owns staged chunks and never calls `_replicate_chunk` (no match in `teledrive/s3/multipart.py`). So a bucket rule covers only single-part plaintext PUTs: the normal path for large files and every SSE-C object are excluded, while `GetBucketReplication` still reports the rule as authoritative. `docs/S3_SPECS.md:88` claims "prefix filters are enforced per object at upload", which is true only for that one upload shape.
  - [high] **A replication rule cannot be honoured per bucket once content is deduplicated.** Replication is resolved per bucket/prefix (`engine.py:208-231,332-336`) but recorded per *content* object (`manifest.py:111-119`, PK `(object_id, ordinal)`), and dedup is global, not bucket-scoped (`engine.py:163-169`). A deduped PUT returns early without replicating at all (`engine.py:125-128`), so bucket B's rule is silently not applied to content that bucket A already stored; the replica rows keep pointing at A's destination. Running repair for B then repoints the single replica row to B's target (`engine.py:280-309` + `manifest.py:584-591`), breaking A. Two buckets with different destinations can never both be satisfied, and which one wins depends on upload/repair order. The module docstring discloses "one replica per chunk" but not this cross-bucket consequence.
  - [medium] **Deleting the replication configuration re-enables replication to the operator-wide default.** `matching_destination` returns `(False, None)` once the row is gone (`replication.py:303-306`), and the engine treats `configured=False` as "use `storage.replica_account`" (`engine.py:229-231`; asserted in `tests/s3/test_s3_replication.py:277-292`). An operator who DELETEs the rule to stop copying gets the opposite: every subsequent object in that bucket is copied to the global account. AWS `DeleteBucketReplication` means stop.
