  - [medium] **Tiering demotes objects under legal hold or retention.** `_conditions` filters only on bucket, `status='ready'`, prefix and tag (`tiering.py:408-423`); neither `legal_hold` nor `retention_until_ns` is consulted before a class transition (`tiering.py:477-493`), and the reset transition clears `restore_until_ns` (`437,493`). No bytes are destroyed, but a WORM-protected object can be made unreadable and its restore window discarded while the hold is active. AWS allows storage-class transitions under Object Lock, so this may be a policy choice — it is not documented as one.
  - [medium] **The DEEP transition is starved by an arithmetic double-subtraction.** `remaining = limit - changed`, then the deep call passes `limit=remaining - changed` (`tiering.py:474-481`), i.e. `limit - 2*changed`. Once earlier configs have consumed more than half the pass budget, later configs get a non-positive limit and deep-eligible objects are never promoted. Because the ARCHIVE transition explicitly requires `idle < deep_ns` (`482-486`), those objects do not merely arrive one pass late — they stay `STANDARD` (or `ARCHIVE_ACCESS`) indefinitely while the budget is consumed the same way each hour.
  - [medium] **Tiering configurations in one bucket may overlap and then fight each other.** Nothing validates filters across documents: `put` stores whatever `parse_document` accepted (`tiering.py:370-395`), and `parse_document` only checks within one document (`234-291`). `apply_due` applies each config's "accessed → STANDARD" reset independently (`489-493`), so a broad config (`prefix=""`, 365 days) undoes a narrow config (`cold/`, 1 day) for objects idle 1..365 days, every hour, with the outcome depending on `config_id` ordering (`449-452`). AWS requires non-overlapping Intelligent-Tiering filters for exactly this reason.
  - [medium] **Public-site and share reads do not refresh the tiering clock.** `record_object_access` is called only from the S3 GET/HEAD path (`web/s3.py:3979`) and `_stream_object_response` (`web/app.py:3813-3817`); `_serve_site` (`web/app.py:6198-6224`) and `_stream_share` (`5968-5983`) gate or stream without it, as do Select (`web/s3.py:4144`) and the copy/part-copy sources (`2340,2611,3572`). Since `apply_due` decides on `COALESCE(last_accessed_ns, created_at)` (`tiering.py:501-505`), an object read daily only through the public site is "idle" and is demoted; the site then starts answering its uniform 404 and S3 answers 403. The claim that REST and public-site reads were fixed to record access is true for REST (`web/app.py:3814-3816`) but **not** for the public site.
  - [medium] **The destination allowlist accepts unusable accounts.** `_replication_destinations` unions every `telegram_sessions.account` with `account` non-empty, ignoring `authorized`/`health_checked_at` and whether the account is loaded (`web/s3.py:676-698`); a session that is expired or revoked is accepted by PUT (`replication.py:340-345,372-379`). Every chunk send then raises "not an available credential set" (`user_mode.py:728-732`) and is swallowed (`engine.py:254-257`), so the API reports a working configuration while `replica_coverage()` reports missing copies and no sweep ever fixes them. It is fail-safe (no misdirected write) but dishonest.
  - [low] **An unknown `storage_class` fails open while RestoreObject fails closed.** The gate tests exact membership in the two archive literals (`catalog.py:259`, `restore.py:110`), so any other stored value (`GLACIER`, a typo, a future class) is immediately readable, yet RestoreObject answers 403 `InvalidObjectState` and listings/attributes echo the raw label (`web/s3.py:3847-3851`). No current API can produce such a value (`web/s3.py:279-290`), so this is defense-in-depth, unverified end to end except by direct SQL.
  - [low] **`_transition` is not the conditional update the module claims.** The docstring says "every transition is a conditional SQL update" (`tiering.py:52-54`), but the code SELECTs ids and then UPDATEs by id only (`429-441`), so a read or restore that lands between the two statements is ignored: the object is still archived, and a freshly opened `restore_until_ns` can be cleared by the reset transition. Outcome is idempotent, not race-free.
  - [low] **A stored-config corruption stalls all tiering.** `_decode` and the archive/deep type checks run inside the per-config loop and raise (`tiering.py:459-468`), rolling back the whole pass; the hourly loop logs and retries forever (`web/app.py:1103-1104`). One bad row means no bucket is tiered. `list_xml` also re-parses every stored document (`tiering.py:325`) and would 500 on a corrupt prefix.
  - [nit] `_prefixes` rejects C0 controls but not DEL 0x7f, unlike `_filter`'s prefix check (`replication.py:130` vs `tiering.py:175`); destination length is counted in characters, not UTF-8 bytes (`replication.py:156`); `matching_destination` runs `inspect(db.engine).has_table(...)` on every upload (`replication.py:301`).
  - *(verified sound, no finding)* Replication is genuinely best-effort: a replica failure never fails the publish and never rotates to another account (`engine.py:244-257`, `user_mode.py:721-732`); the decision is resolved once per object, so a rule edit mid-upload cannot split one object across destinations (`engine.py:332-336`); delete-marker replication is rejected 501 and no destination-delete path exists, so replication cannot delete data it did not create (`replication.py:64-78,177-180`); SSE-C content is replicated as ciphertext with the client key never stored, and repair verifies the ciphertext digest before resending (`engine.py:296-305`); `object_aliases` protect shared content from GC (`gc_collect.py:439-442`); the read gate itself is correct for Get/Head including ranges and versions, uses 403 `InvalidObjectState`, reports `x-amz-restore` with a real expiry, closes on deadline without a job, and refuses restore of a non-archived object (`web/s3.py:3954-3965,4021-4023`, `catalog.py:259-266`, `restore.py:108-128`); the tiering `Days`/deep-vs-archive bounds and duplicate-AccessTier rejection are strict (`tiering.py:202-231,264-280`); and the "metadata only, not Glacier" caveat is repeated in the module, the upgrade guide and the operator panel.
- **Tests that should exist:**
  - `test_gc_collect_deletes_replica_messages_before_dropping_rows` — upload with a replica, delete/expire the version, run `GcCollector` with `ReplicaAwareTelegram`; assert `transport.delete` received both the primary and the replica `ChunkReference` (and that `backend.data` holds no replica key) before `storage_replica_chunks` is emptied. **This is the mandatory regression:** today the row is deleted while the remote copy remains, which is irreversible and silent.
  - `test_archived_object_cannot_be_copied_or_selected_without_restore` — `storage_class='ARCHIVE_ACCESS'`, `restore_until_ns=NULL`; CopyObject to a new key → 403 `InvalidObjectState`; `POST ?select&select-type=2` → 403; after a restore window both 200. Fails today (copy yields a readable `STANDARD` alias; select streams the bytes).
  - `test_public_read_surfaces_refresh_the_tiering_clock` — GET `/site/{b}/{k}` and `/share/{token}`; assert `access_count` incremented and `last_accessed_ns` advanced, and that a share of an archived object is not served (`_rest_read_gate`-equivalent 404).
  - `test_tiering_deep_transition_is_not_starved_by_earlier_configs` — two buckets, limit small enough that the first consumes >half; object in the second idle past `deep_days`; assert `DEEP_ARCHIVE_ACCESS` (today `STANDARD`/`ARCHIVE_ACCESS`).
  - `test_overlapping_tiering_configs_in_one_bucket_are_rejected` — `prefix=""` 365 days plus `prefix="cold/"` 1 day; assert PUT of the second → 400 `InvalidRequest`, or that one scheduler pass leaves the narrow config's archived object archived.
  - `test_replication_covers_multipart_and_sse_c_uploads` — create a bucket rule over `""`; multipart-complete one object and SSE-C PUT another; assert `storage_replica_chunks` has one row per chunk for both (today zero).
  - `test_unknown_storage_class_fails_closed` — set `storage_class='GLACIER'` by SQL; GET/HEAD → 403 `InvalidObjectState`, RestoreObject applies (today GET is 200 and restore is 403).
  - `test_delete_replication_does_not_enable_the_global_default` — set `storage.replica_account`, DELETE the config, upload a non-matching key; assert no replica rows (today it replicates).
  - `test_replication_destination_must_be_an_authorized_session` — `telegram_sessions.authorized=0`; PUT naming that label → 400 `InvalidRequest` (today 200 and every send fails silently).
  - `test_tiering_respects_legal_hold_and_retention` — a legal-held object past `archive_days`; assert either no transition or a documented, asserted exception.
- **Verdict:** The parsers and SQL state are strictly bounded and replication is honestly best-effort, but the state machines are unsafe at the edges: deletion drops replica bookkeeping without deleting replica media, the archive gate is bypassable by copy/select/share, multipart and SSE-C uploads silently escape replication, dedup makes per-bucket destinations unenforceable, and the tiering scheduler both ignores WORM state and mis-computes its own pass limits.

## legacy import and GC administration — web/app.py

- **Handler:** per route, line numbers as re-read at the end of this review (web/app.py was being edited concurrently during the review — earlier reads were 22–34 lines lower, so re-anchor before acting): `import_legacy_folder_jobs` web/app.py:5180; `index_legacy_current_versions` web/app.py:2413; `admin_delete_bucket` web/app.py:3692; `list_stale_pending_objects` web/app.py:5304; `abandon_stale_pending_object` web/app.py:5322; `gc_status` web/app.py:4842; `gc_toggle` web/app.py:4866. Delegated logic: `enqueue_legacy_folder` teledrive/storage/legacy_bridge.py:27-85; `BucketCatalog.index_legacy_current_heads` teledrive/storage/catalog.py:835-874; `BucketCatalog.delete_bucket` teledrive/storage/catalog.py:1337-1449; `ManifestStore.stale_pending`/`abandon_stale_pending` teledrive/storage/manifest.py:294-379; `RetainedMediaGC.state`/`set_enabled`/`metrics` teledrive/storage/gc_collect.py:110-219.
- **Auth model:** every route is `Depends(require_auth)` (web/app.py:603-657) and every mutating route calls `_same_origin` (web/app.py:321-333), so `x-teledrive-csrf: 1` is mandatory (and a cross-origin `Origin` is rejected when present).
  - **Session principal — admin-only:** `GET/PUT /api/v1/admin/storage/gc` match `_ADMIN_ONLY_PREFIXES` (`"/api/v1/admin/storage/gc"`, web/permissions.py:81,138-139), so all methods require role `admin` via `_required_roles`/`_enforce_role_policy` (web/app.py:531-557). Covered by tests/web/test_auth_rbac.py:401-420.
  - **Session principal — NOT admin-only:** `DELETE /api/v1/admin/storage/buckets/{bucket}`, `POST .../versions/index-current`, `POST .../jobs/import-legacy`, `GET .../pending-stale`, `POST .../pending-stale/{id}/abandon` are all reachable by `operator` (write role, `_ROLE_WRITE`, web/app.py:527,549) and any non-admin-only read by `viewer`. This is consistent with the existing object-delete job surfaces, but a review should record it: an operator can enqueue filesystem reads and mutate version history.
  - **Bearer API key:** `require_auth` returns at web/app.py:626-637 **before** `_enforce_role_policy`, so the admin-only classification of `/storage/gc` does not exist for keys. A key minted with `put:/api/v1/admin/storage/gc` (or `admin:*`, which expands over the live route table, teledrive/api_keys.py:90-128) can enable GC and read status with no role and no human gate; see the pre-existing `[high]` at `docs/API_REVIEW.md:278` for the collect twin. `admin:*` still has to be minted by an admin session, so this is a delegation decision, not an external bypass.
  - **Audit actor:** `gc_collect` now writes durable `_audit` rows (web/app.py:4922-4938) and `gc_toggle` writes only `gc_state.updated_by` plus the volatile `_log` (web/app.py:4881-4884); `abandon` and `import-legacy` write neither (only the middleware access log, web/app.py:1359-1377).
- **Inputs:** (all bodies are Pydantic models with no `extra="forbid"`, so unknown fields are dropped)
  - `LegacyFolderImportRequest` (web/app.py:1490-1494): `bucket` 3–63 chars, `prefix` ≤512 (default `""`), `max_files` 1–1000 (default 100), `execute` bool default False. **No path is accepted**; the source is `effective_config().source_dir`, resolved with `strict=True` and required `is_relative_to(_storage_job_root())` (web/app.py:5186-5193). Prefix is re-validated in legacy_bridge.py:42-45 (no leading `/`, no `\`, no empty/`.`/`..` segment). Bucket must already exist (legacy_bridge.py:46-47).
  - `VersionIndexRequest` (web/app.py:1503-1505): `execute` default False, `limit` 1–1000. **No path and no bucket/key** — this endpoint is pure SQL, so the review's "same path question" does not apply to it.
  - `PendingUploadRecoveryRequest` (web/app.py:1479-1481): `execute` default False, `min_age_seconds` 60–2592000. `object_id` is an unvalidated path `str` (no max length); SQL-bound, so no injection, but any string yields a 200 instead of a 404.
  - `GCToggleRequest` (web/app.py:1439-1445): `enabled` required bool; `confirm` optional string compared exactly to `"PHYSICAL-GC"` only when enabling (web/app.py:4869-4871); `grace_seconds` optional, 3600–7776000, re-checked by `set_enabled` (gc_collect.py:127-129).
  - GC status takes no input; the stale-pending list takes `min_age_seconds` 60–2592000 and `limit` 1–1000 as bounded query params (web/app.py:5305-5306).
- **Side effects:**
  - **import-legacy:** opens the DB, acquires the *global uploader lease* (name `uploader.LEASE_NAME`, TTL `uploader.LEASE_TTL_SECONDS`, web/app.py:5198-5201), walks `cfg.source_dir` on the local filesystem (legacy_bridge.py:54), and for each allowed regular file: `stat` + `open(O_NOFOLLOW)` (jobs.py:60-77) and, when `execute`, one INSERT into `storage_job_rows` with a deterministic UUIDv5 id (`scan_id`, jobs.py:91-102,134-144), one commit per file. At most `max_files` ≤1000 rows per request; re-runs are idempotent (`already_queued`). Never uploads (`uploads_started: 0`, web/app.py:5234). Lease is released in `finally` (web/app.py:5236-5237).
  - **index-current:** reads `storage_object_heads` LEFT JOIN `storage_published_versions` and, when `execute`, INSERTs one `storage_published_versions` row per missing current head (`committed_ns=time.time_ns()`), one commit for the batch (catalog.py:844-874). It re-checks `heads.key_hash == _keyhash(objects.object_key)` and aborts the whole request with 409 on mismatch (catalog.py:860-866). No filesystem, no Telegram, no bucket mutation.
  - **admin_delete_bucket:** calls the shared, fixed `BucketCatalog.delete_bucket` (web/app.py:3698; same function used by the S3 gateway at web/s3.py:2004 and the REST route at web/app.py:5600+), i.e. the 13-table orphan fix is not bypassed. It refuses when `storage_objects` has **any** row for the bucket, or when `storage_multipart_uploads` has history, then deletes bucket-scoped tag/lifecycle/CORS/website/metadata/ACL/policy/ABAC/encryption/replication/tiering/report/notification/logging/quota/pin/share rows, orphan tag/annotation rows, and finally the bucket row (catalog.py:1337-1449).
  - **pending-stale (GET):** read-only query over `storage_objects.status='pending'` joined to an **expired** `writer_fences` row, filtered by age, limit 1–1000; the writer token is never selected (manifest.py:294-330).
  - **abandon:** `UPDATE writer_fences SET token='abandoned:<id>', lease_until=now WHERE lease_until < now` (rowcount must be 1), then `UPDATE storage_objects SET status='failed', error=..., updated_at=now WHERE status='pending' AND updated_at <= now-min_age`, one commit (manifest.py:353-379). Chunks and Telegram messages are **retained**.
  - **gc_status:** `RetainedMediaGC.state()` and `.metrics()` — several aggregate SELECTs over chunks/heads/versions/queue/collected (gc_collect.py:110-219). No writes; returns the last toggle's `updated_by`.
  - **gc_toggle:** UPDATE/INSERT `storage_gc_state` (`enabled`, `updated_ns`, `updated_by`, optional `grace_seconds`) and commit (gc_collect.py:124-141); in-memory/file log only.
- **Failure modes:**
  - import-legacy: no/bad session → 401; key without scope → 403; operator+CSRF missing → 403; `TELEDRIVE_JOB_SOURCE_ROOT` unset/missing/not a directory → 503 (web/app.py:2396-2407); `source_dir` unresolvable → 503 (5189-5191); `source_dir` outside approved root → 403 (5192-5193); live lease → 409 (5198-5201); bucket missing → 404; prefix/policy errors → 422; lease heartbeat lost → **uncaught RuntimeError → 500** (5209-5213, no `except RuntimeError` around `enqueue_legacy_folder`); cancel/disconnect → 500/499 with any already-committed jobs left queued.
  - index-current: 401/403 as above; CSRF → 403; limit out of range → 422; inconsistent key metadata → 409 (catalog.py:866); two concurrent executes can raise `IntegrityError` on the `published_versions.object_id` PK (catalog.py:82-88) — not caught (web/app.py:2420-2426 catches only `RuntimeError`) → **500**; `manifests.get()` between SELECT and INSERT raising `FileNotFoundError` → 500.
  - admin_delete_bucket: 404 for unknown bucket; 409 with a constant message for object/multipart history (catalog.py:1355-1358); no 500 paths of note; error text contains no path or SQL.
  - pending-stale: 401/403; 422 for out-of-range `min_age_seconds`/`limit` (manifest.py:301-302, web/app.py:5305-5306).
  - abandon: 401/403/CSRF 403; 422 for `min_age_seconds` outside 60..2592000; unknown/ineligible/stale-lost → **200** with `abandoned: false` (no 404/409), so a client cannot distinguish "never existed" from "lost the race".
  - gc_status: 401/403 only; no 5xx paths (any SQL error propagates as 500 with a generic detail).
  - gc_toggle: 401/403 (session operator is blocked); CSRF 403; enabling without the exact token → 422; `grace_seconds` out of range → 422 from Pydantic or 422 from `set_enabled`; no error text carries a path or SQL.
- **Findings:**
  - [high] **The import's "legacy uploader is running" gate is dead: both lease constants are empty/zero and never assigned anywhere.** `teledrive/uploader.py:33-34` defines `LEASE_NAME = ""` and `LEASE_TTL_SECONDS = 0`; exhaustive grep over the repo finds no assignment to either (reads only at web/app.py:5199, 5210-5211, 5237 and the test at tests/storage/test_legacy_import_admin.py:99). Therefore (a) the lease is stored under the empty name, so it can only collide with another import that also uses the empty name — it can never see the real legacy uploader's lease, and the 409 at web/app.py:5201 cannot enforce the documented "stop the legacy uploader first" rule (legacy_bridge.py:3-5) or prevent a concurrent import from double-queueing uploads; (b) `lease_until = now + 0`, so the lease is already expired and `acquire_lease`'s `lease_until < now` branch (teledrive/db.py:450) lets a second request ≥1 s later steal it; (c) `renew_lease` requires `lease_until >= now` (teledrive/db.py:476), so the very first heartbeat (10 s after acquisition, web/app.py:5205-5214) **always fails**, raising the uncaught `RuntimeError` and returning 500 mid-import with a partially populated queue. The existing test passes only because it seeds the same empty `LEASE_NAME` with a 120 s TTL in the same second (tests/storage/test_legacy_import_admin.py:99-103), which is exactly the collision the production wiring lacks.
  - [high] **One import request walks and sorts the entire source tree before `max_files` can stop it.** `sorted(iter_source_files(str(root)))` at teledrive/storage/legacy_bridge.py:54 materialises every path under `source_dir` in memory; the `max_files` bound is only tested after a file has been processed (`legacy_bridge.py:83-84`). A directory with millions of entries therefore costs an unbounded `os.walk` + `O(n log n)` sort + all paths in RAM per request, even for a `dry_run` with `max_files=1`, and the global uploader lease is held for that entire duration (web/app.py:5198-5237). No heartbeat runs during the walk/sort (the heartbeat is only called inside the per-file loop, legacy_bridge.py:55-56), so the 10 s lease check cannot even start until the whole tree is enumerated. Authenticated-operator DoS plus a long block on the legacy uploader; the row count itself is correctly capped at 1000.
  - [medium] **`admin_delete_bucket` can never delete a bucket that has ever held an object, and its error text says otherwise.** `delete_bucket` refuses if `SELECT id FROM storage_objects WHERE bucket = :bucket LIMIT 1` returns anything (catalog.py:1344-1358), with `"Bucket has object history; safe physical GC required"`. But physical GC does not delete the `storage_objects` row: it sets `status='gc-collected'` (gc_collect.py:510-512), and no production code path deletes `storage_objects` (only tests do). So after a successful, complete GC the bucket is physically empty yet still 409s, and the message directs the operator to an action that cannot satisfy the precondition. (`storage_gc_collected.bucket` rows are likewise never cleared by `delete_bucket`.) Positive: it genuinely calls the shared fixed `delete_bucket`, so the 13-table orphan class fixed on the S3 path is not reintroduced here.
  - [medium] **`gc_status` reports gates that are not enforced and omits the one field the GC plan contract uses.** `gates.independent_payload_backup_verified` and `gates.restore_drill_verified` are hardcoded `False`, `writer_barrier_verified` hardcoded `True` (web/app.py:4853-4858), while `gc_collect` enforces only `enabled` (gc_collect.py:382; web/app.py:4914-4916) — so the status screen shows two failed safety gates while deletion is in fact permitted. There is no `deletion_permitted` key at all, although the GC plan/safety contract defines it (`teledrive/storage/gc.py:147`, `teledrive/storage/gc_safety.py:174`) and `scripts/ops/gc_dry_run.py:64` reads it. `enabled` and `grace_seconds` do come from the DB and are truthful (gc_collect.py:110-122), and `updated_by` is the last actor. Leakage is limited to that username plus aggregate counts, and the route is admin-only for sessions.
  - [medium] **`gc_toggle` has no durable audit and cannot reconstruct on/off history; disabling is not a kill switch.** The toggle writes an in-memory `_log` line (web/app.py:4881-4884) and the single last-writer `updated_by` in `storage_gc_state` (gc_collect.py:130-135), while its twin `gc_collect` was just given durable `_audit` start/done rows (web/app.py:4922-4938). Re-enabling overwrites the previous actor/timestamp, so after a disable-then-enable the trail cannot show that GC was ever off. Separately, `set_enabled` never inspects `storage_gc_leases` and `collect()` reads `enabled` once (gc_collect.py:382) and never re-checks per object, so toggling off during a run does not stop the remaining deletions — the pre-existing `[medium]` at `docs/API_REVIEW.md:281`, restated here as toggle semantics: a 200 `{enabled: false}` is not a promise that deletion has stopped.
  - [medium] **Abandoning a stale PENDING upload permanently orphans any bytes it already sent to Telegram.** `abandon_stale_pending` only flips the object to `status='failed'` and keeps its chunks (manifest.py:369-374). Every physical GC scope requires `objects.status == 'ready'` **and** a `storage_published_versions` row (gc_collect.py:288-300; the liveness re-check also demands `ready`, gc_collect.py:431-435), so a failed/PENDING object is never a candidate; `gc-candidates` is explicitly preview-only and never deletes (`web/app.py:2333-2355`). Journaled chunks of abandoned uploads therefore have no reclaim path and accumulate. The endpoint's docstring ("retain Telegram journal", web/app.py:5321) is accurate about retention but silent that retention is forever. Abandon also writes no audit line and stores no actor — `error` and the fence token name the object, not the operator.
  - [low] **Preview and execute disagree for `abandon` beyond the first 1000 stale rows, and execute conflates "not eligible" with "lost the race".** The dry run searches `stale_pending(..., limit=1000)` (web/app.py:5334-5339) and returns `eligible: false` for a perfectly stale object that happens to sort past row 1000; execute addresses the object directly (manifest.py:351-352) and would abandon it. Execute returns 200 `{eligible: <affected>, abandoned: <affected>}` for unknown ids as well, so callers cannot tell a typo from a competing heartbeat.
  - [low] **`index-current` is repeat-safe but not concurrency-safe, and it is writable by `operator`.** Repeat runs are genuinely idempotent: only heads with no `storage_published_versions` row are selected, and the batch commits once (catalog.py:844-874; verified by tests/storage/test_storage_versions.py:110-149). Two simultaneous `execute` calls both select the same ids and both insert; the second hits the `object_id` primary key (catalog.py:82-88) and raises an uncaught `IntegrityError` → 500 (web/app.py:2420-2426 only maps `RuntimeError`). The route is not in any admin-only list, so an `operator` can permanently add publication-index rows that change restore/history views (benign content-wise — only proven heads are promoted — but it is durable state).
  - [low] **Import mislabels a vanished source directory as a missing bucket, and logs no actor or action.** `enqueue_legacy_folder` re-resolves `cfg.source_dir` at legacy_bridge.py:48, and its `FileNotFoundError` is caught in the handler and reported as 404 `"Destination object bucket not found"` (web/app.py:5223-5224) even though nothing is wrong with the bucket. The handler also emits no audit row (contrast `create_storage_upload_job`, web/app.py:5159) for a request that reads the server filesystem and can insert 1000 queue rows; only the generic middleware line records it.
  - [low] **Error text is mostly clean, with one unproven hole.** `admin_delete_bucket`, `gc_toggle`, `gc_status` and `abandon` use constant strings or validated messages, and `index-current`'s only `str(exc)` is a constant (`RuntimeError` text from catalog.py:866). `import-legacy` returns `str(exc)` for `(UploadJobError, ValueError, OSError)` (web/app.py:5225-5226); no path-bearing OSError is reachable today because `iter_source_files`/`_source_identity` swallow or convert per-file `OSError`s (legacy_bridge.py:79-82), but the catch is broad enough that a future one would be returned verbatim — *(unverified)* whether any current `OSError` path reaches it with a path.
  - [nit] `gc_status` calls `gcx.state()` twice (web/app.py:4851 and 4857), issuing two identical SELECTs per request.
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** path containment for the legacy import is correct — `source_dir` is `resolve(strict=True)` and compared with the resolved approved root (web/app.py:5186-5193), `iter_source_files` does not follow symlinked directories or files (`os.walk(..., followlinks=False)` + explicit `islink` skips, teledrive/uploader.py:192-211), and `_source_identity` rejects a symlinked/outside final path and opens with `O_NOFOLLOW` (jobs.py:60-77). The same checks are repeated at enqueue time (`legacy_bridge.py:61-76`).
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** `abandon` cannot orphan a live writer. It requires both an expired `writer_fences.lease_until < now` and `objects.updated_at <= now - min_age` (default 1 h), consumes the fence with a conditional UPDATE whose rowcount must be 1, and only then transitions the possibly-`pending` row (manifest.py:342-378). A heartbeat that wins first makes the fence update affect 0 rows; after abandonment the writer's token no longer matches, so `heartbeat_writer`, `append` and `finish` fail closed (manifest.py:282-292,471-473). Covered by tests/storage/test_storage_pending_admin.py:82-110.
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** the stale-pending listing never returns the bearer-style writer token (`manifest.py:294-300`; asserted at tests/storage/test_storage_pending_admin.py:40), and neither GC endpoint returns file paths.
- **Tests that should exist:**
  - `test_legacy_import_lease_is_live_and_blocks_a_real_uploader` — assert `teledrive.uploader.LEASE_NAME != ""` and `LEASE_TTL_SECONDS >= 60`; then acquire that same lease with an independent holder (`"legacy-bot"`, TTL 120) and `POST .../jobs/import-legacy` with `execute: true`; assert 409 and zero jobs. Then free it, monkeypatch `web.app.time.monotonic` to advance 11 s during the scan, and assert the import returns 200 with the expected jobs instead of the current `RuntimeError` → 500. **This is the mandatory regression:** it is the only test that proves the endpoint's advertised mutual-exclusion gate exists, and it fails today on both the empty name and the zero TTL.
  - `test_legacy_import_scan_is_bounded_before_sorting` — create a tree much larger than `max_files` (or a counting `iter_source_files` stub) and assert the walk stops at the cap / does not call `sorted()` on the whole tree when `max_files=1`, with wall time independent of total file count (or that the traversal is paged). Fails today by construction (legacy_bridge.py:54 vs :83).
  - `test_delete_bucket_after_full_gc_succeeds_or_says_why` — publish an object, unlink it, enable GC, `collect`, then `DELETE /api/v1/admin/storage/buckets/{bucket}`; assert either 200 with zero rows in every bucket-keyed table (including `storage_gc_collected`) or a 409 whose message names a precondition the operator can actually meet. Pins the `[medium]` mismatch between the message and the implementation.
  - `test_gc_status_deletion_permitted_matches_what_collect_enforces` — with GC enabled, assert `status["state"]["enabled"] is True` **and** that the response's permission signal (currently absent/`gates`) agrees with whether `POST .../gc/collect` with a valid confirm actually deletes; flip the backup/restore gate values and assert they change behaviour or are removed from the payload. No such test exists.
  - `test_gc_toggle_records_each_transition_with_its_actor` — enable then disable as two different admin sessions, then read the durable audit trail (`/api/v1/admin/audit?action=gc.toggle` or `gc_state` history) and assert both transitions and both actors are present. Fails today (only the latest `updated_by`, no `_audit`).
  - `test_disabling_gc_mid_run_stops_further_deletes` (already requested at `docs/API_REVIEW.md:309`) — restated because `gc_toggle` is the endpoint that claims to be the owner's off switch.
  - `test_abandon_reports_journaled_chunks_and_their_reclaim_path` — abandon a PENDING upload with N journaled chunks; assert the response exposes N/bytes and that a documented admin path can later reclaim them (or that the endpoint refuses without an explicit "bytes will be unreclaimable" acknowledgment). Fails today by omission.
  - `test_abandon_preview_agrees_with_execute_beyond_the_sample_limit` — create >1000 stale pending rows, target one outside the first page; assert preview says eligible (or the preview is paged), then execute abandons it.
  - `test_index_current_is_idempotent_and_serialises_concurrent_execute` — run two `execute` calls on separate connections against the same missing head; assert one 200 and one 200/409 with exactly one `storage_published_versions` row and no 500 (today the PK race is an uncaught `IntegrityError`).
  - `test_import_source_vanished_between_checks_is_not_reported_as_missing_bucket` — delete `source_dir` after the handler's pre-check; assert 503/422 with a source-specific message, not 404 "Destination object bucket not found".
- **Verdict:** The path-containment, CSRF and live-writer fencing on this surface are genuinely careful, and `admin_delete_bucket` correctly reuses the fixed `delete_bucket`; but the legacy import's concurrency gate is a no-op (empty lease name, zero TTL, heartbeat guaranteed to fail), its scan is unbounded before `max_files`, and the GC administration surface misstates its own gates, leaves abandoned upload bytes with no reclaim path, and audits the toggle less well than the deletion it unlocks — not shippable as an owner-facing control plane until the lease wiring and the status/audit truthfulness are fixed.

## s3 governance API — web/s3_governance_api.py

- **Handler:** one FastAPI router, all routes under `/api/v1/admin/s3-governance`: page `GET /s3/governance` (`579`); credential list `GET /signing-credentials` (`585`); policy `GET 606`, `PUT 618`, `DELETE 640`; ownership `GET 657`, `PUT 668`, `DELETE 686`; public access block `GET 704`, `PUT 715`; object lock `GET 743`, `PUT 758`; website `GET 845`, `PUT 852`, `DELETE 897`; notifications `GET 917`, `PUT 954`; replication `GET 1007`, `PUT 1029`, `DELETE 1071`; tiering `GET 1089`, `PUT 1101`, `DELETE 1146`; reports `GET 1166`, `PUT 1181`, `DELETE 1201`. There is no encryption, ABAC or access-logging route in this module (contrary to the scope note); notifications are the only event surface.
- **Auth model:** `_actor` (`99-120`) calls `webapp.require_auth`, then requires `admin` for every write and `admin|operator` for reads (`109`). API keys are reachable through `require_auth` but are then **rejected** because `request.state.user["role"] == "api-key"` is never in the allowed set (`602-623` in `web/app.py` sets that role). Every write calls `webapp._same_origin` (`119`), which requires `x-teledrive-csrf: 1` and, only when `Origin` is present, an exact host match (`321-334` of `web/app.py`); all fifteen write routes reach it through `_actor(write=True)`. The page route only calls `require_auth` (`579-582` → `_serve_static_page`, `web/app.py:1794-1805`), so a viewer/operator can load the HTML, but every API call it makes is still role-checked server-side and the UI merely hides buttons. No route is admin-only by the global `is_admin_only` map in a way that would contradict the operator-read contract; the module enforces its own role policy before touching the bucket.
- **Inputs:** all bodies are `dict = Body(...)`; non-dict bodies are 422 by FastAPI. `bucket`/`config_id` are unconstrained `str` path segments, then `_bucket_exists` validates the bucket with `BucketCatalog.validate_bucket` and a catalog lookup (`346-360`); `config_id` is not validated locally and relies on the gateway (`teledrive/s3/tiering.py:254,281-283`, `report_configs.py:161-166,537-540`). Per route: policy — `policy` must be a non-empty `str` or a JSON-serialisable `dict`, ≤ `MAX_POLICY_BYTES` = 20 KiB (`623-630`); the document itself is parsed only by the gateway (`web/s3.py:1276-1284`, `policy.py:393`). Ownership — `model` must be in `MODELS` (`673-675`). PAB — `flags` must be a dict whose key set is exactly the four `PAB_FIELDS` and all values `bool` (`720-726`); stricter than the S3 XML parser, which accepts a subset. Object lock — `mode` required and in `LOCK_MODES`, `days` optional and `1..3650` if present (`763-771`). Website — `public_read` optional **and defaults to False** (`857`), `confirm` must equal `PUBLISH` when true (`860-865`), `index_document` is required by the serializer (missing → caught `KeyError` → 400), `error_document` defaults to `None` (`866-875`); document names are validated only by the gateway (`teledrive/s3/website.py:57-78,105-137`). Notifications — `enabled` bool default True (`959`), `events` list of 1..10 members of `EVENTS` (`973-978`), `destination` is **not validated here** (passed through at `981`), filters are `str()`-coerced with no length bound (`983-984`), `secret` is accepted (`985`) but see findings. Replication — `rules` list 1..10, each `status` in `{Enabled,Disabled}`, `destination` a non-empty string, `prefix` `str()`-coerced, missing `id` becomes `rule-N` (`1034-1055`); the destination is **not checked against `_destination_labels()` here**, only by the gateway (`teledrive/s3/replication.py:154-160,277-290`). Tiering — `status` defaults to `"Enabled"` (`1106`), `tiers` non-empty, distinct access tiers, `days` `1..36500` (`1109-1129`), `prefix` coerced, tag filters unsupported. Reports — `kind` in `REPORT_KINDS` (`1186`), `config_id` unchecked locally; `_report_xml` requires a destination bucket for analytics/inventory (`480-483`), defaults inventory `enabled` to True (`476-479`), schedule to `"Daily"` (`500-502`), `IncludedObjectVersions` to `"Current"` (`507`), and never emits `OptionalFields` or filter tags. Only reports call `_body_size` (`1189`); policy has its own 20 KiB bound, and every other body is bounded only indirectly by the gateway's 16 KiB XML caps.
- **Side effects:** every read/write opens a short-lived `webapp.db_connect()` and instantiates `S3CredentialStore(conn, load_master_key())` (`368-374`), then `store.reveal(match["id"])` decrypts **one active credential's secret in-process** (`338-339`); the plaintext secret exists only in `call_s3`'s locals and is used to compute a SigV4 signature (`150-192`). The signed request goes over loopback HTTP(S) to `127.0.0.1:<s3_port>` (or `TELEDRIVE_S3_INTERNAL_HOST`, `237-246`) and is verified and executed by the real gateway (`web/s3.py:4793`). So policy/ABAC evaluation, ownership, PAB, website XML, notification allowlist, replication destination existence, report destination existence and tiering semantics are all inherited from the S3 implementation — the router is not a second store except for `public_read`. `_set_public_read` (`796-820`) writes `storage_buckets.public_read` directly, in its own transaction, **after** the website XML has already been written (`876-880`). Each successful write appends one `s3-governance` audit entry via `_log` with the WebUI username (`123-126`); failed role checks are logged too (`111-113`). No secret, access key or credential ciphertext is written to the audit line; the notification line logs only the parsed destination hostname (`993-996`), and the website line logs the gate value (`881-884`).
- **Failure modes:** `400` for invalid/unknown bucket (`346-360`), bad policy type/size (`626-630`), unknown ownership model (`674-675`), malformed PAB (`721-726`), object-lock mode/days (`764-771`), invalid `public_read` type is `422` (`858-859`), missing `PUBLISH` (`861-865`), bad index/error document (`874-875`), bad events (`973-978`), bad replication rules (`1035-1049`), bad tiering rows (`1107-1127`), unknown report kind (`1186-1187`), and a credential that does not grant the requested action (`326-328`). `401`/`403` from `require_auth`/`_actor`. `409` when no active credential grants the action (`330-337`). `413` from `_body_size` (reports only). Gateway 4xx/5xx are proxied almost verbatim with `X-AWS-Error-Code` (`390-398`). `502` when the loopback listener is unreachable (`285-289`). `500` for anything unexpected: `store.reveal` failures (master-key change), an S3 `200` with an unparseable list document (`529-547`), or `http.client.HTTPException` (only `OSError` is caught, `285`). Two read paths treat "not configured" specially: `_NOT_CONFIGURED` covers only policy/website/object-lock/replication (`87-92`); notifications and reports rely on the gateway returning a valid empty document, which it does.
- **Findings:**
  - [high] **A website PUT that omits `public_read` silently unpublishes a live site.** `public_read = payload.get("public_read", False)` (`857`) is then always written back with `_set_public_read(bucket, public_read)` (`880`), after the new XML has already been accepted (`876-879`). A caller that legitimately only wants to change `index_document`/`error_document` (a natural partial update; every other field here is partial-tolerant) turns the anonymous `/site/{bucket}/` surface off. The UI avoids this only by sending the value it last loaded (`web/static/app.js:1290-1303`), so a stale second admin tab or any script client reproduces it. The existing regression test always sends `public_read` explicitly (`tests/web/test_s3_governance_ui.py:221-252`), which is why this is not caught. Fix: distinguish absent from false (`public_read` = None → keep `catalog.public_read(bucket)`), or require the field whenever the bucket is currently published. The same handler also resets `error_document` to `None` when omitted (`867`), wiping a configured error page on a partial update.
  - [high] **The notification webhook HMAC secret is accepted by the API and then silently discarded, deleting any existing secret.** `put_notifications` puts `payload.get("secret")` into the config dict (`985`), but `notification_xml` serialises only `Id`, `Endpoint`, `Event` and `Filter` (`teledrive/s3/notifications.py:289-306`) — there is no `Secret` element. The gateway therefore parses `secret=None` and `NotificationStore.replace_config` replaces the row with `encrypted_secret=NULL` (`notifications.py:552-581`), so every save of the notifications tab unsigns the webhook: deliveries lose `X-TeleDrive-Signature` (`notifications.py:730-740`). A caller who supplies a `secret` gets a 200 and no error while the receiver can no longer authenticate TeleDrive, which is a downgrade of a security control. Probe (verified): building the exact XML this route sends and parsing it back yields `secret: None`. Fix: add `<Secret>` to `notification_xml`, or reject `secret` instead of accepting and dropping it; never let a full-replace PUT erase a secret the payload did not mention.
  - [high] **Object-lock updates can erase the default retention and downgrade COMPLIANCE to GOVERNANCE, while the audit line calls the write one-way.** `days` is optional (`767-771`), so `_lock_xml` emits a `DefaultRetention` with `Mode` but no `Days` (`419-424`). The gateway parser accepts that (`web/s3.py:3013-3051`) and `catalog.set_object_lock_config(..., days=None)` stores `object_lock_default_days=NULL` (`teledrive/storage/catalog.py:200-223`), i.e. future objects get no default retention. The same function only refuses to *shorten* days (`214-217`); it never refuses a mode change, so a bucket already locked `COMPLIANCE` can be moved to `GOVERNANCE` and the audit says `object lock ENABLED (one-way)` (`777-779`). The UI labels days optional and sends `null` when blank (`web/static/app.js:1273-1276`, `s3-governance.html:269-274`). This is a defect in the shared core, but this router is the ergonomic route that exposes it with no `ENABLE-LOCK`-style server check (that prompt is client-side only). Fix at `set_object_lock_config`: reject `days is None` once enabled and reject `mode != current_mode`; mirror the PUBLISH-token pattern server-side if a destructive edit is intended.
  - [medium] **Report PUT is a destructive full replace that silently changes fields the caller did not send.** For `inventory`, `_report_xml` always emits `IsEnabled` from `payload.get("enabled", True)` (`476-479`), so an omitted `enabled` re-enables a disabled report; it always emits `Schedule/Frequency` from `payload.get("schedule") or "Daily"` (`500-502`), so an update that only changes the destination or prefix turns Weekly into Daily; it always emits `IncludedObjectVersions=Current` (`507`), so a previously `All` configuration is narrowed; and it never emits `OptionalFields`. Filter tags are not carried either: `_report_payload` exposes only `filter.prefix` (`511-526`) and the UI form has no tag field (`s3-governance.html:502-527`), so editing a tag-filtered inventory/analytics config rewrites the Filter to a prefix-only XML — which **broadens** what the scheduled report exports into the destination bucket. Verified by probe: a payload of `{schedule, destination}` parses back as `enabled=True, included_object_versions='Current', optional_fields=[], filter={'prefix':'','tags':[]}`. Fix: read the existing config and merge absent keys, or make the endpoint an explicit replace with a confirmation and a documented required-field set.
  - [medium] **Tiering PUT has the same partial-update trap for filters and status.** `_tiering_xml` always emits `filter={"prefix":..., "tag":None}` (`457-465`), and `_tiering_payload` only exposes `prefix` (`446-454`); saving a prefix-filtered edit of a tag-filtered configuration therefore drops the tag and applies the tiering rule to the whole bucket, while `status` defaults to `"Enabled"` (`1106`) so an omitted status silently re-enables a disabled rule. Same merge-or-confirm fix as reports.
  - [medium] **`public_read` bypasses the bucket's public-access-block, and the PUBLISH token is not the only way to flip the gate.** `_set_public_read` writes the SQL flag directly (`796-820`); the `/site/` reader checks only website-enabled + `public_read` (`web/app.py:6160-6166`) and never consults `public_access_block` or the bucket policy, so an admin can publish a bucket whose PAB has all four flags set, including `BlockPublicPolicy`/`RestrictPublicBuckets`. This is documented as an intentional app-level gate (`teledrive/s3/website.py:1-20`), but the token does not compensate for it. Worse, the older admin endpoint `PUT /api/v1/admin/storage/buckets/{bucket}/website` (`web/app.py:3975-4025`) performs the same `public_read` write with **no `PUBLISH` token at all**, so any admin session (with CSRF header) can publish without the confirmation this router mandates. A guard that one of two equivalent endpoints does not implement is not a guard; move both onto one helper that requires the token and consults PAB (or explicitly documents why PAB does not apply to `/site/`).
  - [medium] **The S3 identity that executes a governance write is never the WebUI actor.** `_signing_credential` picks the credential named by the request (`credential_id` in every write body, query on deletes) or the first active credential granting the action (`311-339`); credentials have no owner column (`teledrive/s3/credentials.py:186-210`), so the gateway sees a service key, evaluates `bucket policy` and ABAC principal tags against that key, and the `s3-governance` log line records the admin. An explicit `Deny` scoped to a principal, or an ABAC `aws:PrincipalTag/*` condition, can be sidestepped by choosing a credential with the needed tag/permission — the only local check is that the action string is in the key's permission list (`326-328`). Impact is deployment-specific (**unverified** against a real restrictive policy, but the code path is unambiguous). For a "second path to the same state" this means S3-side audit and policy cannot attribute the change to the human; document it, or bind credentials to users/roles.
  - [low] **Destination allowlists are display-only in this module.** `_approved_destinations` (`942-951`) feeds the UI list but `put_notifications` never consults it (`971-991`); the gateway does (`web/s3.py:1497-1504`), so the write fails closed today. Likewise `_destination_labels` (`550-572`) feeds `get_replication` but `put_replication` does not use it; the gateway does (`web/s3.py:1584-1590`, `replication.py:277-290`). This duplicates `_replication_destinations`/`_notification_allowlist` (`web/s3.py:676-698,1076-1085`), two sources for one fact that can drift and two functions that return misleading success shapes if `TRANSPORT` is ever pointed at a less strict stub. Validate locally too, or have the gateway export one helper and call it.
  - [low] **`GET /signing-credentials` exposes every active access key, name and permission list to any operator.** `585-599` returns `row["access_key"]` for all active credentials; the secret is correctly withheld (and `tests/web/test_s3_governance_ui.py:445-461` pins that), but access keys are the non-secret half of an S3 identity and an operator does not need them to use this page. Return `id`/`name` only, or admin-gate this one read.
  - [low] **The 502 path leaks the internal S3 origin.** `_http_send` includes `{origin}` (scheme/host/port) in the client-facing detail (`285-289`), and gateway messages are proxied verbatim (`390-398`). No credential material is echoed, but infrastructure layout is. Return a generic "S3 gateway unreachable" and log the origin server-side.
  - [low] **Replication PUT drops the configured `Role`.** `replication_xml({"role": None, ...})` (`1056`) always writes `DEFAULT_ROLE` (`teledrive/s3/replication.py:294-296`), so editing rules silently resets an opaque label the S3 path preserves. Low because `Role` has no authorization meaning here, but it is still an unannounced state change.
  - [nit] `isinstance(True, int)` means `"days": true` is accepted as 1 in object-lock (`768-769`) and tiering (`1125`); use `type(days) is int`.
  - [nit] `MAX_BODY_BYTES` is enforced only for reports (`405-408`, `1189`); every other handler parses an arbitrary JSON body and relies on the gateway's 16 KiB XML cap. Cheap to apply consistently.
  - [nit] `S3Result.failure()` (`224-234`) is dead code; `_bucket_exists` calls `catalog.list_buckets()` per request (`346-360`) just to test membership.
  - [nit] The module docstring says these operations are "only reachable through the S3 API with a SigV4 credential, which is the wrong interface for an operator" (`3-7`), but it also lists encryption/ABAC/logging in the scope prompt as governance surfaces; they are absent. Not a bypass, just an incomplete bridge.
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** every write is actually executed by the real S3 gateway over loopback with a credential the caller cannot exfiltrate; policy parsing, ownership models, PAB key set, notification destination allowlist, replication destination existence and report destination-bucket existence are therefore as strict as the S3 path. No route returns a secret, and no audit line or error message contains one. `_same_origin` is on every write, API keys cannot reach writes, and `delete_website` does revoke `public_read` in the same transaction as the gateway delete (`teledrive/s3/website.py:207-225`), so the delete path does not leave a public bucket behind.
- **Tests that should exist:**
  - `test_website_put_without_public_read_preserves_a_live_site` — publish with `public_read=true`/`confirm=PUBLISH`, then PUT `{"index_document": "index.html"}` with no `public_read`; assert the response says `public_read is True`, `GET /site/{bucket}/` still serves the index, and the gate row is unchanged. **This is the mandatory regression:** it is the exact destructive default under review, and the existing test (`tests/web/test_s3_governance_ui.py:221-252`) cannot fail because it always sends the flag.
  - `test_notifications_put_preserves_an_existing_webhook_secret` — save a notification with a 32-char secret through the S3 API, then re-save the same destination/events through `/s3-governance` without a `secret`; assert `storage_notification_configs.encrypted_secret` is unchanged and a delivered event still carries `X-TeleDrive-Signature`. Today the column is nulled.
  - `test_object_lock_put_without_days_cannot_erase_default_retention` — enable `COMPLIANCE`/30, then PUT `{"mode":"COMPLIANCE"}` and `{"mode":"GOVERNANCE","days":30}`; assert the first is 400 (or keeps 30) and the second is 400; assert `object_lock_default_days` is still 30 and `object_lock_default_mode` is still `COMPLIANCE`.
  - `test_report_put_merges_unsent_inventory_fields` — create an inventory report with `enabled=false`, `schedule=Weekly`, `included_object_versions=All`, `optional_fields=[Size]`, a tag filter; PUT only a new destination; assert all five survive. Probed today: `enabled` becomes true, schedule Daily, versions Current, optional fields empty, filter empty.
  - `test_tiering_put_preserves_tag_filter_and_status` — same shape with a tag-filtered, disabled tiering config.
  - `test_public_read_requires_publish_token_on_both_website_endpoints` — call `/api/v1/admin/storage/buckets/{bucket}/website` with `public_read=true` and no token; assert 400 once the endpoints are unified, and assert the governance route refuses a bucket with `RestrictPublicBuckets=true` unless the product decision says otherwise.
  - `test_governance_write_is_attributed_to_the_chosen_credential` — create two credentials with different tags, set a bucket policy that denies one principal, and assert the governance write with that credential is denied (or document that the router intentionally bypasses per-principal Deny).
  - `test_delete_routes_require_csrf` — DELETE each of policy/ownership/website/replication/tiering/report without `x-teledrive-csrf`; assert 403 and no state change.
- **Verdict:** This is a genuine gateway-backed bridge, not a shadow implementation — the only SQL it writes directly is `public_read` — but that one direct write defaults a missing field to *false* and takes live sites offline, the notification bridge silently strips webhook signing, the object-lock bridge can erase default retention and downgrade COMPLIANCE, and the report/tiering bridges silently rewrite fields the caller did not send; the fixes are merge-or-reject semantics plus one server-side confirmation path, not a redesign.

## batch delete and form upload — POST /{bucket}?delete, POST /{bucket}

- **Handler:** web/s3.py:978 (`_handle_batch_delete`, dispatched from web/s3.py:1960-1961 after routing at :4282-4283/:4629-4632) and web/s3.py:849 (`_handle_form_upload`, dispatched at :4823-4824 *before* `verify_header_signature` and before the shared `_authorize_resource` at :4868-4873).
- **Auth model:** Batch delete is SigV4-authenticated at route level with `action = "s3:DeleteObject"` (:4632; header/presign verification at :4825), then **every entry is re-authorized individually** against its own key: `_authorize_resource(catalog, ctx.store, ctx.owner, "s3:DeleteObject", bucket, key, request)` (:1003-1005) and a denial becomes a per-entry `<Error>` (:1006-1013), not a failed batch. Explicit `VersionId` deletes are authorized under the *same* `s3:DeleteObject` scope; there is no `s3:DeleteObjectVersion` in `PERMISSIONS` (teledrive/s3/credentials.py:34-110), so a key holding only `DeleteObject` may permanently revoke historical versions (AWS requires `s3:DeleteObjectVersion`). Form upload has **no SigV4 header authentication**: `authorization` is explicitly refused as ambiguous (:852-853), and the only credential proof is `verify_post_policy` (:873-875) → HMAC over the base64 `policy` with a key derived from `x-amz-credential`, resolved via `store.resolve(access, "s3:PutObject")` (teledrive/s3/post_policy.py:201-209), then a per-key `_authorize_resource(..., "s3:PutObject", bucket, post_key, ...)` (:901-906) and `s3:PutObjectTagging` if `x-amz-tagging` is present (:891-896). The POST-policy route is *not* session-reachable: there is no cookie/session code path in web/s3.py at all, and the form route requires a valid signature even though it bypasses header SigV4. `verify_post_policy` is genuinely implemented, not stubbed: expiry is checked (`now < expiration <= signed_at + 15 min`, signed_at within −300..+900 s, post_policy.py:104-106,151-155), the condition set must pin every field exactly (`len(clauses) == len(CONDITIONS)+1+len(extra)`, :151-200), `content-length-range` is mandatory (:199), and the signature is a constant-time comparison over the exact base64 policy string (:204-209).
- **Inputs:** Batch body is streamed and hard-capped at 1 MiB before parse (:989-993, `MAX_XML_BYTES` in teledrive/s3/batch_delete.py:21), then parsed with `defusedxml` (:83) — DTDs/entities are refused, root/field/attribute shape is exact, `Quiet` is one literal true/false, 1..1000 `Object` entries (`MAX_BATCH_OBJECTS`, batch_delete.py:108-110), each with exactly `Key` + optional `VersionId`, key 1..1024 UTF-8 bytes and version 1..80 chars (batch_delete.py:126-131). `verify_body` independently requires `x-amz-content-sha256` to equal the SHA-256 of the body **and** at least one of `Content-MD5`/`x-amz-checksum-crc32`/`x-amz-checksum-sha256` to match (batch_delete.py:43-75), so a body is never applied without a checksum. Form input: the whole body is read into memory up to `min(FORM_LIMIT=20 MiB, REST_MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES)` (:861-872), parsed by `email` into fields + one `file` part; field names are an allowlist plus ≤12 `x-amz-meta-*` (post_policy.py:16-34), ≤26 parts, ≤12 000 bytes per field, duplicate/transfer-encoded/nested parts refused (:52-83); required fields include `x-amz-checksum-sha256`, `key`, `policy`, `x-amz-algorithm`, `x-amz-credential`, `x-amz-date`, `x-amz-signature`; `key` is 1..1024 UTF-8 bytes with `${` refused (:133-135); the policy JSON is ≤8 192 bytes, must contain only `expiration`+`conditions`, and the checksum must match the file part before any Telegram call (:136-144). `filename` from the file part is never used, so there is no traversal surface.
- **Side effects:** Batch delete mutates only SQL: `catalog.delete_marker` per key (new `delete-marker` row + head CAS, or, in `Suspended` buckets, head deletion + `removed_versions` row) or `catalog.delete_version` (lock check, `removed_versions`, head CAS to the previous visible version) — each commits per key (catalog.py:698,797), emits `s3:ObjectRemoved:*` notifications and an S3-Metadata journal row *only when the bucket has a metadata configuration* (catalog.py:692-698,790-797 → :600-615). No Telegram message is deleted synchronously. The intended GC enqueue (`RetainedMediaGC(db).enqueue([version_id], ...)`, :1029-1034) never runs (see findings). Form upload writes one `s3-form-*` staging file (:909-917), verifies the transport (:918-919), checks quota (:920-922), calls `TelegramObjectStorage.put_file` (:923-927), attaches metadata/tags uncommitted (:928-934), publishes (:935; bucket **default object-lock retention is applied at publish time**, catalog.py:1072-1074,1119-1120), applies `BucketOwnerPreferred` ACL (:936-937), and unlinks the staging file in `finally` (:974-975). ETag is the plaintext SHA-256 (`"…"`), and the returned `x-amz-version-id` is the object id (:938-939).
- **Failure modes:** Batch: `403 AccessDenied` for a bad signature / revoked credential / bucket-level or per-key denial raised as an exception; `400` `XAmzContentSHA256Mismatch`/`InvalidRequest`/`InvalidDigest`/`BadDigest` from `verify_body`, `MalformedXML` (size, non-UTF-8, DTD, >1000 entries, unexpected field, bad Quiet), `InvalidObjectName` (>1024-byte or empty key), `InvalidArgument` (version id length) from `parse_batch` (:4957-4958); `404 NoSuchBucket` (:983-984); `501` for `x-amz-mfa`/`x-amz-bypass-governance-retention` (:985-988, unreachable — see findings) and for any other rejected write option at :4831. A successful request is always `200` and carries per-entry `<Error>` `AccessDenied` (object lock or ACL), `NoSuchVersion`, `InvalidArgument`, `InvalidObjectName` (:1014-1063). An unexpected DB/transport exception mid-batch escapes the loop and the shared handler (only `BatchDeleteError`/`SigV4Error`/… are mapped, :4957-4999), so the client gets a `500` with earlier keys already committed and no indication which. Form: `400 InvalidRequest`/`InvalidArgument` (malformed multipart, unsupported/oversized/duplicate field, bad policy JSON, unsafe redirect, unsupported status), `403 AccessDenied`/`SignatureDoesNotMatch` (scope, expiry, condition mismatch, bad signature, revoked key), `400 BadDigest` (file checksum ≠ signed checksum), `413 EntityTooLarge` (body/form over the limit, quota — quota is mapped to a `413` by `_quota_guard`, :180-192), `404 NoSuchBucket`, `501` for SSE-C or any header rejected by `_reject_unimplemented_write_options` (:854), and `200`/`201`/`204`/`303` for success. A transport failure in `put_file` propagates as a generic `500`; nothing is published and the staged file is removed.
- **Findings:**
  - [high] **A COMPLIANCE-retained head can be deleted by `POST ?delete` without a `VersionId` (and by a plain `DELETE`) once the bucket is `Suspended`.** `BucketCatalog.delete_marker`'s suspended branch removes the head row and inserts `removed_versions` with **no object-lock check** (`teledrive/storage/catalog.py:637-662`), while the explicit-version path fences retention/legal hold (`catalog.py:743-751`). Versioning can be suspended on a lock-enabled bucket: `PUT ?versioning` accepts `Status=Suspended` (`web/s3.py:1637-1638`, `teledrive/s3/versioning.py:50-55`) and `set_versioning` does not consult `object_lock_config` (`catalog.py:174-184`); AWS forbids this transition on an Object-Lock bucket. Probe (`/tmp/opencode/probe_worm.db`, catalog-level, no server): publish → `set_retention(COMPLIANCE, +1h)` → `delete_version` correctly raises `PermissionError: Object is under retention` → `set_versioning("Suspended")` → `delete_marker` returns `None` and `cat.get()` raises `FileNotFoundError`; the version is hidden from `list_versions` before expiry. The bytes survive (GC re-checks the lock, `teledrive/storage/gc_collect.py:443-452`), so this is a recoverable WORM/immutability violation, not physical destruction. The same branch is what makes `_handle_object_delete` (`web/s3.py:3623-3637`) report success.
  - [high] **The "GC-integrated delete" enqueue in the batch handler can never execute: `db` is not defined in the function, and `RetainedMediaGC` is not imported anywhere in `web/s3.py`.** `RetainedMediaGC(db)` at `web/s3.py:1030` raises `NameError` for every successful version delete; `except Exception: pass` at :1033-1034 swallows it, so the queue insert silently never happens. Verified by AST scope analysis (`db` is a load, not a local/parameter/global; `RetainedMediaGC` is a load with no import) and by `grep`: the module has no `from teledrive.storage.gc_collect import RetainedMediaGC` and no `import *`/`__getattr__`. The identical bug affects `_handle_multipart` abort (`:2077`) and `_handle_version_delete` (`:3370`), so **all three S3 paths that were supposed to enqueue physical collection are dead code**; only REST/lifecycle enqueue. Consequence: version-deleted and MPU-aborted media stays queued nowhere and is reclaimed only if an operator later runs a broad `scope=revoked|deleted` collection (`gc_collect.py:180-330`), not after the grace period as documented. Not covered by tests — `tests/s3/test_s3_batch_delete.py:69-92` runs with GC disabled and asserts only that parts are unchanged.
  - [high] **Unauthenticated multipart parse amplification: the part-count bound is enforced only after every part has been materialized.** `parse_form` does `items = list(msg.iter_parts())` and *then* compares `len(items) > MAX_FORM_PARTS` (`teledrive/s3/post_policy.py:52-54`), and `_handle_form_upload` calls `parse_form` at `web/s3.py:870` **before** `verify_post_policy` at :873. Any anonymous `POST /{bucket}` with `Content-Type: multipart/form-data; boundary=…` and no query string reaches it (`web/s3.py:4280-4281`), with no signature, credential, or session. Measured on this tree with the vendored venv: 2 000 tiny parts (142 kB) → 1.06 s / 2.1 MB; 20 000 parts (1.42 MB) → 10.7 s / 18.4 MB; 50 000 parts (3.55 MB) → 34.5 s / 45.7 MB peak (`tracemalloc`), all rejected only at the end. Linear extrapolation to the 20 MiB cap is ≈295 000 parts, ≈200 s of CPU and ≈260 MB per request, before any authentication. Fix: count parts during iteration (abort at 27), cap per-part header bytes, and move the cheap signature/credential parse (or at least a bounded pre-scan) ahead of full MIME materialization.
  - [medium] **The form body is buffered, not streamed, and is copied at least three times before it reaches the staging file.** `request.stream()` is accumulated into a `bytearray` (`web/s3.py:865-867`), copied again by `bytes(form_bytes)` (:871), and the `email` parser plus `part.get_payload(decode=True)` create further copies (`post_policy.py:46-47,65`); only then is the payload written to a temp file (:911-917). A single legitimate 20 MiB upload therefore costs roughly 50-60 MiB of RSS, and the `s3-form-*` staging file exists only after the whole body is already in memory, so it provides no memory relief. Compounded by the finding above and by the absence of a concurrency cap: N concurrent anonymous bodies consume N × the cap. Fix: stream parts into the staging file (and hash while writing), enforcing the size cap incrementally.
  - [medium] **Batch delete misreports `Suspended`-bucket deletes as delete-marker creations.** When `delete_marker` returns `None` (suspended semantics, `catalog.py:637-663`), `_handle_batch_delete` still emits `<Deleted><VersionId/><DeleteMarker>true</DeleteMarker><DeleteMarkerVersionId/></Deleted>` (`web/s3.py:1064-1069`) because `marker` is `None` but the elements are unconditional. AWS returns a bare `<Deleted><Key>…</Key></Deleted>`, and the single-object DELETE path gets this right (`:3635-3637`, `204` with no marker headers). Additionally, deleting an explicit *delete-marker* version via `VersionId` never reports `<DeleteMarker>true</DeleteMarker>` (`:1051-1054`), while `DELETE ?versionId` does (`:3376-3377`). SDK/backup clients that branch on `DeleteMarker` will take the wrong path.
  - [low] **An explicit `VersionId` delete is authorized as `s3:DeleteObject`, not `s3:DeleteObjectVersion`.** `_handle_batch_delete` uses one action string for both branches (`:1003-1005`), and the permission does not exist in `PERMISSIONS` (`teledrive/s3/credentials.py:34-110`). A credential minted for "delete the current object" can irreversibly revoke every historical version in a 1000-key request. If this is intentional, it should be stated next to the scope model; otherwise add the scope and use it for the `VersionId` branch (and for `DELETE ?versionId`, `:4929`).
  - [low] **A mid-batch server-side failure returns an unstructured `500` after partial mutation.** Only `ValueError` and `PermissionError` from `delete_version`, and `ValueError` from `delete_marker`, are converted to per-entry results (`web/s3.py:1014-1063`); a `FileNotFoundError` from a concurrently deleted bucket (`catalog.py:625-626`) or any SQLAlchemy/transport error aborts the loop and escapes to the generic handler. Earlier keys are already committed and the response body lists none of them. AWS's contract is per-key results; wrap each entry in a per-entry `Error` (`InternalError`) and keep the `200`, or at least document that a 500 means "some prefix of the entries was applied".
  - [low] **The 201 form response builds `Location` from the unsigned `Host` header.** `str(request.base_url)` (`web/s3.py:964-966`) is attacker-controlled on a path-style form POST (the POST policy binds `bucket` from the path, not `Host`), so an uploader with a valid credential can receive a `Location` pointing at another origin. Impact is limited to the uploader's own client (no server-side fetch, key is percent-quoted), and the `success_action_redirect` path *is* properly validated (https-only, no credentials/fragment, `post_policy.py:119-132`). Pin the public base URL from configuration instead.
  - [low] **`POST /{bucket}` with any query string is not a form upload.** `form_upload` requires `not request.query_params` (`web/s3.py:4280-4281`), so a browser POST to `/bucket?foo` or to a presigned-style URL carrying `X-Amz-*` query fields falls through to header SigV4 and returns `403 AccessDenied` instead of an explanatory error. Either accept the auth-only query fields (mirroring `auth_only_query`, `:4294-4298`) or return `InvalidRequest` naming the constraint.
  - [nit] The `x-amz-mfa` / `x-amz-bypass-governance-retention` branch (`web/s3.py:985-988`) is unreachable: `_reject_unimplemented_write_options` already rejects both headers at `:4831` with the same `501`, so it is dead defence-in-depth.
  - [nit] Duplicate `<Object>` entries are accepted verbatim (`batch_delete.py:96-134`); two entries for the same key without `VersionId` create two delete markers and two `<Deleted>` rows. If that is the intended AWS-compatible behaviour it should be pinned by a test, otherwise dedupe.
  - [nit] `_reject_unimplemented_write_options` inspects `request.headers` only, so a form field mapping to the same semantics (e.g. an `x-amz-storage-class` form field) is rejected earlier by the field allowlist (`post_policy.py:16-34`) rather than with the specific `NotImplemented` code; and `_apply_bucket_owner_preferred` (`:2782`) can never fire on a form upload because it reads `x-amz-acl` from headers, not from the policy-bound fields — harmless but dead at that call site.
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** the batch XML surface is safe — 1 MiB cap, `defusedxml` (DTD/entity payloads rejected; probe: `&#xD800;` numeric references are rejected as `MalformedXML`, never an unhandled `UnicodeEncodeError`), exact element/attribute shape, 1000-entry cap, key/version bounds, and both the SigV4 body SHA-256 and a second MD5/CRC32/SHA-256 checksum must match before a single unlink (`batch_delete.py:43-137`); per-key ACL/policy re-authorization really is per key; each key's mutation is a single SQL transaction with head CAS, notification, tag/annotation cleanup and (conditional) metadata journal; `Quiet` correctly suppresses only successes; object-lock denial on an explicit version is a per-entry `AccessDenied` inside a `200` (`:1042-1050`).
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** the POST-policy verifier is not bypassable by the usual tricks — the signature covers the exact base64 policy, the credential day must equal `x-amz-date` and the scope date, the region/service/terminator scope is regex-pinned, every policy field must appear exactly once as an `eq`/`dict` condition (or the two allowed `starts-with` families: `key`, `Content-Type`, `x-amz-meta-*`), `content-length-range` is mandatory and must contain the real body length, `bucket` is bound from the URL and cannot be supplied by the form, and the file checksum is verified before `verify_target`/`put_file`; redirects must be HTTPS with no userinfo/fragment and are percent-encoded before being echoed; the `201` body is XML-escaped (`_xml`, `:163-167`) and the `Location` key is `quote()`d, so a hostile `key` cannot inject markup or headers (tests at `tests/s3/test_s3_post_policy.py:223,338`).
  - **Verified sound (no finding):** form upload reaches parity with PUT on the parts that matter — 1..1024-byte key (`catalog.py:153-154` is the shared validator), the same `_quota_guard` before `put_file` (compare `:920` and `:3742`), bucket default object-lock retention applied at publish (`catalog.py:1072-1074`), and the same per-key ACL/policy gate.
- **Tests that should exist:**
  - `test_batch_delete_cannot_remove_a_compliance_retained_head_in_a_suspended_bucket` — publish `k`, `put_object_retention` COMPLIANCE +1 h, `PUT ?versioning Status=Suspended`, then `delete_objects` (no `VersionId`) **and** `DELETE /bucket/k`; assert both are denied (per-entry `AccessDenied` + `404`/`AccessDenied` for the single delete), `GET` still returns the bytes, `list_object_versions` still shows the version, and `retention_until_ns` is unchanged; repeat with `LegalHold=ON`. **This is the mandatory regression:** a WORM guarantee that disappears on an otherwise legal control-plane transition is exactly the class of bug that must never ship twice, and the existing lock tests only exercise `delete_version` (catalog.py:743-751), never the suspended head path.
  - `test_batch_version_delete_enqueues_gc_media` — enable GC (`storage_gc_state.enabled=1`), upload two versions, `delete_objects` with the current `VersionId`, assert exactly one `storage_gc_queue` row for that object id with reason `batch-version-delete` (and the same for `DELETE ?versionId` and `AbortMultipartUpload`); today all three are empty because `RetainedMediaGC` is undefined. Keep `tests/s3/test_s3_batch_delete.py:69-92` as the "bytes retained" twin.
  - `test_browser_post_rejects_a_many_part_form_without_parsing_it` — POST 5 000 minimal parts with a boundary and **no** signature fields; assert `400 InvalidRequest` within a small time budget (e.g. < 2 s) and that a tracemalloc/RSS guard stays under a few MB, proving the part cap is applied during iteration rather than after `list(iter_parts())`. Extend with a 20 MiB-of-empty-parts case and a single part with 10 MiB of headers.
  - `test_browser_post_streams_the_file_into_staging` — monkeypatch the staging writer and `parse_form`; assert the payload is written to the temp file incrementally (peak memory well below the body size and the file exists before `verify_post_policy` runs), or at minimum assert `REST_MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES`-sized bodies do not multiply peak RSS.
  - `test_batch_delete_suspended_bucket_wire_shape` — suspend versioning, delete an existing key and a missing key through `?delete`; assert `<Deleted>` has only `<Key>` (no `DeleteMarker`/`VersionId` elements) and that deleting a delete-marker `VersionId` returns `<DeleteMarker>true</DeleteMarker>`, matching `DELETE ?versionId`.
  - `test_batch_delete_version_requires_delete_object_version_scope` — mint a credential with `s3:DeleteObject` but not `s3:DeleteObjectVersion` (once the scope exists), delete a historical `VersionId`, assert per-entry `AccessDenied` and the version still readable; if the current behaviour is kept, pin it explicitly instead.
  - `test_browser_post_query_string_is_diagnosed` — POST a valid signed form to `/bucket?foo=1` and to `/bucket?X-Amz-Algorithm=…`; assert a 4xx that names the unsupported surface (never a bare `AccessDenied`) and that no object is created.
- **Verdict:** Both surfaces are checksum-pinned and re-authorize every key, and the POST-policy verifier is a real signature/expiry/condition check rather than a decorative one — but a COMPLIANCE lock silently evaporates on a suspended bucket, the batch handler's GC enqueue has never executed because `RetainedMediaGC` is undefined, and an anonymous 20 MiB multipart body can burn minutes of CPU in the MIME parser before authentication, so this is a correct happy path surrounded by three serious edges.
